Τοῦτο δὲ συγχωρηθεὶς, τὰς ἐπαλλήλους ἐπινοῶν τῶν ἀκουσίων παθῶν ἐπαγωγὰς, λαίλαπος δίκην ἀπηνῶς ἐπιφέρεται τοῖς καθ᾿ ὧν ἐκομίσατο συγχωρήσει Θεοῦ τὴν ἐξουσίαν· οὐ τὸ πρόσταγμα τὸ θεῖον ἐκπληρῶσαι βουλόμενος, ἀλλὰ τὸ οἰκεῖον τοῦ καθ᾿ ἡμῶν μίσους διαθρέψαι πάθος ἐπιθυμῶν· ἵνα τῷ πολλῷ βάρει τῶν ὀδυνηρῶν συμφορῶν, ἡ ψυχὴ δι᾿ ἀτονίαν ὀκλάσασα, τῆς θείας ἐλπίδος ἑαυτῆς περικόψῃ τὴν δύναμιν, ἀντὶ νουθεσίας, ἀθεΐας αἰτίαν ποιουμένη τὴν τῶν ἀλγεινῶς συμβαινόντων ἐπαγωγήν.

παʹ. Ὅταν οἱ τὴν πρακτικὴν ἕξιν, καὶ τὴν θεωρητικὴν ἐπιστήμην ἀπειληφότες, πρὸς τὴν τῶν ἀνθρώπων ταύτας μετέρχωνται δόξαν, ἤθη μὲν ἀρετῶν τῷ φαινομένῳ τρόπῳ σκιαγραφοῦντες· λόγους δὲ σοφίας καὶ γνώσεως μόνον λαλοῦντες, δίχα τῶν κατὰ δικαιοσύνην ἔργων· καὶ τὸν ὡς ἐπ᾿ ἀρετῇ καὶ γνώσει τοῖς ἄλλοις ἐπιδείκνυνται τύφον, εἰκότως πρέπουσι παραδίδονται πόνοις, διὰ τοῦ πάσχειν τὴν ἀγνοηθεῖσαν αὐτοῖς ἐκ τῆς ματαίας οἰήσεως ταπεινοφροσύνην μεταμανθάνοντες.

πβʹ. Ἕκαστος τῶν δαιμόνων κατὰ τὴν ἐνδιαθέτως ὑποκειμένην ἐπιτηδειότητα, πρὸς τήνδε ἢ τήνδε τῶν πειρασμῶν τὴν ἐπαγωγὴν τυγχάνει λειτουργῶν· ἄλλος γὰρ ἄλλης ἐστὶ ποιητικὸς κακίας, καὶ ἄλλος ἄλλου σαφῶς ἐστι μιαρώτερος, καὶ πρὸς τόδε μᾶλλον τῆς κακίας τὸ εἶδος ἐπιτηδειότερος.

πγʹ. Ἄνευ τῆς θείας συγχωρήσεως, οὐδὲ αὐτοὶ οἱ δαίμονες κατ᾿ οὐδὲν ὑπουργεῖν δύνανται τῷ διαβόλῳ. Οἶδε γὰρ αὐτὸς ὁ Θεὸς ὅπως μετὰ τῆς δεούσης φιλανθρώπου καὶ ἀγαθῆς προνοίας συγχωρεῖ τῷ διαβόλῳ διὰ τῶν αὐτοῦ ὑπουργῶν, τὰς ἐφ᾿ οἷς ἡμάρτομεν, διαφόρους ποιεῖσθαι τιμωρίας· καὶ δηλοῖ τοῦτο σαφῶς, ἡ περὶ τοῦ Ἰὼβ συγγραφὴ μὴ δυνάμενον τῷ Ἰὼβ παντελῶς προσπελάσαι τὸν διάβολον ἀναγραφομένη, δίχα τοῦ θείου βουλήματος.

πδʹ.

The faithful and practical mind, like holy Peter, held by Herod, the law of skin — for Herod is interpreted «of skin», which is the way of thinking of the flesh — is shut up under two guards and one iron gate, being warred upon both by the activity of the passions and by the assent of the understanding to the passions; and having passed through these, as through guards, that is, prisons, by the reason of practical philosophy as by an angel, it comes to the iron gate that leads into the city: I mean the relation of the senses to sensible things, firm and harsh and hard to overcome; and the reason of natural contemplation in the spirit, opening this gate so that it opens of itself, henceforth sends the mind on without fear to the kindred intelligible things, free of Herod's madness.

80. 1213The devil is both enemy of God and avenger: enemy, when, as hating him, he seems somehow to have a destructive love toward us men, through the modes of the voluntary passions persuading our choice, by pleasure, to prefer temporal things to the everlasting goods; by which, stealing the whole aspiration of the soul, he removes us altogether from the divine love, making us voluntary enemies of him who made us; avenger, when, laying bare his hatred toward us, he demands the punishment against us, as of those who have already become subject to him through sin. For nothing is so dear to the devil as a man being punished. And when this has been permitted him, devising successive inflictions of involuntary passions, he falls harshly, like a whirlwind, upon those against whom he has received the authority by God's permission: not wishing to fulfil the divine command, but desiring to nourish his own passion of hatred against us; so that, under the great weight of painful calamities, the soul, sinking down through faintness, may cut off from itself the power of the divine hope, making the infliction of things that befall painfully a cause of godlessness instead of admonition.

81. When those who have received the practical habit and contemplative science pursue these for the glory of men, sketching the manners of the virtues in an outward mode, and only talking words of wisdom and knowledge without the works of justice, and displaying to others their arrogance, as over virtue and knowledge, they are with reason handed over to fitting toils, learning instead, through suffering, the humble-mindedness which, from their vain conceit, they had not known.

82. Each of the demons, according to the aptitude that inwardly underlies it, serves for the infliction of this or that temptation; for one is productive of one vice and another of another, and one is clearly fouler than another, and more apt for this form of vice rather than that.

83. Without the divine permission not even the demons themselves can in any way serve the devil. For God himself knows how, with the fitting providence that loves man and is good, he permits the devil, through his servants, to inflict the different punishments for the things in which we have sinned; and the book about Job shows this clearly, recording that the devil could not approach Job at all without the divine purpose. Job i, 12.

84.

Ἐνυπόστατος πίστις ἐστὶν, ἡ ἐνεργὴς καὶ ἕμπρακτος, καθ᾿ ἣν ὁ τοῦ Θεοῦ λόγος ἐν τοῖς πρακτικοῖς δείκνυται, ταῖς ἐντολαῖς σωματούμενος,

1216δι᾿ ὧν ὡς Λόγος πρὸς τὸν ἐν ᾧ κατὰ φύσιν ἐστὶν, ἀνάγει Πατέρα τοὺς πράττοντας.

πεʹ. Ζωῆς μετάθεσιν καὶ λατρείαν ἀγγελικὴν, καὶ πρὸς σῶμα ψυχῆς ἑκούσιον ἀλλοτρίωσιν, καὶ τῆς ἐν πνεύματι θείας μεταποιήσεως γένεσιν, τὸ τῆς Νέας Διαθήκης καταγγέλλει μυστήριον. Πνευματικὴν δὲ περιτομὴν ὁ λόγος οἶδε καλεῖν, τὴν τῆς ἑμπαθοῦς σχέσεως πρὸς σῶμα τῆς ψυχῆς ἐκτομήν.

πϛʹ. Ἀγαθὸς ὑπάρχων ὁ Θεὸς, καὶ θέλων ἡμῶν ἐκτῖλαι παντελῶς τὸ τῆς κακίας σπέρμα τὴν ἡδονὴν, τὴν τὸν νοῦν τῆς θείας ἀποσυλήσασαν ἀγάπης, συγχωρεῖ τῷ διαβόλῳ πόνους ἡμῖν καὶ τιμωρίας ἐπαγαγεῖν· κατὰ ταὐτὸν καὶ τῆς προλαβούσης ἡδονῆς τὸν ἰὸν, διὰ τῶν πόνων τῆς ψυχῆς ἀποξέων, καὶ πρὸς τὰ παρόντα καὶ μόνην τὴν αἴσθησιν σαίνοντα, μῖσος ἡμῖν καὶ τελείαν ἀποδιάθεσιν, ὡς μηδὲν τιμωρίας πλέον εἰς κέρδος κεκτημένα κατὰ τὴν χρῆσιν, ἐμποιῆσαι βουλόμενος, καὶ τὴν ἐκείνου τιμωρὸν δύναμιν καὶ μισάνθρωπον, τῆς πρὸς ἀρετὴν ἐπαναγωγῆς περιστατικὴν αἰτίαν ποιήσασθαι τῶν αὐτῆς ἑκουσίως ἀπολισθησάντων.

πζʹ. Πρέπον ἐστὶ καὶ δίκαιον ὑπὸ τοῦ διαβόλου κολάζεσθαι, τοὺς τὰς αὐτοῦ πονηρὰς ὑποθήκας τῶν ἑκουσίων ἁμαρτημάτων ἡδέως δεξαμένους. Καὶ γὰρ καὶ ἡδονῆς ἐστι διὰ τῶν ἑκουσίων παθῶν σπορεὺς ὁ διάβολος, καὶ ὀδύνης διὰ τῶν ἀκουσίων ἑ̓παγωγεύς. πηʹ. Ὁ θεωρητικὸς νοῦς καὶ γνωστικὸς, πρὸς τιμωρίαν ἐκδίδοται διὰ τοῦτο πολλάκις τῷ διαβόλῳ, πόνους αὐτῷ δικαίως ἐπάγοντι καὶ συμφορὰς, ἵνα μάθῃ πάσχων περὶ καρτερίας μᾶλλον καὶ πόνων ὑπομονῆς φιλοσοφεῖν, ἢ διακενῆς τοῖς οὐκ οὖσιν ὑπερηφάνως ἐμματαιάζειν.

πθʹ. Ὁ πάσχων ὑπὲρ χάριτος [fort. ὑπὲρ θείας ἐντολῆς] Θεοῦ παραβαθείσης, ἐὰν ἐπιγνῷ τῆς ἰωμένης αὐτὸν θείας προνοίας τὸν λόγον τήν τε συμφορὰν εὐχαρίστως δέχεται χαίρων, καὶ τὴν ὑπὲρ ἧς παιδεύεται διορθοῦται πλημμέλειαν· ὁ δὲ ταύτης ἐναὶσθητῶν τῆς ἰατρείας, τῆς τε δοθείσης ἐνδίκως ἐπάγεται χάριτος καὶ τῇ συγχύσει τῶν παθῶν παραδίδοται, πρὸς τὴν πρᾶξιν ἐλθεῖν καταλιμπανόμενος, ὧν ἐνδιαθέτως εἶχε τὴν ἔφεσιν.

ϟʹ.

Enhypostatic faith is the active and practised faith, according to which the word of God is shown in those who practise, being embodied in the commandments,

1216through which, as Word, he leads up those who do them to the Father, in whom he is by nature.

85. A change of life and angelic worship, and the soul's voluntary alienation toward the body, and the coming-to-be of the divine remaking in the spirit: these the mystery of the New Covenant proclaims. And the word is wont to call spiritual circumcision the cutting-away of the soul's impassioned relation toward the body.

86. God, being good and willing to pluck out of us altogether pleasure, the seed of vice, which has robbed the mind of the divine love, permits the devil to bring toils and punishments upon us: at the same time both scraping from the soul, through the toils, the rust of the pleasure that came before, and wishing to produce in us hatred and complete estrangement toward present things, which fawn on sense alone, as things that in use have won nothing more than punishment by way of profit; and to make that one's punishing and man-hating power a cause, by way of distress, of the return to virtue for those who have voluntarily slipped away from it.

87. It is fitting and just that those be punished by the devil who gladly received his evil suggestions of voluntary sins. For the devil is both the sower of pleasure through the voluntary passions, and the bringer of pain through the involuntary.

88. The contemplative mind, the mind of knowledge, is often handed over to the devil for punishment for this reason, he justly bringing toils and calamities upon it: that by suffering it may learn to philosophize about endurance and patience in toils, rather than to be proudly and idly vain about things that are not.

89. He who suffers on account of the grace of God that has been transgressed, if he recognizes the principle of the divine providence that is healing him, both receives the calamity thankfully, rejoicing, and corrects the fault for which he is being disciplined; but he who is insensible to this healing is both justly led away from the grace that was given and handed over to the confusion of the passions, being left to come to the doing of those things for which he inwardly had the aspiration.

90.

Πᾶς ἀνεχόμενος ἑκουσίως ἐκ τῆς τῶν αὐτῷ πεπραγμένων συναισθήσεως, δέξασθαι τὰς ἐπιπόνους τῶν ἀκουσίων ἐπιφορὰς πειρασμῶν, μετὰ τῆς δεούσης εὐχαριστίας, οὐκ ἐξοικίζεται τῆς κατ᾿ ἀρετὴν ἕξεώς τε καὶ χάριτος, ὡς ὑπελθὼν ἑκουσίως τὸν ζυγὸν βασιλέως Βαβυλῶνος, καὶ ὡς χρέος ἀποτιννὺς, τὰς τῶν βασάνων ἐπιφορὰς καταδεχόμενος· ἀλλ᾿ ἐν αὐταῖς μένων, τελεῖ τῷ μὲν βασιλεῖ Βαβυλῶνος, τοὺς ἐκ τοῦ παθητοῦ τῆς φύσεως βιαίους πόνους, καὶ τὴν 1217ἐπ᾿ αὐτοῖς κατὰ διάνοιαν ὡς ὀφειλέτης αὐτῶν διὰ τὰς προλαβούσας πλημμελείας, συγκατάθεσιν· τῷ δὲ Θεῷ, προσφέρων διὰ λατρείας ἀληθινῆς, τῆς ταπεινῆς λέγω διαθέσεως, τὴν τῶν παρημελημένων διόρθωσιν.

ϟαʹ. Ὁ τὴν κατὰ συγχώρησιν Θεοῦ πρὸς διόρθωσιν ἐπαγομένην αὐτῷ διὰ τῶν ἀκουσίων πειρασμῶν συμφορὰν, εὐχαρίστως μὴ καταδεχόμενος καὶ τὴν ἐπὶ τῷ δοκεῖν δίκαιος εἶναι, μεταγνοὺς, οὐκ ἀποτιθέμενος οἴησιν, ὡς τοῖς θείοις τῶν δικαίων κριμάτων ἀντιπίπτων θεσπίσμασι, καὶ μὴ καταδεχόμενος ἑκουσίως ὑπὸ τὸν ζυγὸν γενέσθαι τοῦ βασιλέως Βαβυλῶνος κατὰ τὴν θείαν διαταγὴν, εἰς αἰχμαλωσίαν παραδίδοται· καὶ κλοιοὺς καὶ δεσμὰ καὶ λοιμὸν καὶ θάνατον καὶ μάχαιραν, καὶ τῆς ἰδίας παντελῶς ἀποικίζεται γῆς. Πάντα γὰρ ταῦτα, καὶ τούτων πλείονα, ὁ τῆς κατ᾿ ἀρετὴν καὶ γνῶσιν ὡς ἰδίας γῆς ἐξοικιζόμενος ἕξεως πάσχει· διὰ τὸ μὴ θέλειν αὐτὸν ἐξ ὑπερηφανίας καὶ ματαίας οἰήσεως τὰς ἐφ᾿ οἷς ἐπλημμέλησεν ἐκτιννὺς δίκας, ἐν θλίψεσιν εὐδοκῆσαι καὶ ἀνάγκαις καὶ στενοχωρίαις κατὰ τὸν θεῖον Ἀπόστολον. Ἤδει γὰρ ὁ μέγας Ἀπόστολος, φυλακτικὴν τῶν θείων κατὰ ψυχὴν θησαυρῶν ὑπάρχουσαν, τὴν ἐκτὸς περὶ τὸ σῶμα συνισταμένην διὰ τῶν πόνων ταπείνωσιν· καὶ διὰ τοῦτο στέργων ὑπέμενε, καὶ διὰ ἑαυτὸν, καὶ τοὺς οἷς ἀρετῆς καὶ πίστεως προέκειτο τύπος· ἵνα κἂν ὡς ὑπεύθυνοι πάσχωσι κατὰ τὸν ἐπιτιμηθέντα Κορίνθιον, εἰς παρηγορίαν ἔχωσι καὶ ὑπομονῆς μίμησιν τὸν ἀνευθύνως πάσχοντα.

ϟβʹ. Ὁ μὴ τοῖς σχήμασι τῶν ὁρατῶν ἐναπομένων διὰ τὴν αἴσθησιν, ἀλλὰ κατὰ νοῦν τοὺς λόγους αὐτῶν ἀναζητῶν, ὡς νοητῶν τύπους ἢ λόγους αἰσθητῶν θεώμενος κτισμάτων, οὐδὲν ἀκάθαρτον εἶναι τῶν ὁρωμένων διδάσκεται· πάντα γὰρ φύσει καλὰ λίαν καθέστηκεν.

ϟγʹ. Ὁ τῇ κινήσει τῶν αἰσθητῶν μὴ συναλλοιούμενος, ἀκίβδηλον τὴν τῶν ἀρετῶν μετέρχεται πρᾶξιν. Ὁ δὲ τοῖς αὐτῶν σχήμασι μὴ διατυπώσας τὸν νοῦν, τὴν ἀληθῆ τῶν ὄντων ἀπείληφε δόξαν. Ὁ δὲ καὶ αὐτὴν τὴν οὐσίαν τῶν ὄντων τῇ διανοίᾳ παραδραμὼν, ὡς ἄριστος θεολόγος, μετὰ ταύτην ἀγνώστως τῇ μονάδι προσέβαλε.

ϟδʹ.

Everyone who, out of awareness of what he has done, voluntarily endures to receive with the fitting thanksgiving the toilsome onsets of involuntary temptations, is not banished from the habit and grace according to virtue, as one who has voluntarily gone under the yoke of the king of Babylon, Jerem. xxvii, 6 seqq. and who accepts the onsets of torments as paying off a debt; but, remaining in them, he pays to the king of Babylon the violent toils that come from the passible part of the nature, and the assent of the understanding 1217to them, as being their debtor because of the faults that went before; and to God, offering through true worship, I mean the lowly disposition, the correction of what was neglected.

91. He who does not receive with thanksgiving the calamity brought upon him for correction, by God's permission, through the involuntary temptations, and who does not, by changing his mind, put off the conceit of seeming to be just, as one who resists the divine decrees of the just judgements, and who does not consent to come voluntarily under the yoke of the king of Babylon according to the divine ordinance, is handed over into captivity, and to collars and bonds and pestilence and death and sword, and is altogether exiled from his own land. For all these things, and more than these, he suffers who is banished from the habit according to virtue and knowledge as from his own land; because out of pride and vain conceit he does not will, paying the penalties for the things in which he did amiss, to take pleasure in afflictions and necessities and distresses, according to the divine Apostle. For the great Apostle knew that the humility which comes about outwardly in the body through toils is protective of the divine treasures in the soul; and for this reason he endured contentedly, both for his own sake and for those to whom he had been set forth as a type of virtue and faith; so that, even if they suffer as answerable, like the Corinthian who was rebuked, I Cor. v, 5. they might have, for consolation and for imitation of patience, him who suffers without being answerable.

92. He who does not remain in the shapes of visible things because of sense, but seeks out their principles with the mind, contemplating them as types of intelligible things or principles of sensible creatures, is taught that nothing of what is seen is unclean; for all things are by nature very good. Gen. i, 31.

93. He who is not altered together with the motion of sensible things pursues the unadulterated practice of the virtues. He who has not imprinted his mind with their shapes has received the true opinion of beings. And he who with his understanding has run past even the very essence of beings, as an excellent theologian, has after it unknowingly drawn near to the Monad.

94.

Πᾶς θεωρητικὸς νοῦς, ἔχων τὴν μάχαιραν τοῦ πνεύματος, ὅ ἐστι ῥῆμα Θεοῦ, ἐν ἑαυτῷ τῆς φαινομένης κτίσεως ἀποκτείνας τὴν κίνησιν, κατώρθωσεν ἀρετήν· καὶ τῶν αἰσθητῶν σχημάτων ἑαυτοῦ τὴν φαντασίαν ἀποτεμὼν, τὴν ἐν τοῖς λόγοις τῶν 1220ὄντων εὗρεν ἀλήθειαν, καθ᾿ ἣν ἡ φυσικὴ θεωρία συνἐστηκε· καὶ τῆς οὐσίας τῶν ὄντων ὑπεράνω γενόμενος, τὸν τῆς θείας καὶ ἀμάχου μονάδος δέχεται φωτισμὸν, καθ᾿ ὃν τῆς ἀληθοῦς θεολογίας συνέστηκε τὸ μυστήριον.

ϟεʹ. Ἑκάστῳ κατὰ τὴν ὑποκειμένην αὐτῷ περὶ Θεοῦ δόξαν, ὁ Θεὸς ἐμφανίζεται· τοῖς μὲν κατ᾿ ἔφεσιν τὴν ὑλικὴν περάσασι σύνθεσιν, καὶ τὰς δυνάμεις τῆς ψυχῆς ἰσοδυναμούσας ἀλλήλαις κεκτημένοις κατὰ μίαν καὶ τὴν αὐτὴν ἀεικινησίαν περὶ Θεὸν, ὡς μονὰς ἐκφαίνεται καὶ Τριὰς, ἵνα καὶ τὴν οἰκείαν ὕπαρξιν παραδείξειε, καὶ τὸν αὐτῆς τρόπον μυστικῶς ἐκδιδάξειε. Τοῖς δὲ περὶ μόνην τὴν ὑλικὴν σύνθεσιν ἔχουσι κινουμένην τὴν ἔφεσιν, καὶ ἀλλήλαις ἀσυνδέτους τὰς τῆς ψυχῆς δυνάμεις κεκτημένοις, οὐχ ὡς ἔστιν, ἀλλ᾿ ὡς εἰσὶν ἐμφανίζεται, δεικνὺς ὅτι τῆς ὑλικῆς δυάδος ἀμφοῖν ἐπελάβοντο ταῖν χεροῖν· καθ᾿ ἣν ὁ σωματικὸς, ὡς ἐξ ὕλης καὶ εἴδους, συνέστηκε κόσμος.

ϟϛʹ. Ὁ θεῖος Ἀπόστολος, τὰς διαφόρους ἐνεργείας τοῦ ἑνὸς ἁγίου Πνεύματος, χαρίσματα λέγει διάφορα, ὑφ᾿ ἑνὸς δηλονότι καὶ τοῦ αὐτοῦ ἐνεργούμενα Πνεύματος. Εἰ τοίνυν κατὰ τὸ μέτρον τῆς ἐν ἑκάστῳ πίστεως δίδοται ἡ φανέρωσις τοῦ Πνεύματος ἐν τῇ μετοχῇ τοῦ τοιοῦδε χαρίσματος, ἕκαστος τῶν πιστῶν δηλονότι κατὰ τὴν ἀναλογίαν τῆς πίστεως, καὶ τῆς ὑποκειμένης αὐτῷ κατὰ ψυχὴν διαθέσεως, συμμεμετρημένην δέχεται τοῦ Πνεύματος τὴν ἐνέργειαν, χαριζομένην αὐτῷ τῆσδε ἢ τῆσδε τῆς ἐντολῆς τὴν ἁρμόζουσαν πρὸς ἐνέργειαν ἕξιν.

ϟζʹ. Ὥσπερ ὁ μὲν λαμβάνει λόγον σοφίας, ὁ δὲ λόγον γνώσεως, ἕτερος δὲ, πίστεως, καὶ ἄλλος ἄλλοτι τῶν ἀπηριθμημένων τῷ μεγάλῳ Ἀποστόλῳ χαρισμάτων τοῦ Πνεύματος· οὕτως ὁ μὲν δέχεται διὰ τοῦ Πνεύματος χάρισμα τῆς τελείας καὶ ἀμέσου πρὸς Θεὸν καὶ μηδὲν ἐχούσης ὑλικὸν ἀγάπης, κατὰ τὴν ἀναλογίαν τῆς πίστεως· ἕτερος δὲ διὰ τοῦ αὐτοῦ Πνεύματος, τῆς τελείας ἀγάπης πρὸς τὸν πλησίον χάρισμα· ἄλλος δὲ ἄλλο τι, κατὰ τὸ αὐτὸ Πνεῦμα, ὡς ἔφην, ἔχοντος ἑκάστου ἐνεργούμενον τὸ οἰκεῖον χάρισμα. Πᾶσαν γὰρ ἐντολῆς ἕξιν ἐνεργουμένην, Πνεύματος εἶναι χάρισμα λέγει.

ϟηʹ. Τὸ τοῦ Κυρίου βάπτισμά ἐστιν, ἡ παντελὴς πρὸς τὸν αἰσθητὸν κόσμον τῆς προαιρέσεως ἡμῶν νέκρωσις· τὸ δὲ ποτήριον, καὶ αὐτῆς ἡμῶν τῆς παρούσης ζωῆς ὑπὲρ ἀληθείας καθέστηκεν ἄρνησις.

ϟθʹ.

Every contemplative mind, having the sword of the spirit, which is the word of God, Ephes. vi, 17. having slain within itself the motion of the visible creation, has achieved virtue; and having cut off from itself the imagination of sensible shapes, has found the truth in the principles of 1220beings, in which natural contemplation consists; and having come to be above the essence of beings, receives the illumination of the divine and unassailable Monad, in which the mystery of true theology consists.

95. To each, according to the opinion of God that underlies him, God appears: to those who in aspiration have passed beyond material composition, and possess the powers of the soul equal in power to one another according to one and the same ever-moving around God, he shows himself as Monad and Trinity, that he may both point out his own existence and mystically teach its mode. But to those who have their aspiration moving about material composition alone, and possess the powers of the soul unconnected with one another, he appears not as he is but as they are, showing that they have laid hold with both hands of the material dyad, by which the bodily world consists, as from matter and form.

96. The divine Apostle calls the different activities of the one Holy Spirit different gifts of grace, worked, clearly, by one and the same Spirit. I Cor. xii, 4. If, then, the manifestation of the Spirit is given according to the measure of the faith in each, in the participation of such and such a gift of grace, each of the faithful clearly receives, according to the proportion of his faith and of the disposition of soul that underlies him, the activity of the Spirit measured to it, bestowing on him the habit suited to the activity of this or that commandment.

97. As one receives a word of wisdom, another a word of knowledge, another faith, and another something else of the gifts of grace of the Spirit enumerated by the great Apostle, ibid. 8-12. so one receives through the Spirit the gift of grace of love perfect and immediate toward God and having nothing material, according to the proportion of faith; another, through the same Spirit, the gift of grace of perfect love toward the neighbour; and another something else, according to the same Spirit, as I said, each having his own gift of grace worked in him. For he says that every habit of a commandment that is worked is a gift of grace of the Spirit.

98. The baptism of the Lord is the complete mortification of our choice toward the sensible world; and the cup is the denial even of our present life itself for the sake of truth. Marc. x, 38.

99.

Τὸ τοῦ Κυρίου βάπτισμα, τῶν ὑπὲρ ἀρετῆς κατὰ πρόθεσιν ἑκουσίων καὶ ἡμετέρων πόνων, τύπος ὑπάρχει· δι᾿ ὧν τὰς κατὰ συνείδησιν ἀποῤῥύπτοντες κηλῖδας, τὸν πρὸς τὰ φαινόμενα τῆς προαιρέσεως ἑκούσιον καταδεχόμεθα θάνατον· τὸ δὲ ποτήριον, τῶν ἐκ περιστάσεως παρὰ προαίρεσιν ἐπανισταμένων ἡμῖν ὑπὲρ τῆς ἀληθείας ἀκουσίων πειρασμῶν 1221τύπος ἐστί· δι᾿ ὧν καὶ αὐτῆς προκρίνοντες τὸν θεῖον πόθον τῆς φύσεως, ἑκόντες τὸν περιστατικὸν ὑπερχόμεθα τῆς φύσεως θάνατον.

ρʹ. Ταύτην ἔχει τὴν διαφορὰν τὸ βάπτισμα πρὸς τὸ ποτήριον· ὅτι τὸ μὲν βάπτισμα, ὑπὲρ ἀρετῆς πρὸς τὰ ἡδέα τοῦ βίου νεκρὰν ἐργάζεται τὴν προαίρεσιν· τὸ δὲ ποτήριον, τὴν ἀλήθειαν καὶ αὐτῆς προκρίνειν πείτει τῆς φύσεως τοὺς εὐσεβεῖς.

ΕΚΑΤΟΝΤΑΣ ΔΕΥΤΕΡΑ.

αʹ. Πρότερον τοῦ βαπτίσματος ἔθετο τὸ ποτήριον, ὅτι διὰ τὴν ἀλήθειάν ἐστιν ἡ ἀρετὴ, ἀλλ᾿ οὐ διὰ τὴν ἀρετὴν ἡ ἀλήθεια. Ὅθεν ὁ διὰ τὴν ἀλήθειαν πράττων τὴν ἀρετὴν, κενοδοξίας οὐ τιτρώσκεται βέλεσιν· ὁ δὲ τὴν ἀλήθειαν ἀρετῆς ἕνεκεν ἐπιτηδεύων, σύνοικον ἔχει τῆς κενοδοξίας τὴν οἴησιν.

βʹ. Ἀλήθειαν μὲν εἶναι λέγει, τὴν θείαν γνῶσιν· ἀρετὴν δὲ, τοὺς ὑπὲρ ἀληθείας τῶν ἐφιεμένων αὐτῆς ἀγῶνας. Ὁ τοίνυν γνώσεως ἕνεκεν, ἀρετῆς ὑπομένων πόνους, οὐ κενοδοξεῖ, γινώσκων ἀπερίληπτον εἶναι φύσει τοῖς πόνοις τὴν ἀλήθειαν· τοῖς δευτέροις γὰρ οὐ περιγράφεται φύσει τὸ πρῶτον. Ὁ δὲ τὴν γνῶσιν διὰ τοὺς ὑπὲρ αὐτῆς ἀγῶνας ἐπιτηδεύων, πάντως κενόδοξος ὡς εἰληφέναι δοκῶν τοὺς στεφάνους πρὸ τῶν ἱδρώτων· οὐκ εἰδὼς ὅτι διὰ τοὺς στεφάνους οἱ πόνοι, ἀλλ᾿ οὐ διὰ τοὺς πόνους οἱ στέφανοι· φύσει γὰρ ἀμελέτητος πᾶσα καθέστηκε μέθοδος, τοῦ δι᾿ ὃ πέφυκεν ἐξανυσθέντος, ἢ ἀνυσθῆναι δόξαντος.

γʹ. Ὁ μόνην τῆς γνώσεως τὴν μορφὴν, ὅπερ ἐστὶν ὁ ψιλὸς λόγος· καὶ ὁ τὴν εἰκόνα τῆς ἀρετῆς, ὅπερ ἐστὶ τὸ ψιλὸν ἦθος, ἐπιτηδεύων, Ἰουδαῖός ἐστι καὶ αὐτὸς, ἀληθείας τύποις φυσιούμενος.

δʹ. Ὁ πᾶσαν τὴν φαινομένην τοῦ νόμου λατρείαν μὴ κατ᾿ αἴσθησιν ὁρῶν, ἀλλὰ ταῖς κατὰ νοῦν ἐφόδοις ἕκαστον τῶν ὁρωμένων συμβόλων διασκοπήσας, τὸν ἐν ἑκάστῳ κεκρυμμένον θεοτελῆ Λόγον ἐκδιδασκόμενος, ἐν τῷ νόμῳ τὸν Θεὸν εὑρίσκει, καλῶς ψηλαφῶν διὰ τῆς νοερᾶς δυνάμεως, ὡς ἐν φορυτῷ, τῇ ὕλῃ τῶν νομικῶν διατάξεων, εἴ που κεκρυμμένον εὕροι τῇ σαρκὶ τοῦ νόμου, τὸν τὴν αἴσθησιν διαφεύγοντα παντελῶς μαργαρίτην Λόγον.

εʹ.

The baptism of the Lord is a type of the toils, voluntary and our own, taken up by intent for the sake of virtue; through which, washing off the stains of conscience, we accept the voluntary death of our choice toward the things that appear; and the cup is a type of the involuntary temptations which, from distress and against our choice, rise up against us for the sake of the truth; 1221through which, preferring the divine longing even to nature itself, we willingly undergo the death of nature that comes by distress.

100. The baptism has this difference from the cup: that the baptism, for the sake of virtue, makes the choice dead toward the pleasant things of life; while the cup persuades the pious to prefer the truth even to nature itself.

SECOND CENTURY

1. He set the cup before the baptism, because virtue is for the sake of truth, but not truth for the sake of virtue. Hence he who does virtue for the sake of truth is not wounded by the arrows of vainglory; but he who cultivates truth for the sake of virtue has the conceit of vainglory living with him.

2. By truth he means the divine knowledge; by virtue, the struggles for truth of those who aspire to it. He, then, who for the sake of knowledge endures the toils of virtue is not vainglorious, knowing that truth is by nature not to be grasped by the toils; for what is first is not by nature circumscribed by what is second. But he who cultivates knowledge for the sake of the struggles on its behalf is altogether vainglorious, as thinking he has received the crowns before the sweat; not knowing that the toils are for the sake of the crowns, but not the crowns for the sake of the toils; for every method by nature falls out of exercise once that for which it exists has been accomplished, or seems to have been accomplished.

3. He who cultivates only the form of knowledge, which is the bare word, and he who cultivates the image of virtue, which is the bare manner, is himself also a Jew, puffed up with types of the truth.

4. He who sees the whole visible worship of the Law not according to sense, but has examined each of the visible symbols by the approaches of the mind, being taught the God-perfect Word hidden in each, finds God in the Law, groping well with the noetic power, as in a heap of sweepings, in the matter of the legal ordinances, whether he may somewhere find, hidden in the flesh of the Law, the pearl, the Word, which altogether escapes sense.

5.

Καὶ ὁ τὴν φύσιν δὲ τῶν ὁρωμένων μὴ τῇ αἰσθήσει μόνῃ περιγράφων, ἀλλὰ κατὰ νοῦν σοφῶς τὸν 1224ἐν ἑκάστῳ κτίσματι λόγον ἐρευνώμενος, εὑρίσκει Θεὸν, ἀπὸ τῆς προβεβλημένης τῶν ὄντων μεγαλουργίας, τὴν αὐτῶν τῶν ὄντων διδασκόμενος αἰτίαν.

ϛʹ. Ἐπειδὴ τοίνυν ἴδιον τοῦ ψηλαφῶντος ἡ διάκρισίς ἐστιν· ὁ τὰ νομικὰ σύμβολα γνωστικῶς ἐπερχόμενος, καὶ τὴν φαινομένην τῶν ὄντων φύσιν ἐπιστημονικῶς θεώμενος, διακρίνων τὴν Γραφὴν καὶ τὴν κτίσιν καὶ ἑαυτὸν, τὴν μὲν Γραφὴν, εἰς γράμμα καὶ πνεῦμα· τὴν δὲ κτίσιν, εἰς λόγον καὶ ἐπιφάνειαν· ἑαυτὸν δὲ, εἰς νοῦν καὶ αἴσθησιν· καὶ τῆς μὲν Γραφῆς, τὸ πνεῦμα· τῆς δὲ κτίσεως, τὸν λόγον· ἑαυτοῦ δὲ, τὸν νοῦν λαβὼν, καὶ ἀλλήλοις ἀλύτως ἑνώσας, εὗρε Θεόν· ὡς ἐπιγνοὺς καθὼς δεῖ, καὶ δυνατόν ἐστι, τὸν Θεὸν, τὸν ἐν Νῷ καὶ Λόγῳ καὶ Πνεύματι· πάντων τῶν πλανώντων καὶ εἰς μυρίας δόξας κατασυρόντων ἀπαλλαγεὶς, λέγω δὲ γράμματος καὶ ἐπιφανείας καὶ αἰσθήσεως, ἐν οἷς ἡ διάφορος ὑπάρχει ποσότης, καὶ τῆς μονάδος ἀντίθετος. Εἰ δὲ τὸ γράμμα τοῦ νόμου, καὶ τὴν τῶν ὁρωμένων ἐπιφάνειαν καὶ τὴν οἰκείαν αἴσθησιν ἀλλήλοις τις προσπλέξας συμφύρῃ, τυφλός ἐστι μυωπάζων, τὴν τῆς Αἰτίας τῶν ὄντων ἀγνωσίαν νοσῶν.

ζʹ. Ὁ θεῖος καὶ μέγας Ἀπόστολος, τί ἐστι πίστις ὁρίζων, φησί· Πίστις ἐστὶν, ἐλπιζομένων ὑπόστεσις, καὶ πραγμάτων ἔλεγχος οὐ βλεπομένων. Εἰ δέ τις καὶ ἐνδιάθετον αὐτὴν ἀγαθὸν ὁρίσαιτο, ἡ γνῶσιν ἀληθῆ τῶν ἀποῤῥήτων ἀγαθῶν ἀποδεικτικὴν, τῆς ἀληθείας οὐ διήμαρτεν.

ηʹ. Ἡ πίστις, δύναμίς ἐστι σχετικὴ, ἢ σχέσις δραστικὴ τῆς ὑπὲρ φύσιν ἀμέσου τοῦ πιστεύοντος πρὸς τὸν πιστευόμενον Θεὸν, τελείας ἑνώσεως.

θʹ.

And he too who does not circumscribe the nature of visible things by sense alone, but wisely searches out with the mind 1224the principle in each creature, finds God, being taught from the magnificence of beings set forth the cause of beings themselves. Sap. xiii, 5.

6. Since, then, distinction is proper to him who gropes, he who goes through the symbols of the Law in the way of knowledge, and contemplates the visible nature of beings scientifically, distinguishing Scripture and creation and himself — Scripture into letter and spirit; creation into principle and surface; himself into mind and sense — and taking, of Scripture, the spirit; of creation, the principle; of himself, the mind; and uniting them unbreakably with one another, has found God; as having recognized, as is fitting and as is possible, God who is in Mind and Word and Spirit; having been freed from all the things that lead astray and drag down into countless opinions, I mean letter and surface and sense, in which is the differing quantity, opposed to the Monad. But if anyone intertwines the letter of the Law and the surface of visible things and his own sense with one another and commixes them, he is blind, short-sighted, sick with ignorance of the Cause of beings.

7. The divine and great Apostle, defining what faith is, says: Faith is the hypostasis of things hoped for, the proof of things not seen. Hebr. xi, 1. And if anyone should define it also as an inward good, or as true knowledge demonstrative of the ineffable goods, he would not have missed the truth.

8. Faith is a relational power, or an efficacious relation, of the perfect, immediate union, above nature, of him who believes with the God in whom he believes.

9.

Ἐπειδὴ τοίνυν ὁ ἄνθρωπος ἐκ ψυχῆς ὑπάρχων καὶ σώματος, δυσὶ σαλεύεται νόμοις, σαρκὸς λέγω καὶ πνεύματος· καὶ ὁ μὲν τῆς σαρκὸς νόμος, κατὰ τὴν αἴσθησιν· ὁ δὲ τοῦ πνεύματος, κατὰ τὸν νοῦν κέκτηται τὴν ἐνέργειαν· καὶ ὁ μὲν τῆς σαρκὸς ὕλῃ συνδεῖν πέφυκε, κατὰ τὴν αἴσθησιν ἐνεργούμενος· ὁ δὲ τοῦ πνεύματος κατὰ νοῦν ἐνεργούμενος, πρὸς τὸν Θεὸν ἀμέσως ποιεῖται τὴν ἕνωσιν· εἰκότως Ὁ μὴ διακριθεὶς ἐν τῇ καρδίᾳ αὐτοῦ· τουτέστιν, ἐν τῷ νοῖ μὴ διακρίνας, ἤγουν διατεμὼν τὴν διὰ τῆς πίστεως πρὸς τὸν Θεὸν γεγενημένην ἄμεσον ἕνωσιν, ὡς ἀπαθὴς, μᾶλλον δὲ θεὸς ἤδη διὰ τῆς πίστεως τῇ ἑνώσει γεγενημένος, ἐρεῖ τῷ ὄρει τούτῳ μεταβῆναι, καὶ μεταβήσεται· 1225δεικτικῶς διὰ τοῦ τοῦτο φάναι, τὸ φρόνημα καὶ τὸν νόμον δηλῶν τῆς σαρκὸς, τὸν βαρὺν ὄντως καὶ δυσμετακίνητον, καὶ ὅσον πρὸς δύναμιν φυσικὴν, παντελῶς ἀκίνητον καὶ ἀσάλευτον.

ιʹ. Τοσοῦτον ἐῤῥίζωται τῇ φύσει τῶν ἀνθρώπων διὰ τῆς αἰσθήσεως ἡ τῆς ἀλογίας δύναμις, ὥστε τοὺς πολλοὺς μηδ᾿ ἄλλο τι νομίζειν εἶναι τὸν ἄνθρωπον, ἢ σάρκα, δύναμιν πρὸς ἀπόλαυσιν τῆς παρούσης ζωῆς τὴν αἴσθησιν ἔχουσαν.

ιαʹ. Πάντα, φησὶ, δυνατὰ τῷ πιστεύοντι, καὶ μὴ διακρινομένῳ, τουτέστι μὴ διαιρουμένῳ τῆς κατὰ νοῦν διὰ πίστεως γεγενημένης αὐτῷ πρὸς τὸν Θεὸν ἑνώσεως, διὰ τὴν πρὸς τὸ σῶμα τῆς ψυχῆς κατὰ τὴν αἴσθησιν σχέσιν· ὅσα κόσμου καὶ σαρκὸς τὸν νοῦν ἀλλοτριοῖ· Θεῷ δὲ προσοικειοῖ, τετελειωμένον τοῖς κατορθώμασι.Τοῦτο γὰρ προσυπακουστέον τῷ, Πάντα δυνατὰ τῷ πιστεύοντι.

ιβʹ. Ἡ πίστις ἀναπόδεικτος γνῶσίς ἐστιν. Εἰ δὲ γνῶσίς ἐστιν ἀναπόδεικτος, ἄρα σχέσις ἐστὶν ὑπὲρ φύσιν ἡ πίστις, δι᾿ ἧς ἀγνώστως, ἀλλ᾿ οὐκ ἀποδεικτικῶς ἑνούμεθα τῷ Θεῷ κατὰ τὴν ὑπὲρ νόησιν ἕνωσιν.

ιγʹ. Τὴν ἄμεσον λαβὼν ἕνωσιν πρὸς τὸν Θεὸν ὁ νοῦς, τὴν τοῦ νοεῖν καὶ νοεῖσθαι παντελῶς δύναμιν ἔχει σχολάζουσαν. Ὁπηνίκα γοῦν ταύτην λύσει, νοήσας τι τῶν μετὰ Θεὸν, διεκρίθη, τεμὼν τὴν ὑπὲρ νόησιν ἕνωσιν, καθ᾿ ἣν ἕως ἐστὶ τῷ Θεῷ συνημμένος, ὡς ὑπὲρ φύσιν καὶ τῇ μεθέξει Θεὸς γεγενημένος, καθάπερ ὄρος ἀκίνητον, ἑαυτοῦ τὸν τῆς φύεως μετατίθησι νόμον.

ιδʹ. Ὁ πρὸς εὐσέβειαν εἰσαγόμενος, περὶ τῶν ἔργων διδασκόμενος τῆς δικαιοσύνης, τὴν πρᾶξιν μόνην ἐπιτελεῖ, μετὰ πάσης ὑπακοῆς καὶ πίστεως, καθάπερ σάρκας ἐσθίων τῶν ἀρετῶν τὰ φαινόμενα, τὴν ἠθικὴν δηλονότι παιδαγωγίαν· τοὺς δὲ τῶν ἐντολῶν λόγους, ἐν οἷς ἡ τῶν τελείων ὑπάρχει γνῶσις, τῷ Θεῷ παραχωρεῖ διὰ τῆς πίστεως, μὴ δυνάμενος συνεπεκταθῆναι τέως τῷ μήκει τῆς γνώσεως.

ιεʹ.

Since, then, man, being of soul and body, is tossed by two laws, I mean of flesh and of spirit; and the law of the flesh has its activity according to sense, and that of the spirit according to the mind; and the law of the flesh is wont to bind to matter, being active according to sense, while that of the spirit, being active according to the mind, effects the union with God immediately: fittingly he who is not two-minded in his heart — that is, who has not distinguished in the mind, or cut asunder, the immediate union with God that has come about through faith — as passionless, or rather as one already become god through faith by the union, will say to this mountain to move, and it will move; 1225by saying this, showing as with a pointing finger the way of thinking and the law of the flesh, which is truly heavy and hard to shift, and, as far as natural power goes, altogether immovable and unshakeable.

10. So deeply has the power of irrationality been rooted in the nature of men through sense, that the many think man to be nothing else than flesh, having sense as a power for the enjoyment of the present life.

11. All things, he says, are possible to him who believes, Marc. ix, 22. and is not two-minded, that is, who is not divided from the union with God that has come about for him in the mind through faith, because of the relation of the soul to the body according to sense: all things, that is, that estrange the mind from world and flesh, and make it akin to God, perfected by its achievements. For this must be understood in addition to the words All things are possible to him who believes.

12. Faith is knowledge without demonstration. And if it is knowledge without demonstration, then faith is a relation above nature, through which we are united to God unknowingly, and not demonstratively, by the union above intellection.

13. The mind that has received the immediate union with God has the power of thinking and being thought altogether idle. Whenever, then, it looses this, having thought of something of the things that come after God, it has become two-minded, cutting the union above intellection, according to which, so long as it is joined to God, as having become above nature and god by participation, it removes from itself, like an immovable mountain, the law of nature.

14. He who is being introduced to piety, being taught about the works of justice, performs the practice alone, with all obedience and faith, as though eating, like flesh, the visible things of the virtues, that is, the moral instruction; but the principles of the commandments, in which the knowledge of the perfect consists, he yields to God through faith, being unable as yet to stretch himself out along with the length of knowledge.

15.

Ὁ τέλειος, οὐ μόνον τὴν τῶν εἰσαγομένων τάξιν, ἀλλὰ καὶ τὴν τῶν προκοπτόντων διαδραμὼν, οὐκ ἀγνοεῖ τῶν ὑπ᾿ αὐτοῦ κατ᾿ ἐντολὴν γινομένων τοὺς λόγους, ἀλλ᾿ ἐκείνους πνεύματι πρῶτον δισοὒν, πᾶσαν ἐσθίει διὰ τῶν ἔργων τὴν σάρκα τῶν

1228ἀρετῶν, πρὸς τὴν κατὰ νοῦν γνῶσιν ἀναβιβάζων, τὴν τῶν γινομένων κατ᾿ αἴσθησιν κίνησιν.

ιϛʹ. Ὁ Κύριος εἰπὼν, Ζητεῖτε πρῶτον τὴν βασιλείαν τοῦ Θεοῦ, καὶ τὴν δικαιοσύνην αὐτοῦ· τουτέστι, πρὸ πάντων τὴν ἐπίγνωσιν τῆς ἀληθείας, καὶ οὕτω τὴν τῶν καθηκόντων τρόπων ἐξάσκησιν· σαφῶς ἔδειξε, περὶ μόνης τῆς θείας γνώσεως δεῖν ζητεῖν τοὺς πιστεύοντας, καὶ τῆς αὐτὴν κοσμούσης διὰ τῶν ἔργων ἀρετῆς.

ιζʹ. Ἐπειδὴ πολλὰ τυγχάνει τὰ πρὸς γνῶσιν Θεοῦ καὶ ἀρετὴν ζητούμενα τοῖς πιστεύουσιν, ἀπὰλλαγὴ παθῶν, ὑπομονὴ πειρασμῶν, ἀρετῶν λόγοι, τρόποι ἐνεργειῶν, ἐξήλωσις τῆς πρὸς σάρκα τῆς ψυχῆς διαθέσεως, ἀποξένωσις τῆς πρὸς τὰ αἰσθητὰ τῆς αἰσθήσεως σχέσεως, τοῦ νοῦ παντελὴς ἀπὸ πάντων τῶν γεγονότων ἀναχώρησις. Καὶ ἀπλῶς μυρία ἄλλα εἰσὶ, τὰ πρὸς ἀποχὴν μὲν κακίας καὶ ἀγνωσίας, κατόρθωσιν δὲ γνώσεως καὶ ἀρετῆς· εἰκότως ὁ Κύριος ἔφησεν, Πάντα ὅσα ἂν αἰτῆσθε πιστεύοντες, λήψεσθε· πάντα ἁπλῶς, τὰ πρὸς ἐπίγνωσιν Θεοῦ καὶ ἀρετὴν συντείνοντα μόνα καὶ ζητεῖν καὶ αἰτεῖν μετ᾿ ἐπιστήμης καὶ πίστεως δεῖν εἰπὼν τοὺς εὐσεβεῖς. Ταῦτα γὰρ πάντα συμφέρει, καὶ πάντως δίδωσιν αὐτὰ τοῖς αἰτοῦσιν ὁ Κύριος.

ιηʹ. Ὁ τοίνυν διὰ μόνην τὴν πίστιν, ἤγουν τὴν πρὸς Θεὸν ἄμεσον ἕνωσιν, πάντα ζητῶν τὰ πρὸς τὴν ἕνωσιν, πάντως λήψεται· ὁ δὲ ταύτης δίχα τῆς αἰτίας, κἂν ἄλλα, κἂν τὰ προειρημένα ζητῇ, οὐ λήψεται. Οὐ γὰρ πιστεύει, ἀλλ᾿ οἰκείαν ὡς ἅπωστος διὰ τῶν θείων πραγματεύεται δόξαν.

ιθʹ. Ὁ γνώμῃ τῆς καθ᾿ ἁμαρτίαν καθαρεύων φθορᾶς, φθείρει τὴν τῶν φθείρειν πεφυκότων φθοράν. Ἡ γὰρ ἀφθαρσία τῆς προαιρέσεως, τὴν φθορὰν συντηρεῖν τῆς φύσεως πέφυκεν ἄφθαρτον, κατὰ πρόνοιαν, διὰ τῆς ἐν αὐτῇ τοῦ Πνεύματος χὲρν τος, μὴ συγχωροῦσα ταῖς ἐναντίαις ποιότησιν αὐτὴν διαφθείρεσθαι.

κʹ. Ἐπειδὴ φύσεως καὶ χάριτος εἱ̓͂ς καὶ ὁ αὐτὸς οὐκ ἔστι λόγος, οὐκ ἔστιν ἀπορίας ἄξιον, πῶς τιο͂ς τῶν ἁγίων, ποτὲ μὲν παθῶν ὑπερεῖχον, ποτὲ δὲ μᾶλλον ὑπέπιπτον πάθεσι· γινωσκόντων ἡμῶν, ὅτι τὸ μὲν θαῦμα, τῆς χάριτος ἦν· τὸ δὲ πάθος, τῆς φύσεως.

καʹ. Ὁ διὰ μιμήσεως τὴν μνήμην ἔχων τῆς τῶν ἁγίων ἀναστροφῆς, τὴν μὲν τῶν παθῶν ἀποτίδθεται νέκρωσιν· τὴν δὲ τῶν ἀρετῶν ζωὴν ὑποδέχεται.

κβʹ. ᾯ τρόπῳ βούλεται ὁ τὴν ἑκάστου διορίσας ζωὴν πρὸ τῶν αἰώνων Θεὸς, ἕκαστον πρὸς τὸ οἰκεῖον τῆς ζωῆς ἄγει τέλος, εἴτε δίκαιον, εἴτε ἄδικον.

κγʹ.

The perfect man, having run through not only the rank of beginners but also that of those progressing, is not ignorant of the principles of the things done by him according to the commandment; but, having first drunk them through in spirit, he eats through works the whole flesh of the

1228virtues, raising up to knowledge according to the mind the motion of the things that come to be according to sense.

16. The Lord, saying, Seek first the kingdom of God and his righteousness Matth. vi, 33. — that is, before all things the full knowledge of the truth, and so the exercise of the fitting modes — clearly showed that those who believe must make their inquiry concerning divine knowledge alone, and the virtue that adorns it through works.

17. Since the things sought by those who believe toward the knowledge of God and virtue happen to be many — release from passions, endurance of temptations, principles of virtues, modes of activities, the unnailing of the soul's disposition toward the flesh, the estrangement of sense's relation to sensible things, the mind's complete withdrawal from all things that have come to be. And, simply, there are countless others, those that make for abstinence from vice and ignorance, and for the achievement of knowledge and virtue: fittingly the Lord said, All things whatsoever you ask believing, you shall receive; Matth. xxi, 22. saying that the pious must both seek and ask, with science and faith, simply all things — only those that tend toward the full knowledge of God and virtue. For all these are profitable, and the Lord certainly gives them to those who ask.

18. He, then, who for the sake of faith alone, that is, the immediate union with God, seeks all the things that make for the union, will certainly receive; but he who, without this cause, seeks either other things or the things aforesaid, will not receive. For he does not believe, but as an unbeliever he trades on the divine things for his own glory.

19. He who by inclination keeps pure of the corruption that is according to sin corrupts the corruption of the things whose nature it is to corrupt. For the incorruption of choice is of a nature to keep the corruption of nature incorrupt, according to providence, through the grace of the Spirit that is in it, not permitting it to be corrupted by the contrary qualities.

20. Since there is not one and the same principle of nature and of grace, it is not worth puzzling over how some of the saints at times rose above passions and at times rather fell under passions; since we know that the wonder was of grace, and the passion of nature.

21. He who through imitation holds the memory of the way of life of the saints puts off the deadness of the passions and takes up the life of the virtues.

22. In the manner that he wills, God, who marked out the life of each before the ages, leads each to the end proper to his life, whether just or unjust.

23.

Τὸν τῷ μακαρίῳ Παύλῳ συμβάντα ζοφερὸν χειμῶνα, τὸ βάρος εἶναι τῶν ἀκουσίων πειρασμῶν τεκμαίρομαι· τὴν δὲ νῆσον, τὴν παγίαν ἕξιν τῆς 1229θείας ἐλπίδος καὶ ἄσειστον· τὴν δὲ πυρὰν, τὴν ἕξιν τῆς γνώσεως· τὰ δὲ φρύγανα, τὴν τῶν ὁρωμένων φύσιν· ἣν συνέστρεφε μὲν τῇ χειρὶ, τῇ κατὰ θεωρίαν λέγω ψηλαφητικῇ τοῦ νοῦ δυνάμει, διατρέφων τοῖς ἐξ αὐτῆς νοήμασι τὴν ἕξιν τῆς γνώσεως, τὴν θεραπεύουσαν τὴν ἐκ τοῦ χειμῶνος τῶν πειρασμῶν προστριβεῖσαν τῇ διανοίᾳ κατήφειαν· τὴν δὲ ἔχιδναν, τὴν λανθανόντως ἐγκεκρυμμένην τῇ φύσει τῶν αἰσθητῶν πονηρὰν καὶ ὀλέθριον δύναμιν· δήξασαν μὲν τὴν χεῖρα, τουτέστι, τὴν κατὰ νοῦν ψηλαφητικὴν τῆς θεωρίας ἐνέργειαν· οὐ βλάψασαν δὲ τὸν διορατικὸν νοῦν, τῷ φωτὶ τῆς γνώσεως αὐτὴν εὐθὺς ὡς ἐν πυρὶ διαφθείραντα, τὴν τῷ πρακτικῷ κινήματι τοῦ νοῦ, προσαφθεῖσαν ἐκ τῆς τῶν αἰσθητῶν θεωρίας, ὀλέθριον δύναμιν.

κδʹ. Ὀσμὴ ἦν ἀπὸ ζωῆς εἰς ζωὴν ὁ Ἀπόστολος, ὡς τοὺς πιστοὺς τῷ καθ᾿ ἑαυτὸν ὑποδείγματι, διὰ πράξεως πρὸς τὴν εὐωδίαν τῶν ἀρετῶν κινεῖσθαι, παρασκευάζων· ἢ τοὺς πειθομένους τῷ λόγῳ τῆς χάριτος, ὡς κήρυξ ἀπὸ τῆς κατ᾿ αἴσθησιν ζωῆς, εἰς τὴν ἐν πνεύματι μετάγων ζωήν. Ὀσμὴ δὲ θανάτου εἰς θάνατον, τοῖς ἀπὸ τοῦ θανάτου τῆς ἀγνοίας εἰς τὸν τῆς ἀπιστίας ἐλάσασι θάνατον, διδοὺς αὐτοῖς αἴσθησιν τῆς μενούσης αὐτοὺς κατακρίσεως. Ἢ πάλιν ὀσμὴ ἀπὸ ζωῆς εἰς ζωὴν, τοῖς ἀπὸ πράξεως εἰς θεωρίαν ἀναγομένοις· ὀσμὴ δὲ θανάτου εἰς θάνατον, τοῖς ἀπὸ τοῦ νεκρῶσαι τῇ καθ᾿ ἁμαρτίαν ἀργίᾳ τὰ μέλη τὰ ἐπὶ τῆς γῆς, εἰς τὴν τῶν ἐμπαθῶν νοημάτων τε καὶ φαντασιῶν ἐπαινουμένην μεταβαίνουσι νέκρωσιν.

κεʹ. Τρεῖς εἰσιν αἱ τῆς ψυχῆς δυνάμεις, λόγος, θυμὸς, καὶ ἐπιθυμία. Τῷ μὲν λόγῳ, ζητοῦμεν· τῇ ἐπιθυμίᾳ δὲ, ποθοῦμεν τὸ ζητηθὲν ἀγαθόν· τῷ δὲ θυμῷ, ὑπεραγωνιζόμεθα. Κατὰ ταύτας δὴ τὰς δυνάμεις προσμένοντες οἱ τὸν Θεὸν ἀγαπῶντες, τῷ θείῳ λόγῳ τῆς ἀρετῆς καὶ τῆς γνώσεως· τῇ μὲν, ζητοῦντες· τῇ δὲ, ποθοῦντες· τῇ δὲ, ὑπεραγωνιζόμενοι, δέχονται τροφὴν ἄφθαρτον, καὶ τὸν νοῦν πιαίνουσαν ·τὴν γνῶσιν τῶν γεγονότων.

κϛʹ. Τὴν κενωθεῖσαν τῆς δοθείσης γνώσεως φύσιν, γενόμενος ἄνθρωπος ὁ τοῦ Θεοῦ Λόγος, πάλιν ἐπλήρωσε γνώσεως· καὶ στομώσας πρὸς ἀτρεψίαν, οὐ φύσει, ποιότητι δὲ, ταύτην ἐθέωσεν· ἀνελλιπῶς αὐτὴν τῷ οἰκείῳ χαρακτηρίσας Πνεύματι· καθάπερ ὕδωρ, οἴνου ποιότητι πρὸς τόνον μετακεράσας. Διὰ τοῦτο γὰρ καὶ γίνεται κατ᾿ ἀλήθειαν ἄνθρωπος, ἵνα κατὰ χάριν ἡμᾶς καταστήσῃ θεούς.

κζʹ.

The gloomy storm that befell the blessed Paul I judge to be the weight of involuntary temptations; the island, the firm habit of 1229divine hope, and unshakeable; the fire, the habit of knowledge; the sticks, the nature of visible things, which he gathered with his hand — I mean with the mind's tactile power according to contemplation — nourishing with the concepts drawn from it the habit of knowledge, which heals the dejection rubbed onto the understanding by the storm of temptations; the viper, the wicked and destructive power secretly hidden in the nature of sensible things, which bit the hand, that is, the tactile activity of contemplation according to the mind, but did not harm the clear-sighted mind, which at once destroyed by the light of knowledge, as in fire, the destructive power fastened onto the practical movement of the mind from the contemplation of sensible things.

24. The Apostle was a savour from life unto life, as preparing the faithful, by the example of himself, to be moved through practice toward the fragrance of the virtues; or as a herald carrying over those persuaded by the word of grace from the life according to sense into the life in the spirit. And a savour of death unto death to those who from the death of ignorance had driven on into the death of unbelief, giving them a sense of the condemnation that awaits them. Or again, a savour from life unto life to those who are led up from practice to contemplation; and a savour of death unto death to those who, from making dead, by idleness as to sin, the members that are upon the earth, Coloss. iii, 5. pass over into the praised deadness of passionate concepts and imaginations.

25. Three are the powers of the soul: reason, anger, and desire. By reason we seek; by desire we long for the good that was sought; by anger we contend on its behalf. According to these powers, then, those who love God, persevering in the divine word of virtue and of knowledge — by the one seeking, by the other longing, by the other contending on its behalf — receive food incorruptible and fattening the mind: the knowledge of the things that have come to be.

26. The nature emptied of the knowledge that had been given it, the Word of God, having become man, filled again with knowledge; and, tempering it to unchangeableness, not by nature but by quality, he deified it, stamping it without deficiency with his own Spirit, as one who blends water with the quality of wine for strength. For it is for this that he truly becomes man, that by grace he may make us gods.

27.

Ὁ τὴν φύσιν τῶν ἀνθρώπων δημιουργήσας Θεὸς, ἅμα βουλήσει τὸ εἶναι αὐτῇ δέδωκεν, συνήρμοσεν αὐτῇ καὶ δύναμιν τῶν καθηκόντων ποιητικήν· δύναμιν δὲ λέγω, τὴν οὐσιωδῶς μὲν κατεσπαρμένην 1232τῇ φύσει, πρὸς ἀρετῶν ἐνέργειαν κίνησιν· γνωμικῶς δὲ, πρὸς τὴν τοῦ κεκτημένου βούλησιν, κατὰ τὴν χρῆσιν ἐκφαινομένην.

κηʹ. Φυσικὸν ἔχομεν κριτήριον, τὸν κατὰ φύσιν νόμον, διδάσκοντα ἡμᾶς, ὅτι πρὸς τῆς ἐν ὅλοις σοφίας, ἔδει πρὸς τὴν τοῦ ποιητοῦ τῶν ὅλων μυσταγωγίαν ἡμῶν κινεῖσθαι τὴν ἔφεσιν.

κθʹ. Τὸ φρέαρ ἐστὶ τοῦ Ἰακὼβ, ἡ Γραφή· τὸ δὲ ὕδωρ ἐστὶν, ἡ ἐν τῇ Γραφῇ γνῶσις· τὸ δὲ βάθος, ἡ τῶν Γραφικῶν αἰνιγμάτων δυσδιεξίτητος θέσις· τὸ δὲ ἄντλημα ἐστιν, ἡ διὰ τῶν γραμμάτων τοῦ θείου λόγου μάθησις, ἣν οὐκ εἶχεν ὁ Κύριος Αὐτολόγος ὑπάρχων, καὶ οὐ τὴν ἐκ μαθήσεως καὶ μελέτης διδοὺς γνῶσιν τοῖς πιστεύουσιν, ἀλλὰ τὴν ἐκ χάριτος πνευματικῆς ἀένναον σοφίαν, καὶ μηδέποτε λήγουσαν τοῖς ἀξίοις δωρούμενος. Τὸ γὰρ ἄντλημα, τουτέστιν ἡ μάθησις, μέρος ἐλάχιστον λαμβάνουσα γνώσεως, τὸ πᾶν ἐᾷ μηδενὶ λόγῳ κρατούμενον. Ἡ δὲ κατὰ χάριν γνῶσις, ὅλην ἔχει καὶ δίχα μελέτης, τὴν ἐφικτὴν ἀνθρώποις σοφίαν, πρὸς τὰς χρείας ποικίλως βλυστάνουσαν.

λʹ. Τὸ ξύλον τῆς ζωῆς, καὶ τὸ μὴ τοιοῦτον, ἐξ αὐτοῦ μόνου, τοῦ, τὸ μὲν ζωῆς ξύλον ὀνομασθῆναι· τὸ δὲ, οὐ ζωῆς, ἀλλὰ μόνον γνωστὸν καλοῦ καὶ πονηροῦ, πολλὴν καὶ ἄφατον ἔχουσι τὴν διαφοράν. Τὸ γὰρ τῆς ζωῆς ξύλον, πάντως καὶ ζωῆς ἐστι ποιητικόν· τὸ δὲ μὴ τῆς ζωῆς ξύλον, δηλονότι θανάτου ποιητικόν. Τὸ γὰρ μὴ ποιητικὸν ζωῆς ἐκ τοῦ μὴ προσαγορευθῆναι ξύλον τῆς ζωῆς, θανάτου σαφῶς ἂν εἴη ποιητικόν· ἄλλο γὰρ οὐδὲν τῇ ζωῇ κατ᾿ ἐναντίωσιν ἀντιδιαιρεῖται.

λαʹ. Τὸ ξύλον τῆς ζωῆς, καὶ ὡς σοφία, πλείστην ἔχει διαφορὰν πρὸς τὸ ξύλον τὸ γνωστικὸν καλοῦ καὶ πονηροῦ, τὸ μήτε ὂν σοφία, μήτε ὠνομασμένον. Τῆς μὲν γὰρ σοφίας ἴδιον, νοῦς καὶ λόγος· τῆς δὲ τῇ σοφίᾳ κατὰ τὸ ἐναντίον ἀντικειμένης ἕξεως ἴδιον, ἀλογία καὶ αἴσθησις.

λβʹ.

God, who created the nature of men, together with giving it being by his wish, fitted to it also a power productive of what is fitting; and by power I mean the motion that is essentially sown 1232in the nature toward the activity of the virtues, but by inclination is manifested, in use, according to the wish of its possessor.

28. We have a natural criterion, the law according to nature, which teaches us that before the wisdom in all things our aspiration ought to be moved toward the mystagogy of the maker of all things.

29. The well of Jacob is Scripture; the water is the knowledge in Scripture; the depth, the hard-to-penetrate setting of the enigmas of Scripture; the bucket is the learning, through the letters, of the divine word, which the Lord did not have, being the Word himself, and giving to those who believe not the knowledge that comes from learning and study, but bestowing on the worthy the ever-flowing wisdom that comes from spiritual grace, which never ceases. For the bucket, that is, learning, taking a very small part of knowledge, leaves the whole, grasped in no way. But the knowledge according to grace has, even without study, the whole of the wisdom attainable by men, welling up variously according to the needs.

30. The tree of life and that which is not such have, from this alone — that the one was named tree of life, and the other not of life, but only of knowledge of good and evil — a great and unspeakable difference. For the tree of life is certainly also productive of life; but the tree not of life is clearly productive of death. For what is not productive of life, from not having been called tree of life, would clearly be productive of death; for nothing else is divided over against life by opposition.

31. The tree of life, also as wisdom, has the greatest difference from the tree of knowing good and evil, which neither is wisdom nor is so named. For proper to wisdom are mind and reason; but proper to the habit set over against wisdom as its contrary are irrationality and sense.

32.

Ἐπειδὴ ἐκ ψυχῆς νοερᾶς καὶ σώματος αἰσθητικοῦ συνεστὼς πρὸς γένεσιν ἦλθεν ὁ ἄνθρωπος, ἔστω κατὰ μίαν ἐπιβολὴν ξύλον ζωῆς ὁ τῆς ψυχῆς νοῦς, ἐν ᾧ τῆς σοφίας ὑπάρχει τὸ χρῆμα· ξύλον δὲ γνωστὸν καλοῦ καὶ πονηροῦ, ἡ τοῦ σώματος αἴσθησις, ἐν ᾗ τῆς ἀλογίας ὑπάρχει σαφῶς ἡ κίνησις, 1233ἧς κατὰ τὴν πεῖραν, μὴ ἅψασθαι δι᾿ ἐνεργείας ὁ ἄνθρωπος τὴν θείαν λαβὼν ἐντολὴν, οὐκ ἐφύλαξεν.

λγʹ. Ἀμφότερα τὰ ξύλα κατὰ τὴν Γραφὴν, τινῶν εἰσι διακριτικὰ, ἤγουν ὁ νοῦς καὶ ἡ αἴσθησις· οἷον, ὁ μὲν νοῦς, ἔχει δύναμιν διακριτικὴν, νοητῶν καὶ αἰσθητῶν, προσκαίρων καὶ αἰωνίων· μᾶλλον δὲ ψυχῆς ὑπάρχων δύναμις διακριτικὴ, τῶν μὲν, αὐτὴν ἀντέχεσθαι πείθει· τῶν δὲ, ὑπεραίρεσθαι. Ἡ δὲ αἴσθησις ἔχει δύναμιν διακριτικὴν ἡδονῆς σωμάτων καὶ ὀδύνης· μᾶλλον δὲ δύναμις ὑπάρχουσα ἐμψύχων καὶ αἰσθητικῶν σωμάτων, τὴν μὲν ἡδονὴν ἐπισπᾶσθαι πείθει, τὴν δὲ ὀδύνην ἀποπέμπεσθαι.

λδʹ. Ὁ ἄνθρωπος, ἐὰν μὲν μόνης τῆς καθ᾿ ἡδονὴν καὶ ὀδύνην αἰσθητικῆς τῶν σωμάτων γένηται διακρίσεως, τὴν θείαν παραβὰς ἐντολὴν, ἐσθίει τὸ ξύλον τὸ γνωστὸν καλοῦ καὶ πονηροῦ· τουτέστι, τὴν κατ᾿ αἴσθησιν ἀλογίαν· ἔχων μόνην τὴν συστατικὴν τῶν σωμάτων διάκρισιν, καθ᾿ ἣν, ὡς μὲν καλοῦ, τῆς ἡδονῆς ἀντέχεται· ὡς δὲ κακοῦ, τῆς ὀδύνης ἀπέχεται. Ἐὰν δὲ μόνης τῆς τῶν αἰωνίων διακρινούσης τὰ πρόσκαιρα δι᾿ ὅλου γένηται νοερᾶς διακρίσεως, τὴν θείαν φυλάξας ἐντολὴν, ἐσθίει τὸ ξύλον τῆς ζωῆς· τὴν κατὰ νοῦν λέγω συνισταμένην σοφίαν· μόνην ἔχων τὴν συστατικὴν τῆς ψυχῆς διάκρισιν· καθ᾿ ἣν, ὡς μὲν καλοῦ, τῆς τῶν αἰωνίων ἀντέχεται δόξης· ὡς δὲ κακοῦ, τῆς τῶν προσκαίρων ἀπέχεται φθορᾶς.

λεʹ. Νοῦ μὲν καλόν ἐστιν, ἡ ἀπαθὴς πρὸς τὸ πνεῦμα διάθεσις· κακὸν δὲ, ἡ ἐμπαθὴς πρὸς τὴν αἴσθησιν σχέσις· αἰσθήσεως δὲ καλόν ἐστιν, ἡ καθ᾿ ἡδονὴν ἐμπαθὴς πρὸς σῶμα κίνησις· κακὸν δὲ, ἡ κατὰ στέρησιν ταύτης ἐπιγινομένη διάθεσις.

λϛʹ. Ὁ πείσας τὸ συνειδὸς ὡς φύσει καλὸν, πραττόμενον ἔχειν τὸ κάκιστον, οὗτος χειρὸς δίκην ἐκτείνας τὸ πρακτικὸν, ἔλαβε ψεκτῶς τοῦ ξύλου τῆς ζωῆς, ἀθάνατον ἡγησάμενος φύσει τὸ κάκιστον. Διόπερ τὴν κατὰ τὸ συνειδὸς τοῦ κακοῦ διαβολὴν τῷ ἀνθρώπῳ φυσικῶς ἐνθέμενος ὁ Θεὸς, διέκρινεν αὐτὸν τῆς ζωῆς, κακὸν τῇ προαιρέσει γεγενημένον· ἵνα μὴ τὸ κακὸν πράττων, δύνηται πεῖσαι τὴν ἰδίαν συνείδησιν, ὅτι φύσει καλὸν ὑπάρχει τὸ κάκιστον.

λζʹ Ἡ ἄμπελος, οἶνον ποιεῖ· ὁ οἶνος, μέθην· ἡ μέθη, ἔκστασιν. Οὐκοῦν ὁ ἐνεργὴς λόγος, ὅπερ ἐστὶν ἡ ἄμπελος, γεωργούμενος ταῖς ἀρεταῖς, γεννᾷ τὴν γνῶσιν· ἡ δὲ γνῶσις, γεννᾷ τὴν καλὴν ἔκστασιν, τὴν τὸν νοῦν τῆς κατ᾿ αἴσθησιν σχέσεως ἐξιστῶσαν.

ληʹ.

Since man came into coming-to-be composed of a noetic soul and a sentient body, let the tree of life be, by one approach, the mind of the soul, in which the very thing that wisdom is resides; and the tree of knowledge of good and evil, the sense of the body, in which clearly resides the motion of irrationality, 1233which man, having received the divine commandment not to touch it by way of experience through activity, did not keep. Gen. ii, 17.

33. Both trees according to Scripture, that is, the mind and sense, are able to distinguish certain things: for example, the mind has a power that distinguishes intelligibles and sensibles, temporal things and everlasting; or rather, being a distinguishing power of the soul, it persuades her to cling to the one and to rise above the other. And sense has a power that distinguishes pleasure and pain of bodies; or rather, being a power of ensouled and sentient bodies, it persuades them to draw pleasure to themselves and to send pain away.

34. Man, if he gives himself to the sensory distinction of bodies alone, according to pleasure and pain, transgressing the divine commandment, eats of the tree of knowledge of good and evil, that is, the irrationality according to sense; having only the distinction that sustains bodies, by which he clings to pleasure as good and holds off from pain as evil. But if he gives himself wholly to the noetic distinction alone, which distinguishes temporal things from the everlasting, keeping the divine commandment, he eats of the tree of life — I mean the wisdom that subsists according to the mind; having only the distinction that sustains the soul, by which he clings to the glory of everlasting things as good and holds off from the corruption of temporal things as evil.

35. The good of the mind is the passionless disposition toward the spirit; its evil, the passionate relation to sense. And the good of sense is the passionate motion toward the body according to pleasure; its evil, the disposition that supervenes by privation of this.

36. He who has persuaded his conscience to hold the worst, when it is done, as good by nature, he, stretching out his practical part like a hand, took blameworthily of the tree of life, thinking the worst immortal by nature. Wherefore God, having naturally placed in man the accusation of evil according to the conscience, set him apart from life, since he had become evil by choice; so that, doing evil, he might not be able to persuade his own conscience that the worst is good by nature.

37. The vine makes wine; wine, drunkenness; drunkenness, ecstasy. Therefore the active word, which is the vine, cultivated by the virtues, begets knowledge; and knowledge begets the good ecstasy, which puts the mind out of its relation to sense.

38.

Τοῖς τῶν αἰσθητῶν νοήμασιν εἴωθε κακούργως ὁ πονηρὸς συμβάλλειν τὰ τῶν αἰσθητῶν εἴδη καὶ σχήματα, δι᾿ ὧν πέφυκε τὰ πάθη δημιουργεῖσθαι περὶ τὰς ἐπιφανείας τῶν ὁρατῶν, στάσιν λαμβανούσης διὰ τῆς μέσης αἰσθήσεως τῆς περὶ τὰ νοητὰ 1236διαβάσεως, τῆς ἐν ἡμῖν λογικῆς ἐνεργείας. Διὸ καὶ κατισχύει πορθεῖν τὴν ψυχὴν, καὶ εἰς τὴν τῶν παθῶν σύγχυσιν κατασύρειν.

λθʹ. Λύχνος ἐστὶ, κατὰ ταυτὸν ὁμοῦ καὶ φῶς, ὁ τοῦ Θεοῦ λόγος, καὶ ὡς φωτίζων τοὺς κατὰ φύσιν λογισμοὺς τῶν πιστῶν, καὶ ὡς καίων τοὺς παρὰ φύσιν· καὶ ὡς λύων τὸν ζόφον τῆς κατ᾿ αἴσθησιν ζωῆς, τοῖς διὰ τῶν ἐντολῶν πρὸς τὴν ἐλπιζομένην ἐπειγομένοις ζωὴν, καὶ ὡς κολάζων τῇ καύσει τῆς κρίσεως τοὺς ταύτης τῆς σκοτεινῆς τοῦ βίου νυκτὸς κατὰ γνώμην διὰ φιλίαν σαρκὸς ἀντεχομένους.

μʹ. Ὁ μὴ πρὸς ἑαυτὸν, φησὶ, πρότερον ἀναχθεὶς, διὰ τῆς ἀποβολῆς τῶν παρὰ φύσιν παθῶν, πρὸς τὴν ἰδίαν αἰτίαν, ἤγουν τὸν Θεὸν, διὰ τῆς ἐν χάριτι τῶν ὑπὲρ φύσιν ἀγαθῶν ἑπικτήσεως, οὐκ ἀναχθήσεται. Τῶν γὰρ πεποιημένων χωρισθῆναι δεῖ κατὰ διάνοιαν, τὸν πρὸς Θεὸν ἀληθῶς συναγόμενον.

μαʹ. Τοῦ μὲν γραπτοῦ νόμου ἔργον ἐστὶν, ἡ τῶν παθῶν ἀπαλλαγή· φυσικοῦ δὲ νόμου, ἡ κατ᾿ ἰσονομίαν πρὸς πάντας ἀνθρώπους ἰσοτιμία· πνευματικοῦ δὲ νόμου τελείωσις, ἡ πρὸς τὸν Θεὸν, ὡς ἔστιν ἀνθρώπῳ δυνατὸν ἐξομοίωσις.

μβʹ. Τῆς μὲν τῶν σωμάτων καὶ ἀσωμάτων γνώσεως, δεκτικὴ κατὰ φύσιν ἐστὶν ἡ τοῦ νοῦ δύναμις· τῆς δὲ τῆς ἁγίας Τριάδος, κατὰ μόνην τὴν χάριν δέχεται τὰς ἐμφάσεις· ὅτι ἐστὶ μόνον πιστεύουσα, ἀλλ᾿ οὐ τί ποτε κατ᾿ οὐσίαν ἐστὶν αὐθαδιζομένη, ὡς ὁ δαιμόνιος νοῦς. Ὁ γὰρ γνώσεως ἄμοιρος, τὸν κατ᾿ ἀρετὴν ῥυπτικὸν τῆς κακίας οὐδαμῶς ἐπίσταται τρόπον.

μγʹ. Ὁ τὸ ψεῦδος ἀγαπῶν, αὐτῷ παραδίδοται πρὸς ἀπώλειαν, ἵνα γνῷ πάσχων ὅπερ ἑκὼν περιεῖπε, καὶ μάθῃ κατὰ τὴν πεῖραν γενόμενος, ὡς ἐλάνθανεν ἀντὶ ζωῆς περιπτυσσόμενος θάνατον.

μδʹ. Μόνην ἔχει τοῦ καλοῦ τὴν γνῶσιν ὁ Θεὸς, ὡς κατ᾿ οὐσίαν τοῦ καλοῦ φύσις τε καὶ γνῶσις ὑπάρχων· τοῦ δὲ κακοῦ, τὴν ἄγνοιαν, ὡς τὴν αὐτοῦ ἔχων ἀδυναμίαν· ὧν γὰρ ἔχει τὴν δύναμιν φυσικῶς, τούτων οὐσιωδῶς κέκτηται τὴν γνῶσιν.

μεʹ.

To the concepts of sensible things the evil one is wont knavishly to join the forms and shapes of sensible things, through which the passions are of a nature to be made about the surfaces of visible things, while, through the sense that lies between, the 1236crossing of the rational activity within us toward intelligible things comes to rest. Wherefore he also prevails to plunder the soul and to drag it down into the confusion of the passions.

39. The word of God is a lamp and, at the same time, likewise a light: Psal. cxviii, 105; Prov. vi, 23. both as illumining the thoughts of the faithful that are according to nature, and as burning those contrary to nature; and as dissolving the gloom of the life according to sense for those who press on through the commandments toward the life that is hoped for, and as punishing with the burning of judgement those who by inclination, through love of the flesh, cling to this dark night of life.

40. He who has not first, he says, been led up to himself through the casting-off of the passions contrary to nature will not be led up to his own cause, that is, God, through the acquisition in grace of the goods above nature. For he who is truly gathered to God must be separated in understanding from the things that have been made.

41. The work of the written law is release from the passions; of the natural law, equality of honour toward all men according to equality of right; and the perfection of the spiritual law, assimilation to God, as far as is possible for man.

42. The power of the mind is by nature receptive of the knowledge of bodies and of bodiless things; but of that of the holy Trinity it receives the reflections by grace alone, believing only that it is, but not presuming as to what it is according to essence, as the demonic mind does. For he who has no share of knowledge in no way knows the mode, according to virtue, that cleanses vice.

43. He who loves the lie is handed over to it for destruction, so that he may know by suffering what he willingly cherished, and, coming to experience, learn that unawares he was embracing death instead of life.

44. God has the knowledge of the good alone, as being according to essence both the nature and the knowledge of the good; but of evil, ignorance, as having incapacity for it; for of the things of which he naturally has the power, of these he essentially possesses the knowledge.

45.

Τὸ ἐν τῷ Λευϊτικῷ στηθύνιον, τὴν ἀμείνω 1237καὶ ὑψηλὴν θεωρίαν δηλοῖ· ὁ δὲ βραχίων, τὴν πρᾶ-Ἁ ξιν, ἤγουν τοῦ διανοητικοῦ τὴν ἕξιν καὶ τὴν ἐνέργειαν, ἢ τὴν γνῶσιν καὶ τὴν ἀρετήν· ὡς τῆς μὲν γνώσεως, αὐτῷ προσαγούσης ἀμέσως τὸν νοῦν τῷ Θεῷ· τῆς ἀρετῆς δὲ, κατὰ τὴν πρᾶξιν πάσης ἀφαιρουμένης αὐτὸν τῆς τῶν ὄντων γενέσεως· ἅπερ ἱερεῦσιν ἀφώρισεν ὁ λόγος, τοῖς μόνον τὸν Θεὸν διὰ πάντων κτησαμένοις κληρονομίαν, καὶ μηδὲν τοσύνολον κεκτημένοις ἐπίγειον.

μϛʹ. Ἐπειδὴ τὰς ἄλλων καρδίας διὰ τοῦ λόγου τῆς διδασκαλίας, οἱ γνώσει καὶ ἀρετῇ δι᾿ ὅλου ποιωθέντες τῷ πνεύματι, εὐσεβείας καὶ πίστεως ποιοῦνται δεκτικὰς, καὶ τὴν πρακτικὴν αὐτῶν ἕξιν καὶ δύναμιν ἀφαιρούμενοι, τῶν ἐπὶ τῇ φθαρτῇ φύσει σπουδασμάτων, πρὸς τὴν τῶν ὑπὲρ φύσιν ἀφθάρτων ἐνἐργειαν ἀγαθῶν μεταφέρουσιν· εἰκότως τῶν προσαγομένων εἰς θυσίαν Θεοῦ, τὸ στηθύνιον τοῦ ἐπιθέματος· τουτέστι, τῶν προσαγομένων τὴν καρδίαν· καὶ τὸν βραχίονα, τῶν αὐτῶν δηλονότι τὴν πρᾶξιν· προσέταξεν ἀφιεροῦσθαι τοῖς ἱερεῦσιν ὁ Λόγος.

μζʹ. Πᾶσα ἡ ἐνταῦθα δικαιοσύνη συγκρινομένη πρὸς τὴν μέλλουσαν, ἐσόπτρου λόγον ἐπέχει, τὴν τῶν ἀρχετύπων πραγμάτων εἰκόνα, οὐκ αὐτὰ δὲ τὰ πράγματα κατ᾿ εἶδος ὑφιστάμενα ἔχουσα· καὶ πᾶσα γνῶσις ἐνταῦθα τῶν ὑψηλῶν συγκρινομένη πρὸς τὴν μέλλουσαν, αἴνιγμά ἐστιν, ἔμφασιν τῆς ἀληθείας, ἀλλ᾿ οὐκ αὐτὴν ὑφισταμένην ἔχουσα τὴν φανήσεσθαι μέλλουσαν ἀλήθειαν.

μηʹ. Ἐπειδὴ ἀρετῇ καὶ γνώσει τὰ θεῖα συνέχεται, τῶν κατ᾿ ἀρετὴν πρωτοτύπων ἐστὶν ἐνδεικτικὸν τὸ ἔσοπτρον, καὶ τῶν κατὰ γνῶσιν ἀρχετύπων ἐκφαντικὸν ὑπάρχει τὸ αἴνιγμα.

μθʹ. Ὁ διὰ πράξεως εὐαρεστήσας τῷ Θεῷ, διὰ θεωρίας πρὸς τὴν τῶν νοητῶν χώραν, τὸν νοῦν δηλαδὴ μετατίθησιν· ἵνα μὴ διὰ φαντασίας τινὸς τὸν ἐν τοῖς πάθεσι θεάσηται θάνατον κατὰ τὴν αἴσθησιν, ὡς ὑπὸ μηδενὸς τῶν ἑλεῖν βουλομένων παντελῶς εὑρισκόμενος.

νʹ.

The breast in Leviticus signifies the better 1237and lofty contemplation; and the arm, practice, that is, the habit and activity of the discursive part, or knowledge and virtue: since knowledge brings the mind immediately to God himself, and virtue, according to practice, withdraws it from all the coming-to-be of beings; which things the word set apart for the priests, who through all things have acquired God alone as an inheritance and possess nothing whatever earthly.

46. Since those who are wholly qualified in the spirit by knowledge and virtue make the hearts of others, through the word of teaching, receptive of piety and faith, and, withdrawing their practical habit and power from the pursuits that concern the corruptible nature, transfer them to the activity of the incorruptible goods above nature: fittingly the Word commanded that, of the things brought for a sacrifice to God, the breast of the wave-offering — that is, the heart of those who are brought — and the arm — that is, clearly, their practice — be consecrated to the priests.

47. All justice here, compared with that to come, holds the role of a mirror, having the image of the archetypal realities, but not the realities themselves subsisting in form; and all knowledge here of lofty things, compared with that to come, is an enigma, having a reflection of the truth, but not having, subsisting, the very truth that is to be made manifest.

48. Since divine things are held together by virtue and knowledge, the mirror is indicative of the prototypes according to virtue, and the enigma is manifestive of the archetypes according to knowledge.

49. He who has pleased God through practice clearly transfers his mind through contemplation to the region of intelligible things, Gen. v, 24; Hebr. xi, 5. so that through some imagination he may not behold, according to sense, the death that is in the passions, since he is not found at all by any of those who wish to seize him.

50.

Ὁ καθαρῷ πίστεως ὀφθαλμῷ τῶν μελλόντων ἀγαθῶν ἰδὼν τὴν εὐπρέπειαν, ὑπακούει γῆς καὶ συγγενείας καὶ οἴκου πατρικοῦ προθύμως ἐξελθεῖν, καταλιπὼν τὴν πρὸς σάρκα καὶ αἴσθησιν καὶ αἰσθητὰ σχέσιν τε καὶ προσπάθειαν· καὶ φύσεως ἐν καιρῷ πειρασμοῦ καὶ ἀγώνων ὑψηλότερος ὢν, προτιμήσας

1240τὴν αἰτίαν τῆς φύσεως, ὡς τοῦ Ἰσαακ, τὸν Θεὸν, ὁ μέγας Ἀβραάμ.

ναʹ. Ὁ μήτε δόξης ἕνεκεν, μήτε προφάσει πλεονεξίας, μήτε κολακείας χάριν, καὶ ἀνθρωπαρεσκίας καὶ ἐπιδείξεως, τὴν ἀρετὴν, ἢ τὴν μελέτην τῶν θείων λόγων μετελθεῖν ἐπιτηδεύων, ἀλλὰ πάντα διὰ τὸν Θεὸν καὶ ποιῶν καὶ λέγων καὶ διανοούμενος, οὗτος ἐν τῇ ὁδῷ τῆς ἀληθείας ἐν γνώσει πορεύεται. Τρίβοις γὰρ οὐκ εὐθείαις ὁ θεῖος οὐ πέφυκεν ἐμφιλοχωρεῖν λόγος, κἂν εὕρῃ τὴν ὁδὸν ἕν τισιν ἕτοιμον.

νβʹ. Νηστεύει τις, καὶ ἀπέχεται τῆς ἐξαπτικῆς τῶν παθῶν διαίτης· τὰ ἄλλα τε ποιεῖ, ὅσα πρὸς ἀπαλλαγὴν κακίας συμβάλλεσθαι δύναται· τὴν λεγομένην ὁδὸν οὗτος ἡτοίμασε· κενοδοξίας δὲ, πλεονεξίας, ἢ κολακείας, ἢ ἀνθρωπαρεσκίας, ἢ ἄλλης τινὸς ἕνεκεν αἰτίας, δίχα τῆς θείας εὐαρεστήσεως τοὺς τοιούτους ἐπιτηδεύει τρόπους, οὗτος οὐκ ἐποίησεν εὐθείας τὰς τρίβους τοῦ Θεοῦ· καὶ τὸν μὲν τῆς ἑτοιμασίας τῆς ὁδοῦ πόνον ὑπέμεινεν, τὸν δὲ Θεὸν οὐκ ἔσχε ταῖς αὐτοῦ τρίβοις ἐμπεριπατοῦντα.

νγʹ. Πᾶσα φάραγξ πληρωθήσεται· οὐχ ἀπλῶς πᾶσα, οὐδὲ πάντων· οὔτε γὰρ τῶν μὴ ἑτοιμασάντων τὴν ὁδὸν τοῦ Κυρίου, καὶ εὐθείας ποιησάντων τὰς τρίβους αὐτοῦ. Ὁπηνίκα οὖν φάραγξ, ἤγουν σὰρξ ἢ ψυχὴ, τῶν ἑτοιμασάντων, ὡς εἶπον, τὴν ὁδὸν τοῦ Κυρίου, καὶ εὐθείας ποιησάντων τὰς τρίβους αὐτοῦ, διὰ τῆς τοῦ περιπατοῦντος ἐν αὐτοῖς διὰ τῶν ἐντολῶν Θεοῦ Λόγου παρουσίας, πληρωθῇ γνώσεώς τε καὶ ἀρετῆς, πάντα τὰ τῆς ψευδωνύμου γνώσεως καὶ κακίας τὰ πνεύματα ταπεινοῦται, πατοῦντος αὐτὰ τοῦ Λόγου καὶ ὑποτάττοντος, καὶ τὸ ἐπαιρόμενον κατὰ τῆς ἀνθρωπίνης φύσεως, πονηρὸν κράτος καταβάλλοντος, καὶ οἷον τὸ μέγεθος καὶ τὸ ὕψος τῶν ὀρέων καὶ τῶν βουνῶν κατασκάπτοντος· καὶ εἰς τὴν τῶν φαράγγων ἄγοντος πλήρωσιν. Ἡ γὰρ τῶν παρὰ φύσιν ἀποβολὴ παθῶν, καὶ ἡ τῶν κατὰ φύσιν ἀρετῶν ἐπιβολὴ, τὴν φαραγγωθεῖσαν ψυχὴν ἀναπληροῖ, καὶ τὴν ὀρινομένην τῶν πονηρῶν πνευμάτων ταπεινοῖ δυναστείαν.

νδʹ. Αἱ τραχεῖαι (τουτέστιν, αἱ τῶν ἀκουσίων πειρασμῶν ἐπιφοραὶ) ἔσονται εἰς ὁδοὺς λείας, ὅταν μάλιστα χαίρων καὶ εὐφραινόμενος ὁ νοῦς, ἐν ἀσθενείαις εὐδοκῇ καὶ θλίψεσι καὶ ἀνάγκαις, διὰ τῶν ἀκουσίων πόνων, τὴν ὅλην τῶν ἑκουσίων παθῶν ἀφαιρούμενος δυναστείαν. Τραχείας γὰρ κέκληκε, τὰς τῶν ἀκουσίων πειρασμῶν συμβάσεις, εἰς ὁδοὺς λείας διὰ τῆς κατὰ τὴν εὐχαριστίαν ὑπομονῆς μεταπιπτούσας.

νεʹ.

He who, with the pure eye of faith, has seen the comeliness of the good things to come obeys readily to go out from land and kindred and father's house, Gen. xii, 1. leaving behind the relation and the passionate attachment to flesh and sense and sensible things; and, being higher than nature in the time of temptation and of contests, preferring

1240the cause of nature, as the great Abraham preferred God to Isaac. Gen. xxii, 1 seqq.

51. He who makes it his practice to pursue virtue, or the study of the divine words, neither for the sake of glory, nor on the pretext of greed, nor for the sake of flattery, and of man-pleasing and display, but who both does and says and thinks all things for God's sake — this man walks in the way of truth in knowledge. For the divine word is not of a nature to linger in paths not straight, Isa. xl, 3. even if it finds the way ready in some.

52. One fasts, and abstains from the diet that kindles the passions, and does the other things that can contribute to release from vice: this man has prepared the way spoken of; ibid. 4; Luc. iii, 5. but for vainglory, greed, or flattery, or man-pleasing, or for the sake of some other cause, apart from the divine good pleasure, he practises such manners: this man has not made the paths of God straight; and he endured the toil of preparing the way, but did not have God walking about in his paths.

53. Every valley shall be filled: ibid. not simply every one, nor of all; for not of those who have not prepared the way of the Lord and made his paths straight. When, then, the valley — that is, the flesh or soul — of those who, as I said, have prepared the way of the Lord and made his paths straight is filled with knowledge and virtue, through the presence of God the Word who walks about in them through the commandments, all the spirits of the knowledge falsely so called and of vice are brought low, the Word treading them down and subjecting them, and casting down the evil might that exalts itself against human nature, and as it were digging down the greatness and the height of the mountains and the hills, and bringing them to the filling of the valleys. For the casting-off of the passions contrary to nature, and the putting-on of the virtues according to nature, fills up the soul that has been made a valley and brings low the dominion of the evil spirits that has been raised up into a mountain.

54. The rough ways (that is, the onsets of involuntary temptations) shall become smooth ways, especially when the mind, rejoicing and glad, takes pleasure in weaknesses and afflictions and necessities, removing through involuntary toils the whole dominion of the voluntary passions. For it has called rough ways the occurrences of involuntary temptations, which change into smooth ways through endurance with thanksgiving.

55.

Ὁ τῆς ἀληθινῆς ἐφιέμενος ζωῆς, γνοὺς ὅτι πᾶς πόνος, εἴτε ἑκούσιος, εἴτε ἀκούσιος, τῆς τοῦ θανάτου μητρὸς ἡδονῆς [Regii duo ὀδύνης] γίνεται θάνατος, πάσας τὰς τραχείας τῶν ἀκουσίων πειρασμῶν 1241ἐπιφορὰς, δέξεται μετ᾿ εὐφροσύνης χαίρων· διὰ τῆς ὑπομονῆς, ὁδοὺς εὐμαρεῖς τε καὶ λείας, τὰς θλίψεις ποιούμενος, καὶ πρὸς τὸ βραβεῖον τῆς ἄνω κλήσεως ἀπλανῶς παραπεμπούσας αὐτὸν, εὐσεβῶς ἐν αὐταῖς τὸν θεῖον δρόμον ποιούμενον. Καὶ γὰρ, τοῦ μὲν θανάτου μήτηρ ἐστὶν ἡ ἡδονή· τῆς ἡδονῆς δὲ θάνατός ἐστιν, ὁ πόνος· ὅ τε προαιρετικὸς, καὶ ὁ παρὰ προαίρεσιν.

νϛʹ. Πᾶς τοιγαροῦν ὁ τὴν πολυέλικτόν τε καὶ πολύπλοκον ἡδονὴν, καὶ πᾶσιν ὁμοῦ τοῖς αἰσθητοῖς πολυτρόπως συμπεπλεγμένην τῇ ἐγκρατείᾳ διαλύσας, τὰ σκολιὰ εἰς εὐθείας ἐποίησε· καὶ ὁ τὴν δύσβατον καὶ τραχεῖαν τῶν πόνων ἐπιφορὰν δι᾿ ὑπομονῆς πατήσας, τὰς τραχείας ἐποίησεν εἰς ὁδοὺς λείας. Ὅθεν ὥσπερ ἔπαθλον ἀρετῆς καὶ τῶν ὑπὲρ αὐτῆς καμάτων, οἷα καλῶς τε καὶ νομίμως ἀθλήσας, καὶ τὴν ἡδονὴν νικήσας τῷ πόθῳ τῆς ἀρετῆς, καὶ τὴν ὀδύνην πατήσας τῷ τῆς γνώσεως ἔρωτι, καὶ δι᾿ ἀμφοτέρων γενναίως τοὺς θείους διενέγκας ἀγῶνας, Ὄψεται, κατὰ τὸ γεγραμμένον, τὸ σωτήριον τοῦ Θεοῦ.

νζʹ. Ὁ τὴν ἀρετὴν ἀγαπῶν, τὴν τῶν ἡδονῶν ἑκουσίως ἀπομαραίνει κάμινον· ὁ δὲ τῇ γνώσει τῆς ἀληθείας πεποιωμένος τὸν νοῦν, ἀκουσίοις οὐκ ἐπέχεται πόνοις, τῆς κατὰ τὴν ἔφεσιν πρὸς τὸν Θεὸν φερούσης ἀεικινησίας.

νηʹ. Ὁ τὰ σκολιὰ τῶν ἑκουσίων παθῶν, ἤγουν τῆς ἡδονῆς τὰ κινήματα διὰ τῆς ἐγκρατείας εὐθύνας, καὶ τὰς τραχείας, τῶν ἀκουσίων πειρασμῶν συμφορὰς, ἤγουν τοὺς τρόπους τῆς ὀδύνης διὰ τῆς ὑπομονῆς ὁμαλίσας, καὶ εἰς ὁδοὺς λείας καταστήσας· ὁ τοιοῦτος εἰκότως ὄψεται τὸ σωτήριον τοῦ Θεοῦ, καθαρὸς τῇ καρδίᾳ γενόμενος· καθ᾿ ἣν διὰ τῶν ἀρετῶν καὶ τῶν εὐσεβῶν θεωρημάτων, ὁρᾷ τὸν Θεὸν ἐπὶ τέλει τῶν ἄθλων, κατὰ τὸ, Μακάριοι οἱ καθαροὶ τῇ καρδίᾳ, ὅτι αὐτοὶ τὸν Θεὸν ὄψονται· τῶν ὑπὲρ ἀρετῆς πόνων, τῆς ἀπαθείας τὴν χάριν ἀντιλαβὼν, ἧς οὐδὲν πλέον τὸν Θεὸν ἐμφανίζει τοῖς ἔχουσι.

νθʹ.

He who aspires to true life, knowing that every toil, whether voluntary or involuntary, becomes the death of pleasure, the mother of death, will receive with gladness, rejoicing, all the rough 1241onsets of involuntary temptations; making the afflictions, through endurance, easy and smooth ways, which escort him unerringly to the prize of the high calling Philipp. iii, 14. as he piously runs the divine course in them. For indeed the mother of death is pleasure; and the death of pleasure is toil, both the elective and that which is against choice.

56. Everyone, therefore, who by self-control has untied the many-coiled and many-twisted pleasure, interwoven in many ways with all sensible things together, has made the crooked straight; and he who through endurance has trodden down the hard-to-pass and rough onset of toils has made the rough ways smooth. Hence, as a prize of virtue and of the labours undertaken for it, as one who has contended well and lawfully, and conquered pleasure by longing for virtue, and trodden down pain by yearning for knowledge, and through both nobly carried through the divine contests, he shall see, as it is written, the salvation of God. Isa. xl, 5.

57. He who loves virtue willingly makes the furnace of pleasures wither; and he who is qualified in mind by the knowledge of the truth is not held back by involuntary toils, since the ever-moving according to aspiration bears him toward God.

58. He who through self-control has straightened the crooked things of the voluntary passions, that is, the movements of pleasure, and through endurance has levelled the rough things, the misfortunes of involuntary temptations, that is, the modes of pain, and has set them as smooth ways — such a man shall fittingly see the salvation of God, having become pure in heart; in which, through the virtues and pious objects of contemplation, he sees God at the end of the contests, according to Blessed are the pure in heart, for they shall see God, Ibid. 9. having received in return for the toils for virtue the grace of passionlessness, than which nothing more manifests God to those who have it.

59.

Λάκκους καλεῖ ἡ Γραφὴ , τὰς δεκτικὰς τῶν οὐρανίων χαρισμάτων τῆς ἁγίας γνώσεως καρδίας, λατομουμένας τῷ στεῤῥῷ λόγῳ τῶν ἐντολῶν, καὶ ἀποβαλλομένας καθάπερ πωρώματα, τήν τε πρὸς τὰ πάθη φιληδονίαν, καὶ τὴν πρὸς τὰ αἰσθητὰ τῆς φύσεως σχέσιν, καὶ πληρουμένας τῆς ἄνωθεν φερομένης ῥυπτικῆς τε τῶν παθῶν, καὶ ζωοποιητικῆς καὶ οἷον θρεπτικῆς τῶν ἀρετῶν ἐν πνεύματι, γνώσεως.

1244ξʹ.Λάκκους λατομεῖ ὁ Κύριος ἐν τῇ ἐρήμῳ· λέγω δὲ τῷ κόσμῳ, καὶ τῇ φύσει τῶν ἀνθρώπων· τὰν τῶν ἀξίων καρδίας ἐκχοΐζων, καὶ ἀποκαθαίρων τοῦ ὑλικοῦ βάρους τε καὶ φρονήματος, καὶ ποιῶν εὔρυχώρους πρὸς ὑποδοχὴν τῶν θείων τῆς σοφίας καὶ τῆς γνώσεως ὑετῶν, ἵνα ποτίζωσι τὰ κτήνη τοῦ Χριστοῦ, τοὺς δεομένους λέγω διὰ ψυχῆς νηπιότητα, τῆς ἠθικῆς διδασκαλίας.

ξαʹ. Ὀρεινὴν ἡ Γραφὴ, τὴν ὑψηλὴν ἐν πνεύματι θεωρίαν τῆς φύσεως λέγει, ἣν γεωργοῦσιν οἱ τῶν αἰσθητῶν ἀπογενόμενοι φαντασιῶν, καὶ πρὸς τοὺς αὐτῶν νοητοὺς διὰ μέσων τῶν ἀρετῶν διαβάντες λόγους

ξβʹ. Ὁ νοῦς ἐφ᾿ ὅσον ἔχει ζῶσαν ἐν ἑαυτῷ τὴν τοῦ Θεοῦ μνήμην, διὰ τῆς θεωρίας ἐκζητεῖ τὸν Κύριον· καὶ οὐχ ἁπλῶς, ἀλλ᾿ ἐν φόβῳ Κυρίου· τουτέστιν, ἐν τῇ πράξει τῶν ἐντολῶν. Ὁ γὰρ ἐκζητῶν διὰ θεωρίας τὸν Κύριον χωρὶς πράξεως, οὐχ εὑρίσκει αὐτὸν, ὅτι οὐκ ἐν φόβῳ Κυρίου τὸν Κύριον ἐξεζήτησε. Καὶ εὐώδωσεν αὐτῷ ὁ Κύριος· παντὶ γὰρ τῷ μετὰ γνώσεως πράττοντι, εὐοδοῖ ὁ Κύριος, τούς τε τῶν ἐντολῶν διδάσκων τρόπους, καὶ τοὺς τῶν ὄντων ἀληθεῖς λόγους ἀποκαλύπτων.

ξγʹ. Ὁ περὶ θεότητος ὑψηλὸς λόγος, πύργος ἐστὶ κατὰ ψυχὴν, ταῖς τῶν ἐντολῶν ἐνεργείαις ὠχυρωμένος. Καὶ τοῦτό ἐστιν ὅ φησιν, ὅτι ᾨκοδόμησεν Ὁζίας πύργους ἐν Ἰερουσαλήμ. Ὁ γὰρ καλῶς εὐοδούμενος ἐπὶ τὴν διὰ θεωρίας ἐκζήτησιν τοῦ Κυρίου, μετὰ τοῦ ἐπιβεβλημένου φόβου, τουτέστι τῆς τῶν ἐντολῶν πράξεως, οἰκοδομεῖ πύργους ἐν Ἰερουσαλὴμ, κατὰ τὴν ἁπλῆν δηλαδὴ καὶ ἠρεμαίαν τῆς ψυχῆς κατάστασιν, τοὺς περὶ θεότητος ἀνυψῶν λόγους.

ξδʹ. Οἱ τῶν ἐπὶ μέρους λόγοι, τοῖς καθόλου προσχωροῦντες, τὰς τῶν διῃρημένων ἑνώσεις ποιοῦνται· διότι τῶν μερικωτέρων ἑνοειδῶς οἱ καθολικώτεραι τοὺς λόγους περιλαμβάνουσι, πρὸς οὓς φυσικῶς τὴν ἀναφορὰν ἔχει τὰ κατὰ μέρος. Ἀλλὰ καὶ νοῦ πρὸς αἴσθησιν, καὶ οὐρανοῦ πρὸς γῆν, καὶ αἰσθητῶν πρὸς νοητὰ, καὶ φύσεως πρὸς λόγον, ἔστι τις καὶ τούτων σχετικὸς ἐν πνεύματι λόγος, τὴν πρὸς ἄλληλα διδοὺς αὐτοῖς ἕνωσιν.

ξεʹ. Ὁ τῶν παθῶν δυνηθεὶς ἐξηλῶσαι τὰς αἰσθήσεις, καὶ τῆς τῶν αἰσθήσεων σχέσεως τὴν ψυχὴν ἀποδιαστείλας, κατίσχυσεν ἀποτειχίσαι τὴν γενομένην τοῦ διαβόλου πρὸς τὸν νοῦν διὰ μέσων τῶν αἰσθήσεων εἴσοδον· καὶ διὰ τοῦτο κατὰ τὴν ἔρημον, φημὶ δὲ τὴν φυσικὴν θεωρίαν, οἱονεὶ πύργους ἀσφαλεῖς, τὰς εὐσεβεῖς περὶ τῶν ὄντων ᾠκοδόμησε δόξας· ἐν αἷς ὁ καταφεύγων, οὐ φοβεῖται τοὺς κατὰ τὴν ἔρημον ταύτην· λέγω δὲ τὴν φύσιν τῶν ὁρωμένων, λῃστεύοντας δαίμονας, καὶ πλανῶντας τὸν νοῦν διὰ τῆς αἰσθήσεως, καὶ πρὸς ἀγνοίας κατασύροντας ζόφον.

Scripture calls cisterns the hearts receptive of the heavenly gifts of grace of holy knowledge, which are hewn out by the firm word of the commandments, and cast off, like callosities, both the love of pleasure toward the passions and the relation of nature to sensible things, and are filled with the knowledge borne from above, that cleanses the passions and gives life to, and as it were nourishes, the virtues in the spirit.

124460. The Lord hews cisterns in the desert — I mean in the world and in the nature of men — digging out the hearts of the worthy, and cleansing them of the material weight and way of thinking, and making them spacious for the reception of the divine rains of wisdom and of knowledge, so that they may water the cattle of Christ — I mean those who, through infancy of soul, need moral teaching.

61. Scripture calls hill country the lofty contemplation of nature in the spirit, which those cultivate who have died away from the imaginations of sensible things and have crossed over, through the virtues in between, to their intelligible principles.

62. The mind, so long as it has the memory of God living within itself, seeks out the Lord through contemplation; and not simply, but in the fear of the Lord, that is, in the practice of the commandments. For he who seeks out the Lord through contemplation without practice does not find him, because he did not seek out the Lord in the fear of the Lord. And the Lord prospered him; for the Lord prospers everyone who acts with knowledge, both teaching the modes of the commandments and revealing the true principles of beings.

63. The lofty word concerning Godhead is a tower in the soul, fortified by the activities of the commandments. And this is what it says: Uzziah built towers in Jerusalem. For he who is well prospered in the seeking out of the Lord through contemplation, with the fear that befits it — that is, the practice of the commandments — builds towers in Jerusalem, that is, in the simple and quiescent state of the soul, raising high the words concerning Godhead.

64. The principles of particular things, coming over to the universal, make the unions of the things divided; because the more universal comprehend uniformly the principles of the more particular, to which the particulars naturally have their reference. But also of mind to sense, and of heaven to earth, and of sensibles to intelligibles, and of nature to principle — of these too there is a certain relational principle in the spirit, giving them union with one another.

65. He who has been able to unnail the senses from the passions, and has separated the soul from the relation of the senses, has prevailed to wall off the entry that the devil had made toward the mind through the senses that lie between; and for this reason in the desert — I mean natural contemplation — he has built, as it were secure towers, the pious opinions concerning beings, in which he who takes refuge does not fear the demons who rob in this desert — I mean the nature of visible things — and lead the mind astray through sense and drag it down to the gloom of ignorance.

Ὁ γὰρ τὴν εὐσεβῆ περὶ ἕκαστον δόξαν κτησάμενος, οὐ δέδοικε τοὺς διὰ τῶν φαινομένων πλανῶντας τοὺς ἀνθρώπους δαίμονας.

1245ξϛʹ. Πᾶς νοῦς ἰσχὺν πρὸς θεωρίαν ἔχων, ἀληθὴς ἐστι γεωργὸς, καθαρὰ ζιζανίων διὰ τῆς οἰκείας σπουδῆς καὶ ἐπιμελείας τὰ θεῖα τῶν ἀγαθῶν διαφυλάττων σπέρματα, μέχρις οὗ συντηροῦσαν αὐτὸν ἔχῃ τὴν τοῦ Θεοῦ μνήμην. Φησὶ γάρ· Καὶ ἦν ἐκζητῶν τὸν Κύριον ἐν ταῖς ἡμέραις Ζαχαρίου ἐν φόβῳ Κυρίου. Ζαχαρίαν δὲ, μνήμην Θεοῦ πρὸς τὴν Ἑλλάδα φωνὴν μεταφερόμενον οἶδεν ὁ λόγος. Διὸ πάντοτε δεηθῶμεν τοῦ Κυρίου, τὴν σωτήριον αὐτοῦ φυλαχθῆναι ἡμῖν μνήμην, ἵνα μὴ καὶ αὐτὸ διαφθείρῃ τὴν ψυχὴν τὸ κατορθούμενον, πρὸς ὕψος ἀρθεῖσαν, καὶ τῶν ὑπὲρ φύσιν, ὡς ὁ Ὀζίας, κατατολμήσασαν.

ξζʹ. Ἡ τῶν ὄντων ἀπλανὴς θεωρία, παθῶν ἀπηλλαγμένης δεῖται ψυχῆς· ἥτις Ἰερουσαλὴμ λέγεται, διά τε τὴν ἄρτιον ἀρετὴν καὶ τὴν ἄϋλον γνῶσιν· ἥτις οὐ μόνον κατὰ στέρησιν παθῶν, ἀλλὰ καὶ φαντασιῶν αἰσθητῶν ἐπιγίνεται.

ξηʹ. Χωρὶς πίστεως καὶ ἐλπίδος καὶ ἀγάπης, οὐδὲν οὔτε τῶν κακῶν καταργεῖται παντελῶς, οὔτε τῶν καλῶν κατορθοῦται τοσύνολον. Ἡ μὲν γὰρ πίστις, πείθει τῷ Θεῷ προσχωρεῖν τὸν νοῦν πολεμούμενον, πάσης αὐτῷ γινομένη παρασκευῆς ὅπλων πνευματικῶν πρὸς τὸ θαῤῥεῖν παραμύθιον. Ἡ δὲ ἐλπὶς, τῆς θείας αὐτῷ καθίσταται βοηθείας ἐγγυητὴς ἀψευδέστατος, τὴν τῶν ἐναντίων καθαίρεσιν ἐπαγγελλομένη δυνάμεων. Ἡ δὲ ἀγάπη, δυσμετακίνητον, μᾶλλον δὲ, πάμπαν ἀκίνητον, τῆς θείας στοργῆς αὐτὸν εἶναι παρασκευάζει καὶ πολεμούμενον, προσηλοῦσα τῷ θείῳ πόθῳ πᾶσαν αὐτοῦ τὴν τῆς ἐφέσεως δύναμιν.

ξθʹ. Ἡ πίστις παραμυθεῖται τὸν νοῦν πολεμούμενον, βοηθείας ἐλπίδι ῥωννύμενον· ἡ δὲ ἐλπὶς ὑπ᾿ ὄψιν ἄγουσα τὴν πιστευθεῖσαν βοήθειαν, ἀποκρούεται τὴν τῶν ἀντικειμένων καταδρομήν· ἡ δὲ ἀγάπη, νεκρὰν καθίστησι τῷ φιλοθέῳ νῷ τῶν πολεμίων τὴν προσβολὴν, τῇ πρὸς Θεὸν ἐφέσει παντελῶς ἀμαυρουμένην.

οʹ. Θείας ὄντως μερίδος, ἤγουν γνώσεως ἀληθοῦς, υἱός, πρῶτός τε καὶ μόνος, ὁ κατὰ τὴν πίστιν τῆς ἐν ἡμῖν θείας ἀναστάσεώς ἐστι λόγος, μετὰ τῆς δεούσης κατὰ τὴν γνώμην οἰκονομίας, ἤγουν διακρίσεως, καλῶς διαφέρων τάς τε τῶν ἑκουσίων καὶ ἀκουσίων πειρασμῶν ἐπαναστάσεις. Καὶ γὰρ πρώτη ἐν ἡμῖν ἀνάστασις τοῦ διὰ τῆς ἀγνοίας ἡμῖν νεκρωθέντος Θεοῦ, ἡ πίστις ἐστὶ, καλῶς τοῖς ἔργοις τῶν ἐντολῶν οἰκονομουμένη.

οαʹ.

For he who has acquired the pious opinion concerning each thing does not dread the demons who lead men astray through the things that appear.

124566. Every mind that has strength for contemplation is a true husbandman, keeping by its own zeal and care the divine seeds of the good things pure of tares, so long as it has the memory of God preserving it. For it says: And he was seeking out the Lord in the days of Zechariah, in the fear of the Lord. And the word knows that Zechariah, translated into the Greek tongue, is "memory of God". Therefore let us always beseech the Lord that his saving memory be guarded for us, lest the very thing that is rightly achieved destroy the soul, lifted up to a height and having dared things above nature, like Uzziah.

67. Unerring contemplation of beings needs a soul released from passions, which is called Jerusalem because of its perfect virtue and its immaterial knowledge; which comes about not only through privation of passions but also of sensible imaginations.

68. Without faith and hope and love, nothing either of evil things is wholly abolished, or of good things achieved at all. For faith persuades the mind under attack to come over to God, becoming for it an encouragement toward confidence consisting in every equipment of spiritual arms. And hope becomes for it a most unlying guarantor of divine help, promising the overthrow of the contrary powers. And love makes it hard to move, or rather wholly unmoved, from divine affection even when under attack, nailing to the divine longing the whole power of its aspiration.

69. Faith encourages the mind under attack, strengthened by the hope of help; hope, bringing before its sight the help that was believed, beats back the onrush of the adversaries; and love makes dead, for the God-loving mind, the assault of the enemies, wholly dimmed by the aspiration toward God.

70. Of the truly divine portion, that is, of true knowledge, the son, first and only, is the word, according to faith, of the divine resurrection within us, with the due economy according to inclination, that is, discernment, nobly bearing the uprisings of both voluntary and involuntary temptations. For the first resurrection within us of God, who has been put to death in us through ignorance, is faith, well ordered by the works of the commandments.

71.

Ἡ πρὸς Θεὸν ἐπιστροφὴ, σαφῶς μηνύει δι᾿ ἑαυτῆς τὸν πληρέστατον τῆς θείας ἐλπίδος λόγον, οὗ χωρὶς, οὐδαμῶς οὐδενὶ καθ᾿ ὁτιοῦν πρὸς Θεὸν ἐπίνευσις γίνεσθαι πέφυκεν· εἴπερ ἐλπίδος ἴδιον, τὸ ὑπ᾿ ὄψιν ἄγειν ὡς παρόντα τὰ μέλλοντα· καὶ μηδαμῶς 1248ἀπεῖναι πείθειν τῶν πολεμουμένων ὑπὸ τῆς ἀντικειμένης δυνάμεως, τὸν ὑπερασπίζοντα Θεόν· ὑπὲρ οὗ, καὶ δι᾿ ὃν ἁγίοις ὁ πόλεμος. Χωρὶς γάρ τινος προσδοκίας ἢ δυσχεροῦς ἢ εὐχεροῦς, ἐπιστροφὴ πρὸς τὸ καλὸν οὐδενί ποτε πέφυκε γίνεσθαι.

οβʹ. Οὐδὲν ὄντως οὕτως ὡς ἡ ἀγάπη συνάγει τοὺς ἐσκορπισμένους, καὶ μίαν αὐτοῖς δημιουργεῖ τὴν γνώμην συμπνοίᾳ κρατουμένην, ἧς χαρακτήρ, τὸ τῆς ἰσοτιμίας καθέστηκε κάλλος.
