The first word concerning the Monad, as the beginning 1345of all virtue, the prophet named head; as in: My head went down into the clefts of the mountains; and the purposes of the spirits of wickedness, the clefts of the mountains, by which our mind was swallowed through the transgression; and lowest earth, the habit that in no way perceives divine knowledge, or has any movement whatever of the life according to virtue; and abyss, the ignorance lying upon the habit of vice, on which, as on earth, the seas of vice stand; or by earth he meant the fixed habit of vice; and everlasting bars, the passionate attachments to material things that hold the worst habit together.

93. The endurance of the saints consumes the wicked power that attacks; as persuading its own ministers to pride themselves on the toils for truth, and teaching them to be enlarged in sufferings more than those who care overmuch for the life in the flesh are in comforts, making the natural weakness of the flesh as to suffering an occasion for them of power surpassing according to the Spirit. For see how the natural weakness of the saints is the occasion of divine power in excess, than which the Lord showed the proud devil weaker.

94. The word of grace, crossing through many temptations to the nature of men, that is, to the Church of the nations, as Jonah crossed through many tribulations to Nineveh the great city, persuaded the reigning law of nature to rise up from its throne (that is, from its former habit, according to sense, concerning evil), and to take off its robe (I mean, to put off the arrogance of worldly glory in matters of character), and to put on sackcloth, namely mourning, and the hard and rough conduct of ill-suffering, befitting the life according to God; and to sit upon ashes. And ashes is the poverty of spirit, upon which everyone sits who is being taught to live piously and has the scourge of conscience over his offences tormenting him.

95. See how he says that the king is the natural law; the throne, the passionate habit according to sense; the robe, the display of vainglory; the sackcloth, the mourning of repentance; the ashes, humble-mindedness; men, those about reason; cattle, those about desire; oxen, those about anger; sheep, those who stumble about the contemplation of visible things.

96. He who through the virtue of the law forgets the passions in the flesh as left things, and through infallible knowledge is not caught over his right deeds by the disease of puffing-up conceit as by a right hand, has become a man not knowing his right hand, as not yearning for glory that is dissolved, nor his left, as not provoked by the passions of the flesh.

Δεξιὰν οὖν, ὡς ἔοικεν, εἶπεν ὁ λόγος, τὴν ἐπὶ τοῖς δῆθεν κατορθώμασι κενοδοξίαν· ἀριστερὰν δὲ, τὴν ἐπὶ τοῖς αἰσχροῖς 1348πάθεσιν ἀκολασίαν. Ὁ γὰρ τῆς ἀρετῆς λόγος, τῆς σαρκὸς ὡς ἀριστερᾶς οὐκ οἶδεν ἁμαρτίαν· ὁ δὲ τῆς γνώσεως, τὴν ψυχῆς ὡς δεξιᾶς οὐ γινώσκει κακίαν.

ϟζʹ. Ἡ κατὰ λόγον γνῶσις τῶν ἀρετῶν, ἤγουν ἡ κατ᾿ ἐνέργειαν ἀληθὴς τῆς τῶν ἀρετῶν αἰτίας ἐπίγνωσις, ἄγνοιαν παντελῆ πέφυκε ποιεῖν, τῆς δεξιᾶς καὶ ἀριστερᾶς ἑκατέρωθεν παρακειμένης τῇ μεσότητι τῶν ἀρετῶν ὑπερβολῆς καὶ ἐλλείψεως. Εἰ γὰρ οὐδὲν ἐν τῷ λόγῳ παντελῶς πέφυκεν εἶναι παράλογον, σαφῶς ὁ πρὸς τὸν λόγον τῶν ἀρετῶν ἀναχθεὶς, τὴν τῶν παραλόγων οὐδαμῶς ἐπιγνώσεται θέσιν. Οὐ γὰρ δυνατὸν ἄμφω κατὰ ταὐτὸν ἅμα καταθρῆσαι τὰ ἀντικείμενα, καὶ θατέρῳ ἅμα τὸ ἕτερον γνῶναι συνεμφαινόμενον.

ϟηʹ. Εἰ ἀπιστίας ἐν τῇ πίστει λόγος οὐδεὶς, οὐδὲ σκότους αἰτία κατὰ φύσιν ὑπάρχει τὸ φῶς, οὐδὲ Χριστῷ συνενδείκνυσθαι πέφυκεν ὁ διάβολος, δῆλον ὡς οὐδὲ τῷ λόγῳ τοπαράπαν συνυπάρχει τὸ παράλογον. Εἰ δὲ τῷ λόγῳ συνεῖναι παντελῶς οὐ δύναται τὸ παράλογον, ὁ πρὸς τὸν λόγον τῶν ἀρετῶν ἀναχθεὶς, τὴν τῶν παραλόγων οὐδαμῶς ἐπιγνώσεται θέσιν, μόνην ὡς ἔστιν, ἀλλ᾿ οὐχ ὡς νομίζεται γινώσκων τὴν ἀρετήν· καὶ διὰ τοῦτο μήτε δεξιὰν διὰ τῆς ὑπερβολῆς, μήτε ἀριστερὰν διὰ τῆς ἐλλείψεως ἐπιστάμενος. Ἐπ᾿ ἀμφοῖν γὰρ θεωρεῖται σαφῶς τὸ παράλογον.

ϟθʹ. Ἀπιστίαν λέγει, τὴν ἄρνησιν τῶν ἐντολῶν· πίστιν δὲ, τὴν αὐτῶν συγκατάθεσιν· τὸ δὲ σκότος, τοῦ καλοῦ τὴν ἄγνοιαν· τὸ δὲ φῶς, τὴν τούτου διάγνωσιν. Χριστὸν δὲ κέκληκε, τὴν οὐσίαν τοῦ καλοῦ καὶ τὴν ὑπόστασιν· τὸν διάβολον δὲ, τὴν πάντων γεννητικὴν τῶν κακῶν χειρίστην ἕξιν.

ρʹ. Εἰ ὅρος ὑπάρχει καὶ μέτρον τῶν ὄντων ὁ λόγος, ἴσον πρὸς ἀλογίαν ἐστὶ, καὶ διὰ τοῦτο παράλογον, τὸ παρὰ τὸν ὅρον καὶ παρὰ τὸ μέτρον, ἢ πάλιν ὑπὲρ τὸν ὅρον κινεῖσθαι καὶ ὑπὲρ τὸ μέτρον. Ἐπίσης γὰρ ἄμφω τοῖς οὕτω κινουμένοις τοῦ κυρίως ὄντος φέρει τὴν ἕκπτωσιν· τὸ μὲν, ἄδηλον αὐτοῖς ποιεῖσθαι τοῦ δρόμου πεῖθον τὴν κίνησιν καὶ ἀόριστον, οὐκ ἔχουσαν σκοπὸν τὸν Θεὸν δι᾿ ἀμετρίαν νοὸς, ὡς τέλος αὐτοῖς προεπινοούμενον· τοῦ δεξιοῦ, δεξιώτερον ἀναπλαττομένοις· τὸ δὲ, παρὰ τὸν σκοπὸν, πρὸς μόνην τὴν αἴσθησιν αὐτοὺς πεῖθον, τοῦ δρόμου ποιεῖσθαι τὴν κίνησιν, δι᾿ ἀτονίας νοὸς, προεπινοούμενον τέλος νομίζοντας, τὸ κατ᾿ αἴσθησιν αὐτοῖς περιγραφόμενον· ἅπερ ἀγνοεῖ μὴ πάσχων. ὁ μόνῳ τῷ λόγῳ τῆς ἀρετῆς συνημμένος, καὶ πᾶσαν αὐτῷ τῆς κατὰ νοῦν οἰκείας δυνάμεως περιγράψας τὴν κίνησιν, καὶ διὰ τοῦτο μηδὲν ὑπὲρ τὸν λόγον ἢ παρὰ τὸν λόγον διανοεῖσθαι δυνάμενος.

ΕΚΑΤΟΝΤΑΣ ΠΕΜΠΤΗ.

αʹ.

The right, then, as it seems, the word called the vainglory over supposed right deeds; and the left, the licentiousness over shameful 1348passions. For the principle of virtue does not know the sin of the flesh as left; and that of knowledge does not know the vice of the soul as right.

97. Knowledge of the virtues according to reason, that is, the true full knowledge in act of the cause of the virtues, naturally produces total ignorance of the excess and the deficiency that lie beside the mean of the virtues on either side, right and left. For if nothing at all in reason is naturally contrary to reason, clearly he who has been led up to the principle of the virtues will in no way recognize the position of the things contrary to reason. For it is not possible to behold both opposites at once in the same respect, and to know the one appearing together with the other at once.

98. If there is no principle of unbelief in faith, nor is light by nature the cause of darkness, nor is the devil naturally shown together with Christ, it is clear that what is contrary to reason does not coexist at all with reason. And if what is contrary to reason cannot be with reason at all, he who has been led up to the principle of the virtues will in no way recognize the position of the things contrary to reason, knowing virtue alone, as it is and not as it is supposed; and for this reason knowing neither the right, through excess, nor the left, through deficiency. For in both, what is contrary to reason is clearly beheld.

99. He calls unbelief the denial of the commandments; and faith, assent to them; and darkness, ignorance of the good; and light, its discernment. And Christ he has called the essence and the hypostasis of the good; and the devil, the worst habit, generative of all evils.

100. If reason is the limit and measure of beings, it is equal to irrationality, and for this reason contrary to reason, to be moved beside the limit and beside the measure, or again above the limit and above the measure. For both alike bring about, for those so moved, a falling away from what truly is: the one persuading them to make the movement of the course unclear and undefined, not having God as goal, through immoderateness of mind, as the end preconceived for them, as they fashion for themselves something more right than the right; the other, beside the goal, persuading them to make the movement of the course toward sense alone, through slackness of mind, thinking the preconceived end to be what is circumscribed for them by sense. Which things he does not know, not suffering them, who is joined to the principle of virtue alone, and has circumscribed by it the whole movement of his own power according to mind, and for this reason is able to think nothing above reason or beside reason.

FIFTH CENTURY

1.

Ὁ κατὰ φύσιν λόγος, διὰ μέσων τῶν ἀρετῶν, 1349πρὸς τὸν νοῦν ἀνάγειν πέφυκε τὸν ἐπιμελούμενον πράξεως· ὁ δὲ νοῦς πρὸς τὴν σοφίαν εἰσάγει διὰ θεωρίας τὸν ἐφιέμενον γνώσεως. Τὸ δὲ παράλογον πάθος, πρὸς τὴν αἴσθησιν πείθει καταφέρεσθαι τὸν ἀμελοῦντα τῶν ἐντολῶν, ἧς τέλος ἐστὶ, τὸ πρὸς τὴν ἡδονὴν καθηλωθῆναι τὸν νοῦν.

βʹ. Ἀρετὴν καλεῖ, τὴν ἀπαθεστάτην καὶ παγίαν περὶ τὸ καλὸν ἕξιν, ἧς ἐφ᾿ ἑκάτερα καθέστηκεν οὐδὲν, Θεοῦ φερούσης χαρακτῆρα, ᾧ οὐδέν ἐστιν ἐναντίον. Αἰτία δὲ τῶν ἀρετῶν ἐστιν ὁ Θεός· τούτου δὲ κατ᾿ ἐνέργειαν γνῶσις, ἡ τοῦ κατ᾿ ἀλήθειαν ἐπεγνωκότος τὸν Θεὸν πρὸς τὸ πνεῦμα κατὰ τὴν ἕξιν ἀλλοίωσις.

γʹ. Εἰ ὁ λόγος διώρισεν ὡς πέφυκεν ἑκάστου τὴν γένεσιν, οὐδὲν τῶν ὄντων ἑαυτὸ φυσικῶς ἢ ὑπερβέβηκεν ἢ ὑποβέβηκεν. Οὐκοῦν ὅρος μὲν τῶν ὄντων ἐστὶν, ἡ κατ᾿ ἔφεσιν τῆς αἰτίας ἐπίγνωσις· μέτρον δὲ, ἡ ἐφικτὴ τοῖς οὖσι κατ᾿ ἐνέργειαν τῆς αἰτίας ἐκμίμησις. Τὸ δὲ ὑπὲρ τὸν ὅρον φέρεσθαι καὶ τὸ μέτρον τῶν κινουμένων τὴν ἔφεσιν, ἀνόνητον ποιεῖ τὸν δρόμον, μὴ καταντώντων εἰς Θεὸν, ἐν ᾧ ἡ κατ᾿ ἐ̔́φεσιν πάντων ἵσταται κίνησις, αὐθυπόστατον δεχομένη τέλος τοῦ Θεοῦ τὴν ἀπόλαυσιν. Τὸ δὲ παρὰ τὸν ὅρον φέρεσθαι καὶ τὸ μέτρον τῶν κινουμένων τὴν ἔφεσιν, ἀνόνητον ποιεῖ τὸν δρόμον, ἀντὶ Θεοῦ καταντώντων πρὸς τὴν αἴσθησιν, ἐν ᾗ καθ᾿ ἡδονὴν βέβηκεν ἡ τῶν παθῶν ἀνυπόστατος ἀπόλαυσις.

δʹ. Ὁ πρὸς τὴν αἰτίαν τῶν ὄντων ἀσχέτως ἀνατιθεὶς νοῦν, ἀγνοήσει παντελῶς, μηδένα θεωρῶν λόγον, ἐν τῷ κατὰ πᾶσαν αἰτίαν ὑπὲρ πάντα λόγον ὄντι κατ᾿ οὐσίαν Θεῷ· πρὸς ὃν ἀπὸ τῶν ὄντων ἁπάντων συσταλεὶς, οὐδένα τῶν ἀφ᾿ ὧν ἀπέστη λόγων, ἐπίσταται, μόνον τὸν πρὸς ὃν γέγονε κατὰ χάριν, ἀνερμηνεύτως θεώμενος. Καὶ γὰρ καὶ τοὺς τῶν ἀσωμάτων καὶ τοὺς τῶν σωμάτων ἀφίησι λόγους, ὁ πρὸς τὸν Θεὸν ἀναδραμὼν κατ᾿ ἔκστασιν νοῦς. Οὐ γὰρ πέφυκέ τι Θεῷ τῶν μετὰ Θεὸν, ἅμα συγκατοπτεύεσθαι.

εʹ. Ἐπάρατον ὄντως πάθος ὁ τύφος, καὶ ἐκ δύο κακῶν κατὰ σύνθεσιν συνιστάμενον, ὑπερηφανίας φημὶ καὶ κενοδοξίας· ὧν ἡ μὲν ὑπερηφανία, τὴν αἰτίαν ἀρνεῖται τῆς ἀρετῆς καὶ τῆς φύσεως· ἡ δὲ κενοδοξία, τήν τε φύσιν, καὶ αὐτὴν νόθον τὴν ἀρετὴν καθίστησιν. Οὐδὲν γὰρ τῷ ὑπερηφάνῳ κατὰ Θεὸν διαπράττεται, καὶ οὐδὲν τῷ κενοδόξῳ κατὰ φύσιν προέρχεται.

ϛʹ.

Reason according to nature, through the virtues as intermediaries, 1349naturally leads up to the mind him who cares for practice; and the mind leads into wisdom, through contemplation, him who aspires to knowledge. But passion contrary to reason persuades him who neglects the commandments to be borne down to sense, whose end is that the mind be nailed to pleasure.

2. He calls virtue the most passionless and fixed habit concerning the good, on either side of which nothing stands, since it bears the character of God, to whom nothing is contrary. And the cause of the virtues is God; and the knowledge of him in act is the alteration, by habit, toward the Spirit, of him who has truly come to know God.

3. If reason has defined the coming-to-be of each thing as it is natural to it, none of beings has naturally either overstepped itself or fallen below itself. Therefore the limit of beings is the full knowledge of the cause by aspiration; and the measure, the imitation of the cause attainable by beings in act. And for the aspiration of things moved to be borne above the limit and the measure makes the course profitless, since they do not arrive at God, in whom the movement of all things by aspiration comes to rest, receiving the enjoyment of God as self-subsistent end. And for the aspiration of things moved to be borne beside the limit and the measure makes the course profitless, since instead of God they arrive at sense, in which, by pleasure, the enjoyment of the passions, which is without hypostasis, has its standing.

4. He who sets his mind toward the cause of beings without relation will be wholly ignorant, beholding no principle in God, who in essence is beyond every principle as regards every cause; toward whom, contracted from all beings, he knows none of the principles from which he has departed, beholding inexpressibly only him toward whom he has come by grace. For the mind that has run up to God in ecstasy lets go both the principles of bodiless things and those of bodies. For none of the things after God can naturally be beheld at once together with God.

5. Arrogance is a truly accursed passion, composed by composition of two evils, pride, I mean, and vainglory; of which pride denies the cause of virtue and of nature, and vainglory renders both nature and virtue itself bastard. For nothing is done by the proud man according to God, and nothing proceeds from the vainglorious man according to nature.

6.

Ὑπερηφανίας ἴδιον, τὸ ἀρνεῖσθαι τὸν Θεὸν ἀρετῆς εἶναι γενέτην καὶ φύσεως· κενοδοξίας δὲ, τὸ μερίζειν τὴν φύσιν πρὸς ὕφεσιν· ὧν ὁ τῦφος εἶναι γέννημα πέφυκεν, ἕξις κακίας ὑπάρχων σύνθετος, Θεοῦ ἄρνησιν ἑκούσιον ἔχουσα, καὶ τῆς κατὰ φύσιν ἰσοτιμίας ἄγνοιαν.

1352ζʹ. Ὑπερηφανίας καὶ κενοδοξίας μίξις, ὁ τῦφος καθέστηκε· πρὸς μὲν τὸν Θεὸν ἔχων τὴν καταφρόνησιν, καθ᾿ ἣν πέφυκε βλασφήμως διαβάλλειν τὴν πρόνοιαν· πρὸς δὲ τὴν φύσιν κεκτημένος τὴν ἀλλοτρίωσιν, καθ᾿ ἣν πάντα τὰ τῆς φύσεως παρὰ τὴν φύσιν μεταχειρίζεται, τῷ κατὰ παράχρησιν τρόπῳ τὴν τῆς φύσεως παραφθείρων εὐπρέπειαν.

ηʹ. Τὸ πνεῦμα τοῦ καύσωνος, οὐ μόνον τοὺς πειρασμοὺς, ἀλλὰ καὶ τὴν ἐγκατάλειψιν τοῦ Θεοῦ, τὴν ἀφαιρουμένην τῶν Ἰουδαίων τὴν τῶν θείων χαρισμάτων χορηγίαν, δηλοῖ. Ἡ δὲ διὰ πνεύματος κατὰ ψυχὴν ἀγχιστεία, σαρκὸς μὲν λύει τὴν σχέσιν τῆς προαιρέσεως, Θεῷ δὲ προσδεσμεῖ κατὰ πόθον προσηλωθεῖσαν.

θʹ. Ὁ μὲν φυσικὸς νόμος, ὅταν οὐκ ἔχῃ πλεονεκτοῦσαν τὸν λόγον τὴν αἴσθησιν, ἀδιδάκτως ἀσπάζεσθαι πάντας πείθει τὸ συγγενὲς καὶ ὁμόφυλον, αὐτὴν ἔχοντας τὴν φύσιν τῆς τῶν δεομένων ἐπικουρίας διδάσκαλον, κἀκεῖνο βούλεσθαι πᾶσιν [male edita πᾶν] ἅπαντας, ὅπερ ἕκαστος ἑαυτῷ παρὰ τῶν ἄλλων γινόμενον, εἶναι δοκεῖ καταθύμιον. Καὶ τοῦτο διδάσκων ὁ Κύριος, φησὶν, Ὅσα θέλετε ἵνα ποιῶσιν ὑμῖν οἱ ἄνθρωποι, ὁμοίως καὶ ὑμεῖς ποιεῖτε αὐτοῖς.

ιʹ. Ἔργον εἶναι τοῦ φυσικοῦ νόμου φησὶ, τὴν γνωμικὴν πάντων πρὸς πάντας ὁμόθυμον σχέσιν. Ἐφ᾿ ὧν γὰρ ἡ φύσις λόγῳ βραβεύεται, τούτων μία πέφυκεν εἶναι διάθεσις. Ὧν δὲ διάθεσις ἡ αὐτὴ, καὶ ὁ κατ᾿ ἦθος τρόπος, καὶ ὁ τοῦ βίου δρόμος εἷς ὑπάρχειν προδήλως πέφυκεν. Ὧν δὲ τρόπος ἠθῶν καὶ βίου δρόμος ἐστὶν ὁ αὐτὸς, εἷς δηλονότι καὶ ὁ αὐτὸς κατὰ τὴν γνώμην τῆς πρὸς ἀλλήλους σχέσεως ὑπάρχει δεσμὸς, κατὰ μίαν γνώμην ἄγων τοὺς πάντας πρὸς τὸν ἕνα λόγον τῆς φύσεως· ἐν ᾧ παντελῶς οὐκἔστιν ἡ νῦν κρατοῦσα τῆς φύσεως διὰ τῆς φιλαυτίας διαίρεσις. Ὁ δὲ γραπτὸς νόμος, φόβῳ τῶν ἐπιτιμίων ἐπέχων τὰς ἀτάκτους τῶν ἀφρονεστέρων ὁρμὰς, ἐθίζει διδάσκων, πρὸς μόνην αὐτοὺς ὁρᾷν τὴν τοῦ ἴσου διανομὴν, καθ᾿ ἣν τῆς δικαιοσύνης τὸ κράτος χρόνῳ βεβαιωθὲν, εἰς φύσιν μεθίσταται, ποιοῦν, τὸν μὲν φόβον, διάθεσιν, ἠρέμα κατὰ μικρὸν τῇ περὶ τὸ καλὸν γνώμῃ συγκρατυνομένην· ἕξιν δὲ, τὴν συνήθειαν, τῇ λήθῃ τῶν προτέρων καθαιρομένην, καὶ τὸ φιλάλληλον ἑαυτῇ συναποτίκτουσαν.

ιαʹ. Ὁ γραπτὸς νόμος, φησὶ, κωλύων φόβῳ τὴν ἀδικίαν, ἐθίζει πρὸς τὸ δίκαιον· χρόνῳ δὲ τὸ ἔθος ποιεῖ διάθεσιν φιλοδίκαιον, ὑφ᾿ ἧς παγία πρὸς τὸ καλὸν ἕξις ἐπιγίνεται, λήθην προλαβούσης πονηρίας ἐπάγουσα.

ιβʹ.

It is proper to pride to deny that God is the begetter of virtue and of nature; and to vainglory, to partition nature toward lowering; of which arrogance is naturally the offspring, being a composite habit of vice, which has a willing denial of God and ignorance of the equality of honour according to nature.

13527. Arrogance is the mixture of pride and vainglory; toward God having contempt, according to which it is wont blasphemously to slander providence; and toward nature having acquired alienation, according to which it handles all the things of nature contrary to nature, corrupting the comeliness of nature by the mode of misuse.

8. The spirit of burning heat signifies not only the temptations, but also the abandonment by God, which takes away from the Jews the supply of the divine gifts of grace. And the near-kinship according to soul through the Spirit loosens the relation of choice to the flesh, and binds it fast to God, nailed to him by longing.

9. The natural law, when it does not have sense overreaching reason, persuades all, without teaching, to embrace what is kindred and of one race, since they have nature itself as teacher of help for those in need, and [persuades] all to wish for all that which each thinks agreeable when it is done to himself by others. And, teaching this, the Lord says: Whatever you wish that men should do to you, do you likewise to them. Matth. vii, 12.

10. He says that the work of the natural law is the relation, by inclination, of all toward all, one in mind. For those whose nature is governed by reason naturally have one disposition. And of those whose disposition is the same, both the mode in character and the course of life are naturally, and manifestly, one. And of those whose mode of character and course of life is the same, one, clearly, and the same is the bond of relation toward one another by inclination, leading all by one inclination to the one principle of nature; in which there is in no way the division of nature which now prevails through self-love. But the written law, restraining by fear of penalties the disorderly impulses of the more foolish, accustoms them by teaching to look only to the distribution of the equal, according to which the strength of justice, confirmed by time, passes over into nature, making fear a disposition, gently and little by little strengthened by the inclination toward the good, and custom a habit, purified by the forgetting of former things and bringing forth together with itself mutual love.

11. The written law, he says, preventing injustice by fear, accustoms to what is just; and in time custom makes a disposition that loves justice, from which there supervenes a fixed habit for the good, bringing on forgetfulness of the wickedness that went before.

12.

Ὁ δὲ τῆς χάριτος νόμος, αὐτὸν ἀμέσως διδάσκει τοὺς ἀγομένους μιμεῖσθαι τὸν Θεὸν, ὃς τοσοῦτον ὑπὲρ ἑαυτὸν ἡμᾶς, εἰ θέμις εἰπεῖν, καὶ ταῦτα διὰ τὴν ἁμαρτίαν ὄντας ἐχθροὺς, ἠγάπησεν, ὥστε καὶ 1353εἰς τὴν καθ᾿ ἡμᾶς οὐσίαν ἐλθεῖν ἀτρέπτως, ὑπὲρ πᾶσαν οὐσίαν ὄντα· καὶ φύσιν ὑπερουσίως καταδέξασθαι καὶ ἄνθρωπον γενέσθαι, καὶ τῶν ἀνθρώπων ἕνα χρηματίσαι βούλεσθαι, καὶ τὴν ἡμετέραν κατάκρισιν οἰκείαν ποιῆσαι μὴ παραιτήσασθαι· καὶ τοσοῦτον ἡμᾶς θεῶσαι κατὰ χάριν, ὅσον κατ᾿ οἰκονομίαν αὐτὸς γέγονεν ἄνθρωπος· ἵνα μὴ μόνον μάθωμεν ἀλλήλων ἀντέχεσθαι φυσικῶς, καὶ ὡς ἑαυτοὺς ἀλλήλους ἀγαπᾷν πνευματικῶς, ἀλλὰ καὶ ὑπὲρ ἑαυτοὺς ἀλλήλων κήδεσθαι θεϊκῶς, καὶ ταύτην τῆς εἰς ἀλλήλους ἀγάπης ἀπόδειξιν ποιεῖσθαι, τὸ κατ᾿ ἀρετὴν ὑπὲρ ἀλλήλων τὸν ἑκούσιον προθύμως αἱρεῖσθαι θάνατον. Οὐκ ἔστι γὰρ ἄλλη μείζων ταύτης ἀγάπη, φησὶν, ἵνα τις θῇ τὴν ψυχὴν αὐτοῦ ὑπὲρ τοῦ φίλου αὐτοῦ.

ιγʹ. Ὁ μὲν τῆς φύσεως νόμος ἐστὶν, ἵνα συνελὼν εἴπω, λόγος φυσικὸς, τὴν αἴσθησιν λαβὼν ὑποχείριον, πρὸς ἀφαίρεσιν τῆς ἀλογίας· καθ᾿ ἣν τῶν φυσικῶς συνημμένων ἐστὶν ἡ διαίρεσις. Ὁ δὲ γραπτὸς νόμος ἐστὶ, λόγος φυσικὸς μετὰ τὴν κατ᾿ αἴσθησιν τῆς ἀλογίας ἀφαίρεσιν, προσλαβὼν καὶ πόθον πνευματικὸν τῆς πρὸς τὸ συγγενὲς ἀλληλουχίας συνεκτικόν. Ὁ δὲ τῆς χάριτος νόμος, ὑπὲρ φύσιν καθἐστηκε λόγος, πρὸς θέωσιν ἀτρέπτως τὴν φύσιν μεταλλάττων, καὶ ὡς ἐν εἰκόνι δεικνὺς ἀκαταλήπτως τῇ φύσει τῶν ἀνθρώπων, τὸ ὑπὲρ οὐσίαν καὶ φύσιν ἀρχέτυπον, καὶ τὴν τοῦ ἀεὶ εὖ εἶναι διαμονὴν παρεχόμενος.

ιδʹ. Τὸ μὲν ἔχειν ὡς ἑαυτὸν τὸν πλησίον, μόνης ἐστὶ φροντίσαι τῆς πρὸς τὸ εἶναι ζωῆς· ὅπερ φυσικοῦ νόμου καθέστηκε· τὸ δὲ τὸν πλησίον ἀγαπᾷν ὡς ἑαυτὸν, καὶ τῆς πρὸς τὸ εὖ εἶναι κατ᾿ ἀρετήν ἐστι προνοεῖσθαι τοῦ πέλας· ὅπερ ὁ γραπτὸς ἐπιτρέπει νόμος· τὸ δὲ ὑπὲρ ἑαυτὸν ἀγαπῆσαι τὸν πλησίον, τοῦ νόμου τῆς χάριτός ἐστιν ἰδιαίτατον.

ιεʹ. Ὁ κωλυόμενος τῶν πρὸς ἡδονὴν σώματος ἀφορμῶν, μανθάνει Προνοίας λόγους, ἐπεχούσης τὴν ἐξαπτικὴν ὕλην τῶν παθῶν· ὁ δὲ τὰς πρὸς ὀδύνην σώματος δεχόμενος μάστιγας, διδάσκεται κρίσεως λόγους, προλαβόντων μολυσμῶν ἀκουσίοις πόνοις αὐτὸν ἐκκαθαιρούσης.

ιϛʹ. Εἰ τὸν προφήτην διὰ τὴν σκηνὴν καὶ τὴν κολόκυνθαν λυπούμενον, λέγω δὲ τὴν σάρκα καὶ τὴν τῆς σαρκὸς ἡδονὴν, τῆς δὲ Νινευῖ τὸν Θεὸν ἀντεχόμενον ἀντεισήγαγεν ὁ τῆς Γραφῆς λόγος, δῆλον ὡς τῶν τοῖς ἀνθρώποις τιμίων καὶ ἐπεράστων, τόδε τῷ Θεῷ φανὲν προσφιλὲς, πάντων τῶν ὄντων πολλῷ κρεῖττόν ἐστι καὶ τιμιώτερον, ἤπουγε [unus Reg.

But the law of grace teaches those who are led by it to imitate God himself immediately, who so loved us above himself, if it is lawful to say so, and that when we were enemies through sin, that he came even 1353into the essence that is ours without change, being above all essence; and superessentially accepted nature, and became man, and willed to be reckoned one of men, and did not refuse to make our condemnation his own; and deified us by grace as far as he himself by economy became man: that we may learn not only to hold to one another naturally and to love one another as ourselves spiritually, but also to care for one another above ourselves divinely, and to make this the proof of love toward one another, eagerly to prefer, by virtue, the voluntary death for one another. For there is no other greater love than this, he says, that one lay down his life for his friend. Joan. xv, 13.

13. The law of nature is, to say it in sum, natural reason taking sense under its hand for the removal of irrationality, according to which there is the division of things naturally joined. The written law is natural reason which, after the removal of irrationality according to sense, has taken on also a spiritual longing that holds together the mutual cohesion toward what is kindred. And the law of grace is reason established above nature, transforming nature without change to deification, and showing incomprehensibly to the nature of men, as in an image, the archetype that is above essence and nature, and providing the permanence of ever-well-being.

14. To hold the neighbour as oneself is to care only for his life unto being; which belongs to the natural law. To love the neighbour as oneself is to provide for the neighbour also unto well-being according to virtue; which the written law enjoins. And to love the neighbour above oneself is most proper to the law of grace.

15. He who is kept from the occasions of the body that lead to pleasure learns principles of Providence, which restrains the inflaming matter of the passions; and he who receives the scourges of the body that lead to pain is taught principles of judgement, which cleanses him by involuntary toils from previous defilements.

16.

ὅπου γε] τῶν οὐκ ὄντων, καὶ μόνῃ προλήψει κατ᾿ ἐσφαλμένην κρίσιν εἶναι δοκούντων, ὧν οὐδεὶς τοπαράπαν ἐστὶ κατὰ τὴν ὕπαρξιν λόγος· μόνη δὲ φαντασία τὸν νοῦν φενακίζουσα, καὶ σχῆμα τοῖς οὐκ οὖσι διάκενον, ἀλλ᾿ οὐχ ὑπόστασιν τῷ πάθει παρεχομένη.

1356ιζʹ. Ἡ μὲν ἀκριβὴς γνῶσις τῶν λογίων τοῦ Πνεύματος, μόνοις ἀποκαλύπτεσθαι πέφυκε τοῖς ἀξίοις τοῦ Πνεύματος· οἳ διὰ πολλὴν τῶν ἀρετῶν ἐπιμέλειαν, τῆς τῶν παθῶν αἰθάλης τὸν νοῦν ἐκκαθάραντες, ἐσόπτρου δίκην καθαροῦ καὶ διαυγοῦς, ἅμα τῇ προσβολῇ, καθάπερ πρόσωπον ἐντυπουμένην αὐτοῖς καὶ ἐμπίπτουσαν τὴν τῶν θείων δέχονται γνῶσιν. Οἷς δὲ ταῖς τῶν παθῶν κηλῖσιν ὁ βίος κατἑστικται, μόλις κἂν ἐξ εἰκότων τινῶν στοχασμῶν τεκμηριῶσαι τὴν τῶν θείων γνῶσίν ἐστι δυνατὸν, μη ὅτι γε νοῆσαί τε καὶ φράσαι ταύτην δι᾿ ἀκριβείας ἀπαυθαδίζεσθαι.

ιηʹ. Ὁ τοῦ νοῦ μορφὴν τὴν ἐξ ἀρετῶν ἐν Πνεύματι θείῳ δεξάμενος γνῶσιν, τὰ θεῖα λέγεται παθεῖν, ὅτι μὴ φύσει κατὰ τὴν ὕπαρξιν, ἀλλὰ χάριτι κατὰ τὴν μέθεξιν, ταύτην προσέλαβεν. Ὁ δὲ τὴν ἐκ χάριτος μὴ δεξάμενος γνῶσιν, κἂν λέγῃ τι γνωστικὸν, οὐκ οἶδε κατὰ πεῖραν τοῦ λεγομένου τὴν δύναμιν. Ψιλὴ γὰρ μάθησις, τὴν καθ᾿ ἕξιν γνῶσιν οὐ δίδωσι.

ιθʹ. Νοῦς εἰς ἄκρον διὰ τῶν ἀρετῶν καθαιρόμενος, τοὺς τῶν ἀρετῶν πέφυκεν εἰκότως ἐκδιδάσκεσθαι λόγους, τὴν ἐξ αὐτῶν θειωδῶς χαρακτηρισθεῖσαν γνῶσιν οἰκεῖον ποιούμενος πρόσωπον. Καθ᾿ ἑαυτὸν γὰρ ἀνείδεός τε καὶ ἀχαρακτήριστος πᾶς καθέστηκε νοῦς, μορφὴν ἔχων ἐπίκτητον, ἢ τὴν ἐκ τῶν ἀρετῶν ὑποστᾶσαν ἐν πνεύματι γνῶσιν, ἢ τὴν ἐκ τῶν παθῶν ἐπισυμβαίνουσαν ἄγνοιαν.

κʹ. Πᾶς ὁ τῆς θείας ἐκπεπτωκὼς ἀγάπης, τὸν ἐν τῇ σαρκὶ νόμον ἔχει διὰ τῆς ἡδονῆς βασιλεύοντα, τὸν μηδεμίαν θείαν ἐντολὴν φυλάξαι δυνάμενον ἢ βουλόμενον. Τῆς γὰρ ἐν πνεύματι Θεοῦ κατ᾿ ἀρετὴν διεξαγομένης ζωῆς τε καὶ βασιλείας, προτιμήσας τὴν φιλήδονον ζωὴν, τὴν ἄγνοιαν ἀντὶ τῆς γνώσεως ἑαυτῷ ἐπεσπάσατο.

καʹ. Ὁ μὴ διαβαίνων κατὰ νοῦν πρὸς τὴν ἔνδον ἐν πνεύματι τοῦ νομικοῦ γράμματος θείαν εὐπρέπειαν, τὴν τῆς γνώμης φιλήδονον σχέσιν, ἤγουν τὴν κοσμικὴν προσπάθειαν καὶ φιλοκοσμίαν γεννᾶν πέφυκεν, ἐκ τῆς κατὰ μόνην τὴν ἐν λόγῳ τοῦ νόμου μαθήσεως προφοράν.

κβʹ. Αἰσχύνη στόματός ἐστιν, ἡ κατὰ νοῦν φιλόκοσμος μελέτη τῶν λογισμῶν καὶ φιλοσώματος. Ἐν ᾧ γὰρ ὁ κόσμος ἐκ τῆς ἐμφαινομένης τῷ γράμματι τοῦ νόμου σωματικῆς διαπλάσεως, κατὰ τὴν γνωμικὴν σχέσιν γεννᾶσθαι πέφυκεν· ἤγουν, ἡ τὸν κόσμον φιλοῦσα διάθεσις, καὶ ἡ κατὰ νοῦν φιλήδονος μελέτη τῶν λογισμῶν.

If the word of Scripture set the prophet, grieving over the booth and the gourd (I mean the flesh and the pleasure of the flesh), over against God, who holds fast to Nineveh, it is clear that, of the things precious and lovely to men, this which has appeared dear to God is far better and more precious than all beings, much more than the things that are not, and that seem to be by prejudice alone, according to a mistaken judgement, of which there is no principle at all as to existence; but only an imagination, deceiving the mind and supplying to the passion an empty shape for things that are not, but not a hypostasis.

135617. The exact knowledge of the oracles of the Spirit is naturally revealed only to those worthy of the Spirit; who, having through much care for the virtues cleansed the mind of the soot of the passions, like a clean and translucent mirror, at the first approach receive the knowledge of divine things imprinted on them and falling in upon them like a face. But for those whose life has been spotted all over with the stains of the passions, it is hardly possible even from some likely conjectures to infer the knowledge of divine things, let alone to presume to understand and express it with exactness.

18. He who has received, as the shape of the mind, the knowledge from the virtues in the divine Spirit is said to suffer divine things, because he has taken it on not by nature as to existence, but by grace as to participation. But he who has not received the knowledge that is from grace, even if he says something of knowledge, does not know by experience the power of what is said. For bare learning does not give knowledge by habit.

19. A mind purified to the summit through the virtues is naturally, and fittingly, taught thoroughly the principles of the virtues, making the knowledge stamped from them in godlike manner its own face. For every mind in itself is formless and unstamped, having an acquired shape: either the knowledge that subsists from the virtues in the Spirit, or the ignorance that supervenes from the passions.

20. Everyone who has fallen from divine love has the law in the flesh reigning through pleasure, which is neither able nor willing to keep any divine commandment. For having set the pleasure-loving life above the life and kingdom conducted according to virtue in the Spirit of God, he has drawn upon himself ignorance instead of knowledge.

21. He who does not cross over by mind to the divine comeliness of the letter of the law that is within, in the spirit, naturally begets the pleasure-loving relation of inclination, that is, worldly passionate attachment and love of the world, from the utterance alone, in word, of the learning of the law.

22. Shame of mouth is the world-loving meditation of the thoughts in the mind, and body-loving. For in that in which the world is naturally born, by the relation of inclination, from the bodily fashioning that appears in the letter of the law (that is, the disposition that loves the world), there is also the pleasure-loving meditation of the thoughts in the mind.

Πρὸς ὃ γὰρ σχετικῶς διακείμεθα, πρὸς ἐκεῖνο καὶ κατὰ νοῦν μελέτην κεκτήμεθα.

1357κγʹ. Ἢ πάλιν αἰσχύνη στόματός ἐστιν, ἡ τοῖς πάθεσιν εἶδος ἐπάγουσα, καὶ τὸ καθ᾿ ἡδονὴν πρὸς αἴσθησιν διαπλάττουσα κάλλος, τοῦ νοῦ κίνησις. Χωρὶς γὰρ τῆς κατὰ νοῦν ἐπινοητικῆς δυνάμεως, πρὸς διάπλασιν μορφῆς, οὐδαμῶς πάθος ἄγεται. Ἢ ἀνάθημά ἐστι, καὶ ἡ πρόσυλος καὶ ἀκαλλὴς τῶν παθῶν καὶ ἀδιατύπωτος κίνησις· αἰσχύνη δὲ στόματος, ἡ τὸ πάθος εἰδοποιοῦσα πρὸς αἴσθησιν τοῦ νοῦ κίνησις, καὶ ταῖς ἐπινοίαις ὕλην ἁρμόδιον τῷ πάθει πορίζουσα.

κδʹ. Ὁ διὰ τρυφὴν σωματικὴν καὶ ἀνάπαυσιν τὰς θυσίας καὶ τὰς ἑορτὰς, τά τε Σάββατα καὶ τὰς νεομηνίας κατὰ τὸν νόμον τὸν Θεὸν διατεταχέναι πιστεύων, πάντη τε καὶ πάντως, ὑπὸ τὴν ἐνοχὴν γενήσεται τῆς τῶν παθῶν ἐνεργείας, καὶ τὴν αἰσχύνην τῆς τῶν ἐπ᾿ αὐταῖς αἰσχρῶν λογισμῶν ῥυπαρίας· ὑπό τε τὸν φθειρόμενον ἔσται κόσμον, καὶ τὴν ἐν λογισμοῖς φιλοσώματον μελέτην· ὑπό τε τὴν ὕλην τῶν παθῶν καὶ τὸ εἶδος, μηδὲν ἄλλο πλὴν τῶν φθειρομένων τίμιον ἔχειν δυνάμενος.

κεʹ. Ὁ πεισθεὶς θείαν εἶναι διαταγὴν τὸ σωματικῶς κατὰ νόμον τρυφᾷν, τὴν γαστριμαργίαν ὡς Θεοῦ δῶρον λαμβάνει πρὸς συμβίωσιν μετὰ χαρᾶς, ἐξ ἧς γεννᾷ τοὺς μολύνοντας τῇ παραχρήσει τὴν ἐνέργειαν τῶν αἰσθήσεων τρόπους.

κϛʹ. Ἐπειδὰν τὸ θεωρητικὸν τῆς ψυχῆς τὴν τρυφὴν κατὰ τὸν ἐν γράμματι νόμον ὡς θείαν διὰ τὴν ἐντολὴν ἀσπάσηται πρὸς συμβίωσιν, τὴν τῶν αἰσθήσεων παρὰ φύσιν μετέρχεται χρῆσιν, μηδεμιᾶς συγχωροῦν κατὰ φύσιν διαφαίνεσθαι τὴν χρῆσιν τῆς ἐνεργείας. Γεννᾷ γὰρ τήν τε τῶν παθῶν ἕξιν καὶ τὴν ἐνέργειαν, καὶ ὡς θείαν εἰσοικίζεται τὴν γαστριμαργίαν, πρὸς γένεσιν τῶν ῥυπαινόντων τῇ παραχρήσει τὰς αἰσθήσεις τρόπων, εἰς ἀναίρεσιν τῶν ἐν τοῖς οὖσι φυσικῶν λόγων τε καὶ σπερμάτων.

κζʹ. Οὐδεὶς μόνῃ προσανέχων τῇ σωματικῇ τοῦ νόμου λατρείᾳ, φυσικὸν τοπαράπαν δύναιτ᾿ ἂν παραδέξασθαι λόγον ἢ λογισμὸν, ὅτι μηδὲ πέφυκε ταυτὸν εἶναι τῇ φύσει τὰ σύμβολα. Ὁ γὰρ τοῖς συμβόλοις ἐναπομένων τοῦ νόμου, οὐ δύναται κατὰ λόγον τὴν τῶν ὄντων φύσιν ὁρᾷν, καὶ τοὺς τεθέντας οὐσιωδῶς ὑπὸ τοῦ δημιουργοῦ τοῖς οὖσι λόγους περιποιεῖσθαι, διὰ τὸ τῶν συμβόλων πρὸς τὴν τῶν ὄντων φύσιν διάφορον.

κηʹ. Ὁ Θεὸν τὴν κοιλίαν ἡγούμενος, καὶ ὡς ἐπὶ δόξῃ τῇ αἰσχύνῃ καλλωπιζόμενος, μόνων οἶδεν ὡς θείων ἀντέχεσθαι διὰ σπουδῆς τῶν παθῶν τῆς ἀτιμίας· καὶ διὰ τοῦτο μόνα περιέπει τὰ χρονικὰ, τουτέστι τὴν ὕλην καὶ τὸ εἶδος, καὶ τὴν κατὰ παράχρησιν πενταπλῆν ἐνέργειαν τῶν αἰσθήσεων. Τῇ γὰρ ὕλῃ καὶ τῷ εἴδει συμπλακεῖσα κατὰ μίξιν ἡ αἴσθησις, ἀποτελεῖν μὲν πάθος, ἀπεκτείνειν δὲ καὶ ἀναιρεῖν τοὺς κατὰ φύσιν πέφυκε λόγους. Πάθος γὰρ καὶ φύσις κατὰ τὸν τοῦ εἶναι λόγον, οὐδαμῶς ἀλλήλοις συνυπάρχουσιν.

For toward that to which we are disposed relationally, toward that we also possess meditation in the mind.

135723. Or again, shame of mouth is the movement of the mind that brings form upon the passions and fashions, for sense, the beauty that is according to pleasure. For without the inventive power in the mind, passion is in no way led to the fashioning of a shape. Or anathema is also the material and unlovely and unfigured movement of the passions; and shame of mouth, the movement of the mind that gives form to the passion for sense, and furnishes to the conceptions matter suited to the passion.

24. He who believes that God ordained according to the law the sacrifices and the feasts, the Sabbaths and the new moons, for bodily luxury and rest will altogether and in every way come under the liability of the activity of the passions, and the shame of the filth of the shameful thoughts upon them; and he will be under the world that is corrupted, and the body-loving meditation in thoughts; and under the matter and the form of the passions, able to hold nothing else precious but the things that are corrupted.

25. He who has been persuaded that it is a divine ordinance to live luxuriously in bodily fashion according to the law takes gluttony as a gift of God to live with in joy, from which he begets the modes that defile the activity of the senses by misuse.

26. When the contemplative part of the soul embraces, to live with it, the luxury according to the law in the letter as divine on account of the commandment, it pursues the use of the senses contrary to nature, allowing the use of no activity to show through according to nature. For it begets both the habit and the activity of the passions, and brings gluttony home as divine, for the coming-to-be of the modes that soil the senses by misuse, to the destruction of the natural principles and seeds in beings.

27. No one who attends only to the bodily worship of the law could at all admit a natural principle or thought, because the symbols are not naturally the same as nature. For he who remains in the symbols of the law cannot see the nature of beings according to reason, and secure the principles placed essentially in beings by the creator, because of the difference of the symbols from the nature of beings.

28. He who considers the belly God, and prides himself in shame as in glory, knows to hold with zeal, as divine, only the passions of dishonour; and for this reason he tends only temporal things, that is, matter and form, and the fivefold activity of the senses by misuse. For sense, entwined with matter and form by mixture, is wont to produce passion, and to kill and destroy the principles according to nature. For passion and nature, in the principle of being, in no way coexist with each other.

Οὐ γὰρ πέφυκε πάθει συνεμφαίνεσθαι 1360τοπαράπαν φύσεως λόγος, ὥσπερ οὔτε φύσει συναποτίκτεσθαι κατὰ γένεσιν πάθος.

κθʹ. Ὁ μὴ πιστεύων τὴν Γραφὴν εἶναι πνευματικὴν, τῆς οἰκείας κατὰ τὴν γνῶσιν πενίας οὐκ αἰσθάνεται, ἀλλὰ λιμῷ διαφθείρεται. Λιμὸς γάρ ἐστιν ὡς ἀληθῶς, ἔκλειψις τῶν κατ᾿ αὐτὴν τὴν πεῖραν ἐγνωσμένων ἀγαθῶν, καὶ ἀπορία καὶ σπάνις παντελὴς τῶν τὴν ψυχὴν συγκρατούντων πνευματικῶν βρωμάτων. Πῶς γὰρ λιμὸν ἡγήσεταί τις ἢ ζημίαν, τὴν τῶν καθάπαξ αὐτῷ μηδαμῶς ἐγνωσμένων ἀφαίρεσιν;

λʹ. Λιμώττει ἐπ᾿ ἀληθείας τῶν πιστῶν καὶ ἐπεγνωκότων τὴν ἀλήθειαν ὁ λαὸς, καὶ ἡ ψυχὴ τοῦ καθ᾿ ἕκαστον ἀφεμένη τῆς ἐν τῇ χάριτι πνευματικῆς θεωρίας, καὶ γενομένη τῆς τυπικῆς ἐν τῷ γράμματι δουλείας, μὴ διατρέφουσα τὸν νοῦν ταῖς τῶν νοημάτων μεγαλοφυΐαις, ἀλλὰ πληροῦσα τὴν αἴσθησιν ἐμπαθοῦς φαντασίας, ταῖς σωματικαῖς τῶν Γραφικῶν συμβόλων διατυπώσεσι.

λαʹ. Πᾶς ὁ τὴν πνευματικὴν τῆς ἁγίας Γραφῆς θεωρίαν μὴ προσιέμενος, τόν τε φυσικὸν ταύτῃ καὶ τὸν γραπτὸν κατὰ τοὺς Ἰουδαίους συναπώσατο νόμον, καὶ τὸν ἐν τῇ χάριτι νόμον ἠγνόησε, καθ᾿ ὃν δίδοται τοῖς κατ᾿ αὐτὸν ἀγομένοις ἡ θέωσις. Ὁ τοίνυν τὸν γραπτὸν νόμον λαμβάνων σωματικῶς, ἀρεταῖς τὴν ψυχὴν οὐ διατρέφει· καὶ ὁ τοῖς λόγοις τῶν ὄντων οὐκ ἐπιβάλλων, τῇ ποικίλῃ τοῦ Θεοῦ σοφίᾳ τὸν νοῦν οὐχ ἑστιᾷ φιλοτίμως· καὶ ὁ τὸ μέγα τῆς καινῆς χάριτος μὴ γινώσκων μυστήριον, τῇ ἐλπίδι τῆς μελλούσης θεώσεως οὐκ ἀγάλλεται. Οὐκοῦν ἡ ἔλλειψις τῆς κατὰ τὸν γραπτὸν νόμον θεωρίας, ἔχει παρεπομένην αὐτῇ τὴν ἔνδειαν τῆς κατὰ τὸν φυσικὸν νόμον νοουμένης ποικίλης σοφίας τοῦ Θεοῦ, ἐφεπομένην ἔχουσαν τῆς κατὰ τὸ καινὸν μυστήριον χάριτι δοθησομένης θεώσεως πάντως τὴν ἄγνοιαν.

λβʹ. Πᾶς νοῦς κατὰ Χριστὸν διορατικὸς καὶ ἰσχυρὸς ὁράσει, ἀεὶ ἐφίεται καὶ ζητεῖ τὸ πρόσωπον Κυρίου. Πρόσωπον δὲ Κυρίου καθέστηκεν, ἡ κατ᾿ ἀρετὴν τῶν θείων ἀληθὴς θεωρία, καὶ γνῶσις, ἣν ζητῶν, διδάσκεται τὴν αἰτίαν τῆς κατ᾿ αὐτὸν ἐνδείας καὶ ἀπορίας. Ὥσπερ γὰρ τὸ πρόσωπον χαρακτηριστικόν ἐστι τοῦ καθ᾿ ἕκαστον, οὕτω καὶ ἡ πνευματικὴ γνῶσις ἐμφαντικῶς χαρακτηρίζει τὸ Θεῖον. Ἣν ὁ ζητῶν, λέγεται ζητεῖν τὸ πρόσωπον Κυρίου.

For the principle of nature cannot 1360at all appear together with passion, just as neither is passion born together with nature in coming-to-be.

29. He who does not believe that Scripture is spiritual does not perceive his own poverty in knowledge, but is wasted by famine. For famine is, truly, the failing of the goods known by experience itself, and the total lack and scarcity of the spiritual foods that hold the soul together. For how will anyone consider famine or loss the removal of things never at all known to him?

30. Truly the people of the faithful and of those who have come to know the truth suffers famine, and the soul of each one, having let go the spiritual contemplation in grace, and having become the typical servitude's in the letter, not nourishing the mind with the grandeurs of the concepts, but filling the sense with passionate imagination by the bodily figurations of the Scriptural symbols.

31. Everyone who does not admit the spiritual contemplation of the holy Scripture has thrust away together with it, like the Jews, both the natural and the written law, and has been ignorant of the law in grace, according to which deification is given to those led according to it. He, then, who takes the written law bodily does not nourish the soul with virtues; and he who does not apply himself to the principles of beings does not feast the mind lavishly with the manifold wisdom of God; and he who does not know the great mystery of the new grace does not exult in the hope of the deification to come. Therefore the lack of the contemplation according to the written law has following upon it the want of the manifold wisdom of God understood according to the natural law, which has following upon it, in every way, the ignorance of the deification to be given by grace according to the new mystery.

32. Every mind according to Christ, clear-sighted and strong in sight, always aspires to and seeks the face of the Lord. Psal. xxv, 8. And the face of the Lord is the true contemplation and knowledge of divine things according to virtue, seeking which he is taught the cause of his own want and lack. For as the face is characteristic of each one, so also spiritual knowledge characterizes the Divine reflectively. Which he who seeks is said to seek the face of the Lord.

Ὁ δὲ κατασαρκούμενος κατὰ τὸ γράμμα τοῦ νόμου ταῖς ἐναίμοις θυσίαις, ποθουμένην ἄγνοιαν ἔχει, πρὸς μόνην

1361σαρκὸς ἡδονὴν ἐντολὴν ἐκδεχόμενος, καὶ μόνης κατ᾿ αἴσθησιν ἀντεχόμενος σωματικῶς τῆς ὕλης τοῦ γράμματος.

λγʹ. Ὁ μὲν σωματικῶς κατὰ τὸν νόμον λατρεύων, καθάπερ ὕλην γεννᾷ τὴν κατ᾿ ἐνέργειαν ἁμαρτίαν, καὶ ὡς εἶδος τὴν ἐπ᾿ αὐτῇ τοῦ νοῦ συγκατάθεσιν ταῖς προσφόροις ἡδοναῖς τῶν αἰσθήσεων ὑλικῶς διαπλάττεται. Ὁ δὲ πνευματικῶς τὴν Γραφὴν ἐκδεχόμενος, ὡς μὲν ὕλην τὴν ἐνέργειαν, ὡς εἶδος δὲ τῆς ἁμαρτίας τὴν συγκατάθεσιν, μετὰ τῶν κατὰ παράχρησιν πρὸς ἡδονὴν τῆς αἰσθήσεως τρόπων θανατοῖ, διὰ τῶν φυσικῶν λογισμῶν ἐν τῷ ὕψει τῆς θεωρίας.

λδʹ. Τὴν ὕλην καὶ τὸ εἶδος, καὶ τοὺς περὶ τὴν ὕλην καὶ τὸ εἶδος κατὰ παράχρησιν πέντε τρόπους τῶν πέντε αἰσθήσεων· λέγω δὲ τὴν ἐμπαθῆ τε καὶ παρὰ φύσιν πρὸς τὰ αἰσθητὰ τῶν αἰσθήσεων συμπλοκὴν, ἤγουν τὰ ὑπὸ χρόνον καὶ ῥεῦσιν, μετὰ τὴν πάροδον τῆς ἐν νόμῳ κατὰ τὸ γράμμα σωματικῆς λατρείας, καὶ τὴν τῆς ἀγνοίας διάβασιν, παραδίδωσι θανατωθῆναι τοῖς κατὰ φυσικὴν θεωρίαν ὑψηλοτέροις λόγοις καὶ λογισμοῖς, ὁ πνευματικὸς νόμος, ἢ νοῦς· τὴν ἐν τοῖς συμβόλοις πρὸς αἴσθησιν καὶ σῶμα καθολικωτάτην τῶν ὑπὸ χρόνον σχέσιν, ἀφανίσαι καὶ θανατῶσαι, κατὰ τὸ ὕψος γινομένοις τοῦ νόμου τῆς πνευματικῆς θεωρίας.

λεʹ. Χωρὶς φυσικῆς θεωρίας, οὐδεὶς τὴν πρὸς τὰ θεῖα τῶν νομικῶν συμβόλων ἀπέμφασιν διαγινώσκει. Εἰ γὰρ μὴ φυσικῶς πρότερόν τις τὴν ἐν τοῖς συμβόλοις τῶν θείων καὶ νοητῶν διασκοπήσας ἀπέμφασιν, ἐλθεῖν ποθήσει κατὰ νοῦν, ἔξω παντελῶς τῶν θείων ἀδύτων τὴν αἴσθησιν θέμενος, πρὸς τὴν τῶν νοητῶν εὐπρέπειαν, τῆς ἐν τοῖς τύποις ἀπολυθῆναι καθάπαξ οὐ δύναται σωματικῆς ποικιλίας· καθ᾿ ἣν ἕως βέβηκε τοῦ γράμματος ἀντεχόμενος, τὴν ἐκ τοῦ λιμοῦ τῆς γνώσεως εἰκότως ἀπορίαν οὐ παραμυθεῖται, τῆς Γραφῆς τὴν γῆν, ἤγουν τὸ σῶμα, κατὰ τὸν ἀπατηλὸν ὄφιν ἐσθίειν ἑαυτὸν καταδικάσας· ἀλλ᾿ οὐ κατὰ Χριστὸν, τὸν οὐρανὸν, ἤγουν τῆς Γραφῆς τὸ πνεῦμα καὶ τὴν ψυχὴν, τουτέστι τὸν οὐράνιον καὶ ἀγγελικὸν ἄρτον· λέγω δὲ, τὴν ἐν Χριστῷ τῶν Γραφῶν πνευματικὴν θεωρίαν καὶ γνῶσιν βρωματιζόμενος, ἣν δίδωσι δαψιλῶς ἐπιχορηγουμένην τοῖς ἀγαπῶσιν αὐτὸν ὁ Θεὸς, κατὰ τὸ γεγραμμένον, Ἄρτον οὐρανοῦ ἔδωκεν αὐτοῖς, ἄρτον ἀγγέλων ἔφαγεν ἄνθρωπος.

λϛʹ.

But he who is made flesh according to the letter of the law in the bloody sacrifices has a longed-for ignorance, toward only

1361… taking the commandment as pleasure of the flesh, and clinging bodily, according to sense, to the matter alone of the letter.

33. He who worships bodily according to the Law begets, as matter, sin in activity, and, as form, materially fashions upon it the mind's assent to the pleasures of the senses suited to it. But he who receives Scripture spiritually puts to death the activity as matter, and the assent as the form of sin, together with the modes of the misuse of sense for pleasure, through the natural thoughts, on the height of contemplation.

34. The matter and the form, and the five modes of the five senses that are about the matter and the form by misuse (I mean the passionate and contrary-to-nature entanglement of the senses with sensible things, that is, with the things under time and flux), after the passing-by of the bodily worship in the Law according to the letter, and the crossing-over of ignorance, the spiritual law, or mind, hands over to be put to death by the loftier principles and thoughts of natural contemplation: that these, having come to be on the height of the law of spiritual contemplation, may abolish and put to death the most universal relation, in the symbols, of the things under time toward sense and body.

35. Without natural contemplation no one discerns the incongruity of the symbols of the Law with respect to divine things. For unless one, having first examined naturally the incongruity of divine and intelligible things in the symbols, shall long to come in mind, having put sense wholly outside the divine sanctuaries, to the comeliness of the intelligibles, he cannot be released once for all from the bodily variety in the types; in which, as long as he walks clinging to the letter, he fittingly does not relieve the want that comes from the famine of knowledge, having condemned himself, like the deceiving serpent, to eat the earth of Scripture, that is, its body; Gen. iii, 14. and not, according to Christ, feeding on the heaven of Scripture, that is, its spirit and soul, which is the heavenly and angelic bread: I mean the spiritual contemplation and knowledge of the Scriptures in Christ, which God gives, abundantly supplied, to those who love him, as it is written: He gave them the bread of heaven; man did eat the bread of angels. Psal. lxxvii, 24.

36.

Χρὴ τὴν κατ᾿ αἴσθησιν πρὸς σῶμα τῆς Γραφῆς ἐκδοχὴν, ὡς παθῶν προδήλως καὶ τῆς πρὸς τὰ χρονικὰ καὶ ῥέοντα σχετικῆς διαθέσεως οὖσαν γεννητικὴν, ἤγουν τῆς πρὸς τὰ αἰσθητὰ τῶν αἰσθήσεων ἐμπαθοῦς ἐνεργείας, ὡς Σαοὺλ τέκνα καὶ ἔκγονα, διὰ 1364τῆς φυσικῆς ἐν ὄρει, τῇ τῶν θείων ἀναγωγῇ, λογίων ἀφανίσαι θεωρίας, εἴπως ἐμφορηθῆναι θείας ἐφιέμεθα χάριτος.

λζʹ. Ἀδικεῖ τὴν ἀλήθειαν ὅ τε νόμος κατὰ μόνον τὸ γράμμα νοούμενος, καὶ ὁ τῶν Ἰουδαίων λαὸς, καὶ εἴ τις ἐκείνων ὑπάρχει κατὰ διάνοιαν μιμητὴς, μόνῳ τῷ γράμματι περιγράφων τοῦ νόμου τὴν δύναμιν, καὶ μὴ δεχόμενος πρὸς τὴν φανέρωσιν τῆς μυστικῆς ἐγκεκρυμμένης τῷ γράμματι γνώσεως τὴν φυσικὴν θεωρίαν, μέσην ὑπάρχουσαν τύπων καὶ ἀληθείας· τῶν μὲν, τοὺς ἀγομένους ἀπάγουσαν, πρὸς δὲ τὴν ἐπανάγουσαν· ἀλλ᾿ οἷον ταύτην πάμπαν ἀρνούμενος, καὶ τῆς τῶν θείων αὐτὴν ἔξω ποιούμενος μυσταγωγίας. Ταύτην τοιγαροῦν χρὴ τοῦ νόμου τὴν σωματικὴν καὶ πρόσκαιρον ἐκδοχὴν, ὡς ὑπὸ χρόνον καὶ ῥέουσαν, ἀποκτείνειν διὰ τῆς φυσικῆς θεωρίας, ὡς ἐν ὄρει τῷ ὕψει τῆς γνώσεως, τοὺς τῶν θείων ἐπιμελητὰς θεαμάτων.

ληʹ. Τὸν σωματικὸν νοῦν τῆς Γραφῆς ἀφανίζει παντελῶς, ὁ πράξει διὰ τῆς φυσικῆς θεωρίας ἀποκτείνων τὴν γενομένην ἐκ τοῦ γραπτοῦ νόμου, πρὸς τὴν ἄστατον ὕλην καὶ ῥέουσαν τῇ ψυχῇ φιλήδονον σχέσιν καὶ φιλοσώματον, κατασφάττων ὡς τέκνα τοῦ Σαοὺλ καὶ ἔκγονα, τὴν χαμαίζηλον τοῦ νόμου διάνοιαν, διὰ μέσης τῆς φυσικῆς θεωρίας, ὡς ἐν ὄρει τῷ ὕψει τῆς γνώσεως, καὶ φανερὰν ποιούμενος ἐνώπιον Κυρίου, δι᾿ ἐξομολογήσεως δηλαδὴ, τὴν πρὸς σῶμα τοῦ νόμου προτέραν ἐκδοχήν. Τοῦτο γὰρ τὸ, Ἐξηλιάσαι, νοεῖσθαι δύναται τοῖς φιλομαθέσιν, ἔναντι Κυρίου. Καὶ ἐξηλίασαν αὐτοὺς, φησὶν, ἐν τῷ ὄρει ἔναντι Κυρίου· εἰς φῶς ἀγαγόντες, διὰ τῆς γνώσεως δηλαδὴ, τὴν κατὰ τὸ γράμμα πεπλανημένην πρόληψιν· ὅπερ ἐστὶ, φανερῶσαι κατὰ τὸ ὕψος τῆς θεωρίας τὸ γράμμα τοῦ νόμου νεκρὸν διὰ τῆς ἐν πνεύματι γνώσεως.

λθʹ. Τὸ γράμμα, φησὶν, ἀποκτείνει· τὸ δὲ πνεῦμα ζῳοποιεῖ. Διὸ χρὴ τὸ ἀποκτείνειν πεφυκὸς, ἀποκτείνεσθαι πνεύματι ζωοποιῷ. Συνυπάρχειν γὰρ ἀλλήλοις κατ᾿ ἐνέργειαν ἅμα κατὰ τὸ αὐτὸ τὸ σωματικὸν τοῦ νόμου καὶ τὸ θεῖον, ἤγουν τὸ γράμμα καὶ τὸ πνεῦμα, παντελῶς ἀμήχανον· ὅτι μηδὲ συμφωνεῖν πέφυκε τῷ παρεχομένῳ κατὰ φύσιν ζωὴν, τὸ ταύτην ἀφαιρεῖσθαι δυνάμενον.

μʹ. Τὸ μὲν πνεῦμα, ζωῆς ὑπάρχει παρεκτικόν· τὸ δὲ γράμμα, ζωῆς ἐστιν ἀφαιρετικόν.

The reception of Scripture by sense toward the body, as being plainly begetting of passions and of the relational disposition toward temporal and flowing things, that is, of the passionate activity of the senses toward sensible things, we must abolish like the children and descendants of Saul, 1364through natural contemplation on the mountain, that is, in the anagogy of the divine oracles, if indeed we aspire to be filled with divine grace.

37. The Law understood according to the letter alone wrongs the truth, and so does the people of the Jews, and anyone who is their imitator in understanding, circumscribing the power of the Law by the letter alone, and not admitting, for the manifestation of the mystical knowledge hidden in the letter, natural contemplation, which is the middle between types and truth: leading away from the former those who are being led, and leading them back up to the latter; but as it were denying it altogether, and putting it outside the mystagogy of divine things. Those, therefore, who tend the divine spectacles must kill this bodily and temporary reception of the Law, as being under time and flowing, through natural contemplation, as on a mountain, on the height of knowledge.

38. He utterly abolishes the bodily meaning of Scripture who by practice, through natural contemplation, kills the pleasure-loving and body-loving relation toward unstable and flowing matter that arose in the soul from the written Law; slaughtering, as the children and descendants of Saul, the earth-bound understanding of the Law, through the mediation of natural contemplation, as on a mountain, on the height of knowledge; and making manifest before the Lord, through confession, that is, the former reception of the Law toward the body. For this is what the lovers of learning can understand by to expose to the sun before the Lord. And they exposed them to the sun, it says, on the mountain before the Lord: ibid. 9. bringing into the light, through knowledge, that is, the preconception that went astray according to the letter; which is, to make manifest on the height of contemplation that the letter of the Law is dead, through knowledge in the spirit.

39. The letter, it says, kills; but the spirit gives life. II Cor. iii, 7. Therefore what is of a nature to kill must be killed by the life-giving spirit. For that the bodily element of the Law and the divine, that is, the letter and the spirit, should coexist with each other in activity at once and in the same respect is utterly impossible; since what can take life away is not of a nature even to agree with what provides life according to nature.

40. The spirit is what bestows life; the letter is what takes life away.

Οὐκοῦν οὐ δύναται καὶ τὸ γράμμα πράττειν κατὰ τὸ αὐτὸ καὶ τὸ πνεῦμα, ὥσπερ οὐδὲ τὸ ζωοποιὸν τῷ φθοροποιῷ συνυπάρχειν.

1365μαʹ. Περιτομή ἐστι μυστικὴ, τῆς ἐμπαθοῦς κατὰ νοῦν περὶ τὴν ἐπείσακτον γένεσιν σχέσεως, παντελὴς περιαίρεσις. Φυσικῶς γὰρ ἐπιβάλλοντες τοῖς πράγμασιν, οἴδαμεν ὡς οὐκ ἔστι τελειότης ἡ τῆς ἐκ Θεοῦ κατὰ φύσιν ἀρτιότητος περιαίρεσις. Οὐ γὰρ ποιεῖ τελειότητα φύσις διὰ τέχνης κολοβουμένη, καὶ διὰ περινοίας, ἀποτιθεμένη τὸ προσὸν αὐτῇ θεόθεν κατὰ λόγον δημιουργίας, ἵνα μὴ τὴν τέχνην, Θεοῦ πρὸς βεβαίωσιν δικαιοσύνης ἰσχυροτέραν εἰσάγωμεν, καὶ τὴν περινενοημένην τῆς φύσεως ἔλλειψιν, ἀναπληρωτικὴν ποιώμεθα τῆς ἐν δικαιοσύνῃ κατὰ δημιουργίαν ἐλλείψεως· ἀλλ᾿ ἐκ τῆς τοπικῆς [qu. 65, τυπικ.] τοῦ περιτεμνομένου μορίου θέσεως, διδασκώμεθα ποιεῖσθαι γνωμικῶς τὴν περιτομὴν τῆς κατὰ ψυχὴν ἐμπαθοῦς διαθέσεως· καθ᾿ ἣν μᾶλλον τῇ φύσει συντρέχειν ἡ γνώμη ῥυθμίζεται, τὸν ἐμπαθῆ τῆς ἐπικτήτου γενέσεως νόμον διορθουμένη.

μβʹ. Φυσικὸν ἡ ἀκροβυστία. Πᾶν δὲ φυσικὸν, θείας ἔργον δημιουργίας ἐστὶ, καὶ λίαν καλὸν, κατὰ τὴν φάσκουσαν φωνήν· Καὶ εἶδεν ὁ Θεὸς πάντα ὅσα ἐποίησε, καὶ ἰδοὺ καλὰ λίαν. Ὁ δὲ νόμος ὡς ἀκάθαρτον περιαιρεῖσθαι κελεύων τὴν ἀκροβυστίαν διὰ περιτομῆς, τὸν Θεὸν εἰσάγει, διὰ τέχνης τὸ οἰκεῖον διορθούμενον ἔργον, ὅπερ κἂν ἐννοεῖν ἀσεβέστατον. Οὐκοῦν ὁ τοῖς συμβόλοις τοῦ νόμου φυσικῶς ἐπιβάλλων, οἶδεν ὡς οὐ τὴν φύσιν ὁ Θεὸς διορθοῦται διὰ τῆς τέχνης, ἀλλὰ τὸ ἐπιπειθὲς λόγῳ τῆς ψυχῆς παθητικὸν περιτέμνεσθαι κελεύει, τὸ διὰ τοῦ σωματικοῦ μορίου τυπικῶς δηλούμενον, ὅπερ γνῶσις διὰ τῆς κατὰ πρᾶξιν ἀνδρίας, τῆς γνώμης ἀποτίθεσθαι πέφυκεν. Ὁ γὰρ περιτέμνων ἱερεὺς, σημαίνει τὴν γνῶσιν, τὴν ἔχουσαν καθάπερ σίδηρον κατὰ τοῦ πάθους, τὴν τοῦ λόγου κατὰ τὴν πρᾶξιν ἀνδρίαν. Ἀφανίζεται γὰρ ἡ τοῦ νόμου παράδοσις, πλεονεκτοῦντος τὸ γράμμα τοῦ πνεύματος.

μγʹ.

Therefore the letter and the spirit cannot both act in the same respect, just as the life-giving cannot coexist with the corrupting either.

136541. Mystical circumcision is the complete removal of the mind's passionate relation to the introduced coming-to-be. For, applying ourselves naturally to things, we know that the removal of the integrity which is from God according to nature is not perfection. For nature, mutilated by art and through contrivance putting off what belongs to it from God by the principle of its making, does not make perfection; lest we bring in art as stronger than God for the confirming of justice, and make the contrived deficiency of nature a filling-up of a deficiency in justice according to its making. But from the local position of the member that is circumcised we are taught to perform by inclination the circumcision of the passionate disposition in the soul; by which the inclination is regulated to run together rather with nature, correcting the passionate law of the acquired coming-to-be.

42. The foreskin is natural. And everything natural is a work of divine making, and very good, according to the voice that says: And God saw all things that he had made, and behold, they were very good. But the Law, commanding the foreskin to be removed through circumcision as unclean, brings in God correcting his own work through art, which is most impious even to conceive. Therefore he who applies himself naturally to the symbols of the Law knows that God does not correct nature through art, but commands the passible part of the soul that is obedient to reason to be circumcised, which is typically signified through the bodily member; and this, knowledge, through courage in practice, is of a nature to put away from the inclination. For the circumcising priest signifies knowledge, which has, like iron against passion, the courage of reason in practice. For the tradition of the Law is abolished when the spirit prevails over the letter.

43.

Τὸ Σάββατον, παθῶν ἐστι καὶ τῆς περὶ τὴν φύσιν τῶν ὄντων τοῦ νοῦ κινήσεως ἀνάπαυσις· ἢ τελεία παθῶν ἀπραξία, καὶ τῆς περὶ τὰ πεποιημένα καθολικὴ τοῦ νοῦ παύλα κινήσεως, καὶ πρὸς τὸ Θεῖον τελεία διάβασις· ἐν ᾧ τὸν δι᾿ ἀρετῆς καὶ γνώσεως κατὰ τὸ θεμιτὸν ἀφικόμενον, οὐ δεῖ καθάπερ ξύλα τὴν οἱανοῦν παντελῶς ὕλην ἐξαπτικὴν ἐνθυμεῖσθαι παθῶν, οὔτε μὴν φύσεως τὸ παράπαν ἀναλέγεσθαι λόγους, ἵνα μὴ πάθεσιν ἡδόμενον, ἢ φύσεως ὅροις μετρούμενον, κατὰ τοὺς Ἕλληνας τὸν Θεὸν δογματίζωμεν· ὅνπερ ἡ τελεία μόνη κέκραγε σιγὴ, καὶ ἡ παντελὴς καθ᾿ ὑπεροχὴν ἀγνωσία παρίστησι.

1368μδʹ. Στέφανός ἐστι χρηστότητος,πίστις καθαρὰ, λίθων τιμίων δίκην ταῖς τῶν δογμάτων ὑψηγορίαις, καὶ λόγοις διηνθισμένη πνευματικοῖς καὶ νοήμασι, τὸν θεοφιλῆ καθάπερ κεφαλὴν διασφίγγουσα νοῦν. Ἢ μᾶλλον στέφανος χρηστότητός ἐστιν, αὐτὸς ὁ τοῦ Θεοῦ Λόγος, ὁ τῇ ποικιλίᾳ τῶν κατὰ πρόνοιαν καὶ κρίσιν τρόπων, ἤγουν ἐγκρατείᾳ τῶν ἑκουσίων, καὶ ὑπομονῇ τῶν ἀκουσίων παθῶν, ὡς κεφαλὴν τὸν νοῦν περιγράφων, καὶ τῇ μετοχῇ τῆς κατὰ θέωσιν χάριτος αὐτὸν ἑαυτοῦ τὸν νοῦν ποιῶν ὡραιότερον.

μεʹ. Ἐνταῦθα τὴν ἐγκράτειαν, ἔργον εἶναι λέγει τῆς Προνοίας, ὡς τῶν γνωμικῶν καθαρτικὴν παθῶν· τὴν ὑπομονὴν δὲ, τῆς κρίσεως εἶναι κατόρθωμά φησιν, ὡς τοῖς ἀκουσίοις ἀντιτασσομένην πειρασμοῖς, καὶ τῆς πρακτικῆς φιλοσοφίας ὑπάρχουσαν σύμβολον· πρὸς ἀρετὴν, ὡς ἀπ᾿ Αἰγύπτου τῆς ἁμαρτίας, τοὺς ὑπ᾿ αὐτῆς κρατουμένους διαβιβάζουσαν.

μϛʹ. Οὐ τὰς ἡμέρας τιμᾶσθαι θέλων ὁ Θεὸς ὑπὸ τῶν ἀνθρώπων, προσέταξε τιμηθῆναι τό τε Σάββατον, καὶ τὰς νεομηνίας, καὶ τὰς ἑορτάς· οὕτω γὰρ ἂν λατρεύειν τῇ κτίσει παρὰ τὸν κτίσαντα κατὰ νόμου ἐντολὴν ἐδογμάτισεν, οἰομένους σεπτὰς εἶναι φύσει τὰς ἡμέρας, καὶ διὰ τοῦτο προσκυνητάς· ἀλλ᾿ ἑαυτὸν τιμᾶσθαι συμβολικῶς διὰ τῶν ἡμερῶν παρεδήλωσεν. Αὐτὸς γάρ ἐστι Σάββατον μὲν, ὡς τῶν ἐν σαρκὶ τῆς ψυχῆς μόχθων ἀνάπαυσις, καὶ τῶν κατὰ δικαιοσύνην πόνων κατάπαυσις· Πάσχα δὲ, ὡς ἐλευθερωτὴς τῶν τῇ πικρᾷ δουλείᾳ κεκρατημένων τῆς ἁμαρτίας· Πεντηκοστὴ δὲ, ὡς ἀπαρχὴ καὶ τέλος τῶν ὄντων, καὶ λόγος, ᾧ τὰ πάντα φύσει συνέστηκε. Σκόπει δὲ πῶς ὁ νόμος ἀπόλλυσι τοὺς νοοῦντας αὐτὸν σωματικῶς, τῇ κτίσει λατρεύειν παρὰ τὸν Κτίσαντα πείθων, καὶ ἡγεῖσθαι φύσει σεπτὰ, τὰ δι᾿ αὐτοὺς γεγονότα, τὸν δι᾿ ὃν αὐτοὶ γεγόνασιν ἀγνοήσαντας.

μζʹ. Ὁ μὲν κόσμος, τόπος ἐστὶ πεπερασμένος, καὶ στάσις περιγεγραμμένη· ὁ δὲ χρόνος, περιγραφομένη καθέστηκε κίνησις, ὅθεν καὶ ἀλλοιωτὴ τῶν ἐν αὐτῷ καθέστηκεν ἡ κατὰ τὴν ζωὴν κίνησις. Ὁπηνίκα δὲ τὸν τόπον διελθοῦσα καὶ τὸν χρόνον, κατ᾿ ἐνέργειάν τε καὶ ἔννοιαν ἡ φύσις, ἤγουν τὰ ὧν οὐκ ἄνευ, τουτέστι τὴν πεπερασμένην στάσιν καὶ κίνησιν, ἀμέσως συναφθῇ τῇ προνοίᾳ, λόγον εὑρίσκει τὴν πρόνοιαν, κατὰ φύσιν ἁπλοῦν τε καὶ στάσιμον, καὶ μηδεμίαν ἔχοντα πάντη περιγραφὴν, καὶ διὰ τοῦτο παντελῶς οὔτε κίνησιν.

μηʹ.

The Sabbath is rest from the passions and from the motion of the mind about the nature of beings; or complete inactivity of the passions, and universal cessation of the mind's motion about things made, and complete crossing-over to the Divine; in which he who has arrived through virtue and knowledge, as far as is permitted, must not, as with sticks of wood, Num. xv, 32. take thought for any matter whatever that kindles the passions, nor indeed gather up the principles of nature at all; lest we dogmatize God, after the manner of the Greeks, as delighting in passions or measured by the limits of nature: him whom perfect silence alone has cried aloud, and complete unknowing by way of pre-eminence presents.

136844. A crown of goodness is pure faith, flowered, after the manner of precious stones, with the lofty utterances of the doctrines and with spiritual words and concepts, binding round the God-loving mind as a head. Or rather, the crown of goodness is the very Word of God, who by the variety of the modes of providence and judgement, that is, by self-control over voluntary passions and endurance of involuntary ones, encircles the mind as a head, and by participation in the grace of deification makes the mind itself more beautiful than itself.

45. Here he says that self-control is a work of Providence, as purifying the passions of inclination; and endurance, he says, is an achievement of judgement, as arrayed against involuntary temptations, and being a symbol of practical philosophy, bringing across to virtue, as from Egypt, from sin, those held fast by it.

46. It was not because he wished the days to be honoured by men that God commanded the Sabbath and the new moons and the feasts to be honoured; for so he would have decreed, by commandment of the Law, that they worship the creation rather than the Creator, Rom. i, 25. supposing the days to be venerable by nature and therefore to be worshipped; but he indicated that he himself is honoured symbolically through the days. For he himself is the Sabbath, as the rest of the soul from its toils in the flesh and the cessation of labours for justice; the Pasch, as the liberator of those held fast by the bitter slavery of sin; Pentecost, as the beginning and end of beings, and the principle in which all things by nature subsist. Consider how the Law destroys those who understand it bodily, persuading them to worship the creation rather than the Creator, and to consider venerable by nature the things that came to be for their sake, having failed to know him for whose sake they themselves came to be.

47. The world is a limited place and a circumscribed rest; time is a circumscribed motion; whence also the motion of the life of the things in it is subject to alteration. But when nature, having passed through place and time in activity and in notion, that is, through the things without which it is not, namely limited rest and motion, is joined immediately to providence, it finds providence to be a principle, simple and stable by nature, and having no circumscription in any way, and therefore no motion at all.

48.

Ἐν μὲν τῷ κόσμῳ ὑπάρχουσα χρονικῶς ἡ 1369φύσις, ἀλλοιωτὴν ἔχει τὴν κίνησιν, διὰ τὴν τοῦ κόσμου πεπερασμένην στάσιν, καὶ τὴν καθ᾿ ἑτεροίωσιν τοῦ χρόνου φοράν· ἐν δὲ τῷ Θεῷ γινομένη, διὰ τὴν φυσικὴν τοῦ ἐν ᾧ γέγονε μονάδα, στάσιν ἀκίνητον ἕξει, καὶ στάσιμον ταυτοκινησίαν, περὶ τὸ ταυτὸν καὶ ἓν καὶ μόνον, ἀϊδίως γινομένην, ἣν οἶδεν ὁ λόγος ἄμεσον εἶναι περὶ τὸ πρῶτον αἴτιον, τῶν ἐξ αὐτοῦ πεποιημένων μόνιμον ἴδρυσιν.

μθʹ. Μυστήριόν ἐστι Πεντηκοστῆς, ἡ πρὸς τὴν Πρόνοιαν ἄμεσος τῶν προνοουμένων ἕνωσις· ἤγουν ἡ πρὸς τὸν λόγον κατὰ τὴν τῆς Προνοίας ἐπίνοιαν τῆς φύσεως ἕνωσις, καθ᾿ ἣν οὐδεμία τὸ παράπαν ἐστὶ χρόνου καὶ γενέσεως ἔμφασις. Σάλπιγξ δὲ πάλιν ἡμῶν ἐστιν ὁ λόγος, ὡς τὰς θείας καὶ ἀῤῥήτους ἡμῖν ἐνηχούμενος γνώσεις· ἱλασμὸς δὲ, ὡς ἐν ἑαυτῷ τὰ ἡμέτερα, καθ᾿ ἡμᾶς γενόμενος, διαλύων ἐγκλήματα· καὶ τῇ δωρεᾷ τῆς χάριτος ἐν πνεύματι, τὴν ἁμαρτήσασαν φύσιν θεοποιῶν· σκηνοπηγία δὲ, ὡς τῆς ἡμῶν κατὰ τὴν θεομίμητον ἕξιν περὶ τὸ καλὸν ἀτρεψίας πρᾶξις, καὶ ὁ τῆς πρὸς ἀθανασίαν μεταποιήσεως συνεκτικὸς δεσμός.

νʹ. Ὁ ψιλαῖς ταῖς ἐναίμοις χαίρων θυσίαις, περὶ τὰ πάθη σπουδάζειν ὡς ἐμπαθὴς παρασκευάζει τοὺς θύοντας. Φιλεῖ γὰρ χαίρειν τὸ γνησίως σέβον, οἷς χαίρει τὸ προσκυνούμενον. Ὅθεν θυσίας οἶδεν ὁ λόγος, τὴν τῶν παθῶν μᾶλλον σφαγὴν, καὶ τὴν τῶν φυσικῶν δυνάμεων προσαγωγήν· ὧν, τοῦ μὲν λόγου, τύπος ἐστὶν ὁ κριός· τοῦ δὲ θυμοῦ φέρει σύμβολον ὁ ταῦρος· τῆς δὲ ἐπιθυμίας, ἡ αἳξ ὑπάρχει δήλωσις.

ναʹ.

Existing temporally in the world, 1369nature has its motion subject to alteration, because of the limited rest of the world and the course of time by way of becoming-other; but when it comes to be in God, because of the natural monad of him in whom it has come to be, it will have an unmoved rest and a stable motion-in-sameness, coming to be eternally about the same and one and only; which the word knows to be immediate about the first cause, the abiding foundation of the things made by it.

49. The mystery of Pentecost is the immediate union of the things provided for with Providence; that is, the union of nature with the principle according to the conception of Providence, in which there is no reflection whatever of time and coming-to-be. Again, the Word is our trumpet, as sounding divine and ineffable knowledge into us; the expiation, as, having become like us, dissolving in himself the charges against us, and deifying by the gift of grace in the Spirit the nature that sinned; the feast of Tabernacles, as the practice of our unchangeableness about the good according to the God-imitating habit, and the bond that holds together our transformation into immortality.

50. He who delights in bare bloody sacrifices, being passionate, prepares those who sacrifice to be zealous about the passions. For what genuinely reveres is wont to delight in the things in which what is worshipped delights. Hence the word knows as sacrifices rather the slaughter of the passions and the offering of the natural powers: of which the ram is the type of reason; the bull bears the symbol of anger; and the goat is the indication of desire.

51.

Θυσίας πνευματικὰς εἶναι γινώσκομεν, οὐ μόνον τὴν τῶν παθῶν νέκρωσιν σφαττομένων τῇ μαχαίρᾳ τοῦ πνεύματος, ὅπερ ἐστὶ ῥῆμα Θεοῦ, καὶ πάσης τῆς ἐν σαρκὶ ζωῆς, ὡς αἵματος τὴν κατὰ πρόθεσιν κίνησιν, ἀλλὰ καὶ τῶν κατὰ φιλοσοφίαν ἠθῶν, καὶ πασῶν τῶν κατὰ φύσιν δυνάμεων προσαγωγὴν ἀφιερουμένων Θεῷ, καὶ τῷ πυρὶ τῆς ἐν Πνεύματι χάριτος πρὸς τὴν θείαν λῆξιν ὁλοκαυτοῦμένων.

1372νβʹ. Ὁ χοϊκὸς νοῦς τῆς Γραφῆς, κρατῶν τῆς ψυχῆς, τοὺς φυσικοὺς ἀποβάλλεται λόγους, τῇ παραχρήσει τῶν κατὰ φύσιν δυνάμεων αὐτοὺς ἐξαφανίζων. Οὗτος γὰρ ζῶν, ἐπ᾿ ἀληθείας συντελεῖ καὶ διώκει καὶ ἀπόλλυσι, σαρκὶ μόνῃ τὸν νόμον περιγράφων, τοὺς κατὰ φύσιν φημὶ λόγους καὶ λογισμοὺς, τιμῶν ὡς θεῖα τὰ πάθη τῆς ἀτιμίας, ἅπερ οἱ κατὰ φύσιν λογισμοὶ, λαμβάνοντες ἄδειαν ὑπὸ τοῦ νόμου τοῦ πνεύματος, ἀποκτείνουσιν ἐπὶ τὸ αὐτὸ καὶ θανατοῦσιν.

νγʹ. Ἅμα τις τὴν τῶν ἀρετῶν λογικῶς μετέλθοι φιλοσοφίαν, ἅμα καὶ τὴν τῶν Γραφῶν πρὸς τὸ πνεῦμα φυσικῶς μετήνεγκεν ἐκδοχὴν, ἐν καινότητι πνεύματος πρακτικῶς διὰ τῶν ὑψηλῶν θεωρημάτων τῷ Θεῷ λατρεύων, καὶ οὐ παλαιότητι γράμματος, διὰ τῆς κατ᾿ αἴσθησιν πρὸς σῶμα ταπεινοτέρας τοῦ νόμου παραδοχῆς, τροφεὺς παθῶν κατὰ τοὺς Ἰουδαίους γινόμενος, καὶ ἁμαρτίας θεραπευτής.

νδʹ. Ἅμα τις παύσεται κατ᾿ αἴσθησιν πρὸς σῶμα τὴν Γραφὴν ἐκδεχόμενος, ἅμα καὶ πρὸς τὸ πνεῦμα κατὰ νοῦν διὰ μέσης ἀνατρέχει τῆς φύσεως, ἐκεῖνα πράττων πνευματικῶς, ἅπερ σωματικῶς ὁ Ἰουδαῖος ἐπιτελῶν, ἔχει τὸν Θεὸν ὀργιζόμενον.

νεʹ. Πᾶς νοῦς κατὰ Θεὸν ὑψηλὸς καὶ μετέωρος, κατὰ τὸ αὐτὸ, τήν τε τῶν παθῶν κατασφάττει ἐνέργειαν, καὶ τὴν τῶν λογισμῶν ἀσχήμονα κίνησιν· πρὸς δὲ, καὶ τοὺς κατὰ παράχρησιν τῆς τῶν αἰσθήσεων ἐνεργείας ἀκολάστους τρόπους. Ἀναιροῦνται γὰρ τὰ πάθη ὑπὸ τῶν ὑψηλῶν τῆς φύσεως λογισμῶν, κατὰ τὴν ὑψηλὴν θεωρίαν θριαμβευόμενα.

νϛʹ. Τὸ κράτος τῆς ἁμαρτίας, ἤγουν τὸ φρόνημα τῆς σαρκὸς, ἡ χάρις ἀφανίζειν πέφυκε τοῦ ἁγίου βαπτίσματος· καὶ ἡ κατ᾿ ἐνέργειαν ὑπακοὴ τῶν θείων ἐντολῶν, ἀποκτείνειν τῇ μαχαίρᾳ τοῦ πνεύματος, ἤγουν, τῷ ῥήματι τῆς θείας ἐν πνεύματι γνώσεως, βοῶσα μυστικῶς πρὸς τὸ πάθος τῆς ἁμαρτίας, ὡς ὁ μέγας πρὸς τὸν Ἄγαγ, Σαμουὴλ, Ἀνθ᾿ ὧν ἡτέκνωσε γυναῖκας ἡ μάχαιρά σου, ἀτεκνωθήσεται σήμερον ἐκ γυναικῶν ἡ μήτηρ σου.

νζʹ. Πολλὰς τὸ πάθος τῆς γαστριμαργίας ὡς ἐν μαχαίρᾳ, τῷ τελείῳ τῆς ἡδονῆς λογισμῷ, ἡτέκνωσεν ἀρετάς. Τῆς μὲν γὰρ σωφροσύνης, διὰ τῆς ἀκρασίας ἀποκτείνει τὰ σπέρματα· τῆς δικαιοσύνης δὲ, διὰ τῆς πλεονεξίας διαφθείρει τὴν ἰσονομίαν· τῆς φιλανθρωπίας δὲ, διὰ τῆς φιλαυτίας, τὴν ἐκ φύσεως διατέμνει συνέχειαν. Καὶ συντόμως εἰπεῖν, πάντων τῶν κατ᾿ ἀρετὴν γεννημάτων ἀναιρετικόν ἐστι τῆς γαστριμαργίας τὸ πάθος.

νηʹ.

We know spiritual sacrifices to be not only the mortification of the passions slaughtered by the sword of the Spirit, which is the word of God, Ephes. vi, 17. and the motion by intent, as of blood, of the whole life in the flesh, but also the offering of the habits of philosophy and of all the powers according to nature, dedicated to God and offered whole as a burnt offering by the fire of grace in the Spirit unto the divine lot.

137252. The earthly meaning of Scripture, when it rules the soul, casts off the natural principles, obliterating them by the misuse of the powers according to nature. For this one, while living, truly consumes and persecutes and destroys, circumscribing the Law by the flesh alone, the principles and thoughts according to nature, I mean, honouring as divine the passions of dishonour; Rom. i, 26. which the thoughts according to nature, receiving licence from the law of the Spirit, kill and put to death together.

53. As soon as one pursues rationally the philosophy of the virtues, at once he has also naturally transferred the reception of the Scriptures to the spirit, worshipping God practically in newness of spirit through the lofty objects of contemplation, and not in oldness of letter, Rom. vii, 6. through the lowlier reception of the Law by sense toward the body, as one who becomes, like the Jews, a nurturer of passions and a servant of sin.

54. As soon as one ceases receiving Scripture by sense toward the body, at once he also runs up in mind toward the spirit through the mediation of nature, doing spiritually those things which the Jew, performing bodily, has God angered.

55. Every mind lofty and raised up according to God slaughters in the same act both the activity of the passions and the unseemly motion of the thoughts; and besides, the licentious modes of the activity of the senses by misuse. For the passions are destroyed by the lofty thoughts of nature, being led in triumph in the lofty contemplation.

56. The grace of holy baptism is of a nature to abolish the might of sin, that is, the way of thinking of the flesh; and obedience in activity to the divine commandments, to kill it with the sword of the Spirit, Ephes. vi, 17. that is, with the word of divine knowledge in the Spirit, crying mystically to the passion of sin, as the great Samuel to Agag: Because thy sword has made women childless, thy mother shall be made childless today among women. I Reg. xv, 33.

57. The passion of gluttony has made many virtues childless, as with a sword, with the full-grown thought of pleasure. For it kills the seeds of temperance through incontinence; it corrupts the equity of justice through greed; it cuts through the continuity from nature of love for man through self-love. And to say it briefly, the passion of gluttony is destructive of all the offspring of virtue.

58.

Τὸ τῆς γαστριμαργίας πάθος, πάντα τὰ θεῖα τῶν ἀρετῶν ἀποκτείνειν γεννήματα πέφυκεν· αὐτὸ δὲ τῇ τε χάριτι τῆς πίστεως, καὶ τῇ ὑπακοῇ τῶν θείων ἐντολῶν, διὰ τοῦ κατὰ τὴν γνῶσιν ἀποκτείνεται λόγου.

1373νθʹ. Φῶς ἐστιν ὡς ἀληθῶς ἐθνῶν ὁ Κύριος ἡμῶν, ἀποκαλύπτων αὐτοῖς διὰ τῆς ἀληθοῦς ἐπιγνώσεως, τοὺς τῷ ζόφῳ τῆς ἀγνοίας ἐπιμεμυκότας ὀφθαλμοὺς τῆς διανοίας, καὶ θείας ἀγωγῆς πάλιν ἑτοιμάσας ἑαυτὸν τοῖς πιστοῖς λαοῖς, ἀγαθὸν ἀρετῆς ἐξεμπλάριον, προσώπου τοῦ κατ᾿ ἀρετὴν, ὑπογραμμὸς αὐτοῖς γενόμενος καὶ ὑποτύπωσις, πρὸς ὃν ἀφορῶντες ὡς ἀρχηγὸν τῆς σωτηρίας ἡμῶν, τὰς ἀρετὰς κατὰ μίμησιν, ὡς ἡμῖν ἐστι δυνατὸν, κατορθοῦμεν διὰ τῆς πράξεως.

ξʹ. Πᾶς ὁ διὰ φθόνον μισῶν καὶ κακῶς διαθρυλῶν ταῖς διαβολαῖς τὸν ἐν τοῖς ἀγῶσι τῶν ἀρετῶν, καὶ τῇ περιουσίᾳ τοῦ λόγου τῆς πνευματικῆς γνώσεως δυνατώτερον, Σαούλ ἐστι πονηρῷ καταπνιγόμενος πνεύματι, μὴ φέρων τὸ κλέος τῆς κατ᾿ ἀρετὴν καὶ γνῶσιν εὐδαιμονίας τοῦ κρείττονος· καὶ διὰ τοῦτο πλέον μαινόμενος, ὅτι μὴ δύναται τὸν εὐεργέτην διαχειρίσασθαι. Πολλάκις δὲ καὶ αὐτὸν ἀποπέμπεται πικρῶς τὸν φίλτατον Ἰωνάθαν, τὸν ἕμφυτον λέγω κατὰ συνείδησιν λογισμὸν, τὸ ἄδικον μῖσος ἐλέγχοντα, καὶ τοῦ μισουμένου φιλαλήθως ἀφηγούμενον τὰ κατορθώματα.

ξαʹ. Παρακαλέσωμεν καὶ ἡμεῖς τὸν νοητὸν Δαβίδ, ἐπηχῆσαι τῇ κιθάρᾳ τῆς πνευματικῆς θεωρίας καὶ γνώσεως, ἐπιληπτευόμενον τοῖς ὑλικοῖς τὸν ἡμέτερον νοῦν, καὶ ἀπελάσαι τὸ πονηρὸν πνεῦμα τῆς πρὸς αἴσθησιν ὑλικῆς περιπετείας, ἵνα δυνηθῶμεν νοῆσαι τὸν νόμον πνευματικῶς, καὶ τὸν ἐν αὐτῷ κεκρυμμένον λόγον μυστικὸν εὑρεῖν, καὶ κτῆμα ποιήσασθαι διαρκὲς πρὸς ἐφόδιον ζωῆς ἀϊδίου.

ξβʹ. Πᾶς ὁ σωτηρίας ἐρῶν, ἢ πράξει πάντως ἢ θεωρίᾳ προσανέχει. Ἀρετῆς γὰρ καὶ γνώσεως χωρὶς, οὐδαμῶς οὐδεὶς πώποτε τυχεῖν σωτηρίας δεδύνηται· εἴπερ ἀρετῆς μὲν, τὸ τάσσειν τὴν τοῦ σώματος κίνησιν, οἷον χαλινῷ τινι τῷ ὀρθῷ λογισμῷ τῆς πρὸς ἀτοπίαν φορᾶς ἐπιστημόνως ἀναχαιτιζούσης· θεωρίας δὲ, τὸ τὰ καλῶς νοηθέντα τε καὶ κριθέντα ἐμφρόνως αἱρεῖσθαι ψηφίζεσθαι.

ξγʹ. Ἐπειδὴ νοερὸν μέν ἐστι τὸ νοοῦν, νοητὸν δὲ τὸ νοούμενον, τροφὴ δὲ καὶ οἷον σύστασις τοῦ νοοῦντος τὸ νοούμενον· εἰκότως ὁ Θεὸς ἅτε νόων ὄντων τῶν ἀσωμάτων, αὐτὸς νοούμενος, καὶ νοητὸς αὐτοῖς καθ᾿ ὅσον ἐφικνοῦνται γινόμενος, ἔσωθεν αὐτοὺς ἐλλάμπει, τοῦ νοῦ νοοῦντός τε καὶ τρεφομένου.

ξδʹ. Ἕτερόν ἐστι νοητὸν, καὶ ἕτερον νοερόν.

The passion of gluttony is of a nature to kill all the divine offspring of the virtues; but it itself is killed by the grace of faith and by obedience to the divine commandments, through the word of knowledge.

137359. Our Lord is truly a light of the nations, Luc. ii, 32. uncovering for them, through true full knowledge, the eyes of the understanding that had closed in the gloom of ignorance; and again having prepared himself for the faithful peoples as a good exemplar of virtue in divine training, becoming for them the outline and sketch of the person that is according to virtue; looking to whom as the author of our salvation, we achieve the virtues by imitation, as far as is possible for us, through practice.

60. Everyone who out of envy hates, and noises abroad evilly with slanders, him who is stronger in the contests of the virtues and in the abundance of the word of spiritual knowledge, is Saul choked by an evil spirit, not bearing the fame of the better man's blessedness in virtue and knowledge; and raving the more for this reason, that he cannot lay hands on his benefactor. And often he even bitterly sends away his dearest Jonathan himself, I mean the innate thought in the conscience, who reproves the unjust hatred and truth-lovingly recounts the achievements of the one hated.

61. Let us too beseech the intelligible David to play with the lyre of spiritual contemplation and knowledge upon our mind, seized as in a fit by material things, and to drive away the evil spirit of the material vicissitude toward sense, that we may be able to understand the Law spiritually, and find the mystical word hidden in it, and make it a lasting possession as provision for eternal life.

62. Everyone who yearns for salvation applies himself entirely either to practice or to contemplation. For without virtue and knowledge no one has ever in any way been able to attain salvation; since it belongs to virtue to order the motion of the body, reining it back skilfully, as with a kind of bridle, by right thought from its course toward what is out of place; and to contemplation, to decree that what has been well thought and judged be prudently preferred.

63. Since what thinks is noetic, and what is thought is intelligible, and what is thought is the food and as it were the constitution of what thinks: fittingly God, the bodiless beings being minds, himself being thought, and becoming intelligible to them as far as they reach, illumines them from within, the mind both thinking and being fed.

64. The intelligible is one thing, and the noetic another.

Τὸ γὰρ νοητὸν, τροφὴ οἷά τις, ὡς εἴρηται, τοῦ νοεροῦ 1376ἐστιν· εἴπερ τὸ νοούμενον, ὅ ἐστι νοητὸν, μεῖζόν ἐστι καὶ προεπινοούμενον τοῦ νοοῦντος, ὅ ἐστι νοερόν. Νοερὰ γὰρ λέγεται, ὅσα διὰ τὸ νοῦς εἶναι, νοεῖ τὰ ὑπερκείμενα νοητά· νοητὸν γάρ ἐστι τὸ νοούμενον, ὅπερ καὶ τροφή ἐστι τοῦ νοεροῦ, ἤτοι τοῦ νοοῦντος.

ξεʹ. Ἰστέον ὡς ἔχει τὰ αἰτιατὰ, τὰς τῶν αἰτίων ἐνδεχομένας εἰκόνας. Αἰτιατὰ γάρ ἐστι πάντα τὰ παραχθέντα εἰς κτίσιν· αἴτια δὲ, τὰ παραγαγόντα· οὐδεμία δὲ ἐμφέρεια τούτων πρὸς ἄλληλα αἰτίων φημὶ καὶ αἰτιατῶν.

ξϛʹ. Οὐκ ἔστιν ἀκριβὴς ἐμφέρεια τοῖς αἰτιατοῖ, καὶ τοῖς αἰτίοις· ἀλλ᾿ ἔχει μὲν τὰ αἰτιατὰ τὰς τῶν αἰτίων ἐνδεχομένας εἰκόνας· αὐτὰ δὲ τὰ αἴτια, τῶν αἰτιατῶν ἐξῄρηται, καὶ ὑπερίδρυται κατὰ τὸν τῆς οἰκείας ἀρχῆς λόγον. Καὶ γὰρ περισσῶς καὶ οὐσιωδῶς προένεστι τὰ τῶν αἰτιατῶν τοῖς αἰτίοις.

ξζʹ. Αἰτιατά ἐστι, πάντα τὰ παραχθέντα εἰς κτίσιν· εἴτε ἐν οὐρανῷ, εἴτε ἐπὶ γῆς· αἴτια δὲ, τὰ παραγαγόντα, τουτέστιν, αἱ τρεῖς ὑποστάσεις τῆς ἁγίας Τριάδος. Πρόδηλον οὖν, ὅτι οὐδεμία ἐμφέρεια τούτων πρὸς ἄλληλα, αἰτίων φημὶ καὶ αἰτιατῶν.

ξηʹ. Δέον εἰδέναι τὸν καθ᾿ ἡμᾶς νοῦν, τὴν μὲν ἔχειν δύναμιν εἰς τὸ νοεῖν, δι᾿ ἧς τὰ νοητὰ βλέπει· τὴν δὲ ἕνωσιν ὑπεραίρουσαν τοῦ νοῦ τὴν φύσιν, δι᾿ ἧς συνάπτεται πρὸς τὰ ἐπέκεινα ἑαυτοῦ. Κατὰ ταύτην οὖν τὰ θεῖα νοητέον, οὐ καθ᾿ ἡμᾶς· ἀλλ᾿ ὅλους ἑαυτοὺς, ὅλων ἑαυτῶν ἐξισταμένους, καὶ ὅλους Θεοῦ γινομένους. Κρεῖττον γὰρ εἶναι Θεοῦ, καὶ μὴ ἑαυτῶν. Οὕτω γὰρ ἔσται τὰ θεῖα δοτὰ τοῖς μετὰ Θεοῦ γινομένοις.

ξθʹ. Ὁ νοῦς νοεῖν βουλόμενος, κάτεισιν ἑαυτοῦ εἰς τὰς νοήσεις καταβαίνων. Αἱ γὰρ νοήσεις, κατώτεραί εἰσι τοῦ νοοῦντος, ὡς νοούμεναι καὶ καταλαμβανόμεναι, καὶ σκεδασμὸς εἰκότως καὶ μερισμὸς τῆς ἑνότητος τοῦ νοῦ αὐτοῦ. Ὁ μὲν γὰρ νοῦς, ἁπλοῦς καὶ ἀμερής· αἱ δὲ νοήσεις, παμπληθεῖς καὶ σκεδασταὶ, καὶ οἷον εἴδη τοῦ νοῦ.

For the intelligible is a kind of food, as has been said, of the noetic, 1376since what is thought, which is intelligible, is greater than what thinks, which is noetic, and is conceived before it. For those things are called noetic which, because they are minds, think the intelligibles that lie above them; for the intelligible is what is thought, which is also the food of the noetic, that is, of what thinks.

65. It must be known that effects have such images of the causes as are possible. For effects are all the things brought forth into creation; causes, those that brought them forth; and there is no likeness of these to one another, I mean of causes and effects.

66. There is no exact likeness between effects and causes; but the effects have such images of the causes as are possible, while the causes themselves are exempt from the effects and are seated above them according to the principle of their own origin. For the things of the effects pre-exist in the causes superabundantly and essentially.

67. Effects are all the things brought forth into creation, whether in heaven or on earth; causes, those that brought them forth, that is, the three hypostases of the holy Trinity. It is plain, then, that there is no likeness of these to one another, I mean of causes and effects.

68. One must know that our mind has, on the one hand, the power for thinking, through which it sees the intelligibles; on the other, the union that surpasses the nature of the mind, through which it is joined to the things beyond itself. According to this, then, divine things are to be thought, not in our fashion, but with ourselves wholly standing outside our whole selves and becoming wholly God's. For it is better to be God's and not our own. For thus will divine things be given to those who come to be with God.

69. The mind, wishing to think, goes down below itself, descending into intellections. For intellections are lower than what thinks, as being thought and comprehended, and fittingly a scattering and partition of the unity of the mind itself. For the mind is simple and partless; but intellections are very many and scattered, and as it were forms of the mind.

Διὰ τοῦτο τὰ νοερὰ, τουτέστι τὰ νοοῦντα, ἐλάττονά ἐστι τῶν νοητῶν, ἤγουν τῶν 1377νοουμένων. Ἕνωσιν δέ φησι τοῦ νοῦ, καθὼς ἐν τοῖς ἑξῆς σαφέστερον λέγει, δι᾿ ἧς πρὸς τὰ ἐπέκεινα ἑαυτοῦ ἀνατείνεται, ἤγουν τῇ περὶ Θεοῦ προσβάλλει θεωρίᾳ· τὸ, πάντων ἐκστῆναι τῶν αἰσθητῶν καὶ τῶν νοητῶν, ἔτι δὲ καὶ τῆς οἰκείας κινήσεως· εἶθ᾿ οὕτως τῆς θείας γνώσεως δέξασθαι τὴν ἀκτῖνα.

οʹ. Εἰ κινεῖται ἀναλόγως ἑαυτῷ νοερῶς τὸν νοερὸν, καὶ νοεῖ πάντως· εἰ δὲ νοεῖ, καὶ ἐρᾷ πάντως τοῦ νοηθέντος· εἰ δὲ ἐρᾷ, καὶ πάσχει πάντως τὴν πρὸς αὐτὸ ὡς ἐραστὸν ἔκστασιν· εἰ δὲ πάσχει, δῆλον ὅτι καὶ ἐπείγεται· εἰ δὲ ἐπείγεται, καὶ ἐπιτείνει πάντως τὸ σφοδρὸν τῆς κινήσεως. Εἰ δὲ ἐπιτείνει σφοδρῶς τὴν κίνησιν, οὐχ ἵσταται μέχρις ἂν γένηται ὅλον ἐν ὅλῳ τῷ ἐραστῷ, καὶ ὑφ᾿ ὅλου περιληφθῇ ἑκουσίως, ὅλον κατὰ προαίρεσιν τὴν σωτήριον περιγραφὴν δεχόμενον [male edita δεδόμενον] · ἵν᾿ ὅλον ὅλῳ ποιωθῇ τῷ περιγράφοντι, ὡς μηδ᾿ ὅλως λοιπὸν βούλεσθαι ἐξ ἑαυτοῦ αὐτὸ ἐκεῖνο ὅλον γνωρίζεσθαι δύνασθαι τὸ περιγραφόμενον· ἀλλ᾿ ἐκ τοῦ περιγράφοντος, ὡς ἀὴρ διόλου πεφωτισμένος φωτί· καὶ πυρὶ σίδηρος ὅλος ὅλῳ πεπυρακτωμένος, ἢ εἴ τι ἄλλο τῶν τοιούτων ἐστί.

οαʹ. Πολλὴ ἡ πρὸς τὰ νοούμενα τῶν νοούντων, καὶ πρὸς τὰ αἰσθητὰ τῶν αἰσθανομένων σχέσις. Ὁ δὲ ἄνθρωπος ἐκ ψυχῆς καὶ σώματος τυγχάνων αἰσθητικοῦ, διὰ τῆς κατ᾿ ἐπαλλαγὴν πρὸς ἑκάτερα τὰ τῆς κτίσεως τμήματα φυσικῆς σχέσεώς τε καὶ ἰδιότητος, καὶ περιγράφεται καὶ περιγράφει· τὸ μὲν, τῇ οὐσίᾳ· τὸ δὲ, τῇ δυνάμει. Περιγράφεσθαι γὰρ τοῖς νοητοῖς καὶ αἰσθητοῖς ὡς ψυχὴ τυγχάνων καὶ σῶμα, καὶ περιγράφειν ταῦτα κατὰ δύναμιν πέφυκεν, ὡς νοῶν καὶ αἰσθανόμενος· ὁ δὲ Θεὸς ἁπλῶς καὶ ἀορίστως ὑπὲρ πάντα τὰ ὄντα ἐστὶ, τὰ περιέχοντά τε καὶ περιεχόμενα, ὡς πᾶσι παντελῶς ἄσχετος ὤν.

οβʹ. Πᾶσα ἡδονὴ τῶν ἀπηγορευμένων, ἐκ πάθους διὰ μέσης αἰσθήσεως πρός τι πάντως γίνεσθαι πέφυκεν αἰσθητόν. Οὐδὲ γὰρ ἄλλο τί ἐστιν ἡδονὴ, ἢ εἶδος αἰσθήσεως, ἐν τῷ αἰσθητικῷ διά τινος αἰσθητοῦ μορφουμένη· ἢ τρόπος αἰσθητικῆς ἐνεργείας, κατ᾿ ἐπιθυμίαν ἄλογον συνιστάμενος. Ἐπιθυμία γὰρ αἰσθήσει προστεθεῖσα, εἰς ἡδονὴν μεταπίπτει, εἶδος αὐτῇ ἐπάγουσα· καὶ αἴσθησις κατ᾿ ἐπιθυμίαν κινηθεῖσα, ἡδονὴν ἀπεργάζεται, τὸ αἰσθητὸν προσλαβοῦσα. Γνόντες οὖν οἱ ἅγιοι, ὅτι διὰ μέσης σαρκὸς πρὸς τὴν ὕλην ἡ ψυχὴ παρὰ φύσιν κινουμένη τὴν χοϊκὴν μορφὴν ὑποδύεται, διὰ μέσης μᾶλλον ψυχῆς κατὰ φύσιν αὐτοὶ πρὸς τὸν Θεὸν κινούμενοι, καὶ τὴν σάρκα τῷ Θεῷ πρεπόντως οἰκειῶσαι διενοήθησαν, δι᾿ ἀσκήσεως ἀρετῶν ἐνδεχομένως αὐτὴν ταῖς θείαις ἐμφάσεσι καλλωπίσαντες.

ογʹ.

Therefore the noetic, that is, the things that think, are less than the intelligibles, that is, the 1377things thought. He calls «union» of the mind, as he says more clearly in what follows, that through which it stretches up to the things beyond itself, that is, applies itself to the contemplation concerning God: namely, to stand outside all sensible and intelligible things, and further also its own motion; and then thus to receive the ray of divine knowledge.

70. If the noetic moves noetically in proportion to itself, it certainly also thinks; if it thinks, it certainly also yearns for what it has thought; if it yearns, it certainly also suffers ecstasy toward it as toward the yearned-for; if it suffers, it is clearly also urged on; if it is urged on, it certainly intensifies the vehemence of its motion; and if it intensifies its motion vehemently, it does not stop until it comes to be whole in the whole yearned-for, and is encompassed by the whole willingly, receiving wholly by choice the saving circumscription, that it may be wholly qualified by the whole that circumscribes it, so that what is circumscribed no longer wishes at all to be able to be known, that very thing, wholly, from itself, but from what circumscribes it: as air wholly illumined by light, and iron wholly fired through by the whole fire, or whatever else there is of the kind.

71. Great is the relation of those who think to the things thought, and of those who perceive to the sensible things. And man, being of soul and of a sensitive body, through his natural relation and property toward each of the two sections of creation by way of interchange, both is circumscribed and circumscribes: the one by essence, the other by power. For he is of a nature to be circumscribed by the intelligibles and sensibles, as being soul and body, and to circumscribe them by power, as thinking and perceiving. But God is simply and without bound above all beings, both those that contain and those contained, as being utterly without relation to all.

72. Every pleasure in forbidden things is of a nature to arise entirely from passion, through the mediation of sense, toward some sensible thing. For pleasure is nothing other than a form of sense, shaped in the sensitive faculty by some sensible thing; or a mode of sensitive activity, constituted according to irrational desire. For desire added to sense turns into pleasure, bringing a form upon it; and sense moved by desire produces pleasure, taking on the sensible thing. The saints, then, knowing that the soul, moved contrary to nature toward matter through the mediation of the flesh, puts on the earthly form, themselves moving rather according to nature toward God through the mediation of the soul, resolved to bring the flesh too fittingly into kinship with God, adorning it, as far as possible, through the ascesis of the virtues, with the divine reflections.

73.

Μεγαλοφυῶς οἱ ἅγιοι κατὰ τὸν ἀληθῆ καὶ 1380ἄπταιστον τῆς κατὰ φύσιν κινήσεως τρόπον, τὸν παρόντα τῶν σκαμμάτων αἰῶνα διέβησαν· τὴν μὲν αἴσθησιν, ἁπλοῦς διὰ μέσου τοῦ λόγου πρὸς τὸν νοῦν τοὺς τῶν ὄντων ἔχοντα λόγους ἑνώσαντες· τὸν δὲ νοῦν, τῆς περὶ τὰ ὄντα πάντα κινήσεως καθαρῶς ἀπολυθέντα, καὶ αὐτῆς τῆς καθ᾿ αὐτὸν φυσικῆς ἐνεργείας ἠρεμοῦντα, τῷ Θεῷ προσκομίσαντες· καθ᾿ ὃν ὁλικῶς πρὸς Θεὸν συναχθέντες, ὅλοι ὅλῳ Θεῷ ἐγκραθῆναι διὰ τοῦ πνεύματος ἠξιώθησαν, ὅλην τοῦ ἐπουρανίου κατὰ τὸ δυνατὸν ἀνθρώποις, τὴν εἰκόνα φορέσαντες, καὶ τοσοῦτον ἕλξαντες τῆς θείας ἐμφάσεως, εἰ θέμις τοῦτο εἰπεῖν, ὅσον ἑλχθέντες αὐτοὶ τῷ Θεῷ ἐνετέθησαν.

οδʹ.
